# Design Backlog — the Topic Ledger

Every idea gets a row the moment it's uttered. Statuses:
`💡 candidate` (mentioned, not designed) → `📐 designed` (has a home in a design doc or
archive section) → `🔬 researched` (coverage-matrix row ✅ in `research/README.md`) →
`📜 RFC` (spec'd/implementing) → `⛔ rejected` (with reason, kept for the record).

Rules: **an idea missing from this ledger is a process bug** · **do NOT RFC from a
candidate row** (and per `rfc/0000-rfc-process.md`, not from any row until the
exploration gate opens). Honesty note: everything marked 🔬 below rests on the archive's
**desk research** — provenance effectively `[P]` until Phase-0 hands-on work upgrades it
(`archive/brief-v2/research/research_limitations.md` is the package's own accounting).

Archive homes are cited as `arch/NN §…` = `archive/brief-v2/NN_….md`.

## Breadth-first product surfaces (owner-ratified 2026-08-11)

These are one product, not optional bolt-ons behind a one-pack drill screen.
Each must reach a minimal real end-to-end workflow before content depth and
branch-scoring polish become the main work. Canonical matrix:
`03-product-breadth.md`.

Every row below is **scheduled**, not optional. The "Program" column names the
RFC-program item in `03-product-breadth.md` that owns it and the current
verified state from the 2026-08-12 alignment pass (`planning/breadth/`).
"Foundation" means it is blocked only by one of the three primitives F1–F3 in
`planning/breadth/synthesis.md` §2, not by design.

| Surface | Breadth requirement | Program | Home |
|---|---|---|---|
| Just Play — normal/from-position play with branch-and-learn as the game develops | Pack-optional runs, phase/structure recognition, honest dynamic evidence, rewind/fork/compare/replay | #3 · blocked on **F2** (pack is mandatory at six layers) | `03-product-breadth.md`, `planning/breadth/session-contexts.md` |
| Phase-oriented product discovery | Opening/early game, middlegame, endgame, and trajectory navigation exists independently of catalog size | #3 · blocked on **D6** — `phase` is never projected to the client | `03-product-breadth.md`, `01-training-model.md` |
| Streamer/Twitch mode | Real host board + chat voting + overlay + rewind/branch/compare scenario | ✅ **SHIPPED — flip owed since 2026-08-12, applied 2026-08-15.** It ships as **`SESSION_KINDS = ["stream", …]`** (`live-types.ts:3`): a closed enum, SQL-constrained, route-validated, offered as a three-way client dropdown. Chrome-free overlay at `/live/overlay/:runId`, 2–8-option 15–600 s advisory votes, a disjoint chat-relay namespace capped at 50k voters, CAS'd host board lease. Every `design/03` §Live commitment verified met (`design/research/broadcast-and-teacher-surfaces.md`). **This row reading "blocked" for three days is the proximate cause of the impression that the surface had nothing behind it.** *Original:* #8 · **RULED 2026-08-12: the streamer may cheat on themselves, and that is documented rather than engineered against.** The overlay is not fixable by per-viewer reveal (corrected 2026-08-12 by the F3 draft): under any account model a player can hold a second account, be granted spectator on their own run, and read what they are playing blind. So "audience sees eval while streamer does not" needs a channel the player's own client is provably never authorized on — or an accepted decision that a streamer may cheat on themselves. Chat voting and host rewind/branch/compare are unaffected and blocked only on F3 roles | `03-product-breadth.md`, `planning/breadth/live-and-platform.md` |
| Academy/coached sessions | Host/participant/spectator roles, voting/proposals, replay and session-to-pack distillation | ✅ **SHIPPED — same flip, same date.** Coached sessions ship as **`academy`**, the second member of `SESSION_KINDS`. Roles, voting/proposals, replay and session→pack distillation all verified (`distill.ts:82` is the producer, which also makes `design/03:286`'s "does NOT exist" correction stale). The stated F3 blocker was corrected as inaccurate in `rfc/archive/live-session-platform.md` and the correction was never applied. *Original:* #8 · roles blocked on **F3**; the lease cannot express a second writer role | `03-product-breadth.md`, `arch/11` |
| Position Arena | Real external challenge/invite + two-leg/PGN return workflow before native matchmaking depth | #8 · needs PGN import (`parsePgn` has no production caller) and both legs imported as root-forked branches of **one** run | `03-product-breadth.md`, `arch/11`, `arch/rfcs/RFC-0007` |
| Learn/return system | History/resume, progress, episode/concept SRS, related retries, optional recommendations | #7 · history/resume ship; everything else blocked on **F3** | `03-product-breadth.md`, `planning/breadth/create-and-return.md` |
| Episode/concept SRS | Schedule whole rehearsal episodes and related-position retries rather than memorized moves | #7 · `transfer.scheduled` is a declared event with no producer; scheduling unit is an open owner ruling | B7, `01-training-model.md §Repetition` |
| Optional personal-history recommender | Recommend relevant packs/positions without making game import mandatory or restoring the rejected v1 identity | #7 · strictly additive; follows F3 | B7, ADR-0003 |
| Create/curate system | Pack studio, study/repertoire/game/session imports, provenance, review and regression workflow | #6 · validation/lint/digest/graduation ship; no write endpoint, no UI, no importer | `03-product-breadth.md`, `arch/product/content_pack_authoring.md` |
| Automatic candidate-pack mining | Real corpus pipeline emits unpublished candidates into the author/review workflow; never auto-publishes lessons | #6 · proof is one unpublished candidate through the real pipeline | B6, `arch/08` |
| Theory-sourcing dossier executable re-verification | 📝 owner-tier update proposed by B6a: record `[V]` for pinned `lichess-org/chess-openings` commit `4b8622759e7ae6f93f011cc6c83a3823401ab45e`, its `eco/name/pgn` TSV header, and successful offline D35 candidate emission; preserve the existing CC0 licence note | `design/research/theory-sourcing.md`; proposal text also in `planning/archive/content-sourcing-foundation/log.md` |
| Puzzle-source dossier executable re-verification | 📝 owner-tier replacement proposed by B6d: “2026-08-12 `[V]`: the live Lichess puzzle dump has eleven columns ending in `DailyDate`; `00008`, `0000D`, and `000Pw` replay legally through their complete UCI lines; and the official theme vocabulary contains `opening`, `middlegame`, and `endgame`. B6c produced an available priority artifact; Q6’s offline-explorer revival condition did not fire. Operator sourcing credentials remain separate from optional learner-facing Lichess account linking.” | `design/research/theory-sourcing.md`; `planning/exploration/log.md` |
| Lichess account linking vs sourcing credential | 💡 keep two boundaries: an operator/admin OAuth token may supply shared catalog-authoring explorer data; optional learner-facing “Link with Lichess” later serves identity/import/recommendation workflows and must never be required for shared content or lend individual credentials to the sourcing pipeline | B6c operator config; B7 optional personal history/import |
| Share/spectate/deep links | Drill/run URLs, read-only projections, export/import and spectator-safe views | #3 · the follower loop already works end to end; blocked on **D1** (a run link is currently a write credential) | `03-product-breadth.md` |
| Drill-in-a-URL | Address a FEN/objective or pack/run directly for coaches, forums, stream sessions, and handoff between contexts | #3 · **half-shipped** — grammar, validator and resolver exist and are tested in `schema/src/drill-pack/urls.ts` with zero production consumers | B8, `03-product-breadth.md` |
| Full evidence/explanation surface | Authored + Stockfish + Maia + corpus/history + Syzygy + features + evidence-bound LLM, timing controlled | #2 · blocked on **F1**; six of nine contracts pin against merged code today | `03-product-breadth.md`, `arch/09`, `planning/breadth/evidence-explanation.md` |
| Full-spectrum application shell | Play/Learn/Review/Live/Create/Library/Settings with shared board/run/branch/evidence primitives | #1 · routes ship; residuals (`/settings` has no form control, `phase` unprojected, URL grammar unused) belong to the foundation edge | `03-product-breadth.md`, `02-product-shape.md` |

## Foundation primitives (✅ ALL THREE IMPLEMENTED 2026-08-12)

**Status: complete.** F1 shipped as `rfc/archive/authored-explanation-surface.md`;
F3 then F2 shipped in that order as migrations 2 and 3. Defects D1, D2 and D3
closed with them. The table below is kept because the *finding* — that the work
missing across eight program items reduced to three primitives — is what made
the sequencing possible, and because later RFCs cite these names.

Note for anyone citing this section: `feedbackIsRevealed` no longer exists. F2
replaced it with `feedbackDisclosed` (`packages/runtime/src/feedback.ts:3`).
Coordinates below are pre-implementation and are historical.

Six parallel code-verified passes (`planning/breadth/`) found that the missing
work across all eight program items reduces to **three absent primitives**, each
blocking areas that never talk to each other, each small in isolation, and all
three mutually independent. Full evidence: `planning/breadth/synthesis.md` §2.

| Primitive | What is absent | Blocks |
|---|---|---|
| **F1 — per-scope reveal** | `feedbackIsRevealed(pack, run)` (`feedback-policy.ts:11`) is one boolean per run; reaching Pack A's first of three checkpoints would release prose for all three | B4 authored-prose delivery (the named prerequisite in the *implemented* `rfc/archive/authored-feedback-delivery.md`); the streamer overlay, which needs per-viewer withholding; coach-reveals-prose in academy sessions |
| **F2 — pack-optional run identity** | A pack is mandatory at six layers; the pack-blind branch at `service.ts:145-152` is reachable only from tests, and the browser's `CreateRunRequest` cannot express a pack-less start | Just Play and from-position starts (#3), Arena leg import (#8), the pack playtest harness (#6). Must land together with D2 |
| **F3 — a subject** | `grep -riE "learnerId\|userId\|profileId\|accountId"` over all source returns nothing; the only actor token is a browser-local UUID and there is one database table | all of B7; host/participant/spectator roles in B5; the fix for D1. One migration now versus three later |

**A companion finding worth keeping visible:** the foundation RFCs cut slots that
no surface ever filled — `outcome.reached`, `transfer.scheduled`,
`human_model_predicted`, `feedback.generated`, `CheckpointInteraction.prediction`,
the drill-address grammar, and the generic predicate evaluator are all shipped,
tested, and have **zero producers**. Breadth-first worked: the contracts exist
and what remains is mostly producers and surfaces. The mirror risk is that a
slot with no producer reads exactly like a working feature — which is how five
RFC drafts died, and why every row in this pass carries a `file.ts:line`.

## Open defects (🐞 — found by the alignment pass, not yet fixed)

**Ledger id assignment — instituted 2026-08-15, AMENDED 2026-08-16 after it failed once.** Ids
here are assigned by *reading this file*, so a row committed between an agent-s read and its
write collides silently — it happened twice on 2026-08-15 (renumbered to D117 and D121–D127).
The fix was disjoint id blocks per parallel agent. **That fix then failed on its own third
run:** blocks were issued *in briefs* and never written here, so codex — correctly told to take
*"the next free id above the highest issued block"* — took **D203**, which was a
cross-reviewer-s, because it could not see a block that existed only in another agent-s prompt.
Renumbered to D233. **The hole was the convention-s, not codex-s.**

**Amended rule: a block is registered HERE when it is issued, not when its rows land.** The
table below is the register. An agent may use only its own block; anyone without one takes the
next free id above the highest registered block.
| Block | Finding / obligation | Disposition / history (not status) |
|---|---|---|
| D480 ✅ | **The auth gate loaded the current route before authentication, then did not reload it after registration or login.** On `/play`, the initial pack request could finish with `AUTH_REQUIRED` before the session probe; `startRouter()` only installed `popstate` and left that route error rendered even though the new session cookie was valid. Refresh appeared to fix registration because it reran the route under the stored cookie. **CLOSED 2026-08-16:** route work is suppressed while there is no learner, and starting the authenticated router explicitly loads the current route and its polling contracts. The regression pins zero pre-auth pack requests followed by exactly one successful post-register load. | ✅ closed by the D480 post-auth route fix |
| D479 ✅ | **Migration 6 projected quarantined legacy runs and could crash the packaged backend before it opened a port.** An existing Docker volume at storage version 5 contained an attempt-producing v0.4 run with no `sessionKind`; `#addProgressTables` selected every `drill_runs` row, and `projectAttempts` passed `undefined` as SQLite parameter 4. This escaped the migration test because its synthetic v0.4 snapshot still carried current fields and contained no learner move. **CLOSED 2026-08-16:** migration 6 now selects only the frozen literal `schema_version = '0.7'`; the regression uses an attempt-producing v0.4 snapshot with `sessionKind` removed and proves the row remains quarantined while the database reaches the current version. No persisted data is deleted. | ✅ closed by the D479 backend-startup fix |
| D497 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-20 by the A0 active-RFC truth audit.** `engine-leverage` and `vocabulary-wiring` passed an independent clean-tree closeout and moved to the archive; pack lanes 0.23/0.24 now point to implemented archive rows rather than advertising held work below HEAD 0.27. The missing derived-register mechanism remains with `shared-resource-registers` and D499/D504. *Original:* two implemented RFCs remained `implementing` and held historical lanes. | ✅ closed by RFC archival; systemic register guard remains open |
| D498 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-21 by shared-resource-registers.** Shape-entry 0.3 has a landed row, 0.4 has a live declaration/register join, and C2/C4 fail on stale or missing lanes. | ✅ closed by `shared-resource-registers` |
| D499 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-21 by shared-resource-registers.** `EVIDENCE_KINDS` has a member-derived register; C3 catches duplicate member claims and C4/C6 bind every landed member and the count to the exported constant. | ✅ closed by `shared-resource-registers` |
| D500 🐞 | **`rfc/README.md` uses states RFC-0000 does not define, so the parity check queued to guard it cannot run without a mapping first.** *"Returned to author"* appears in the register and in no process document. Run by hand during the pass: **8 of 9 Active RFCs agree** with their bodies — so the check would pass today and is still worth having, but [[D477]]'s remedy needs a state vocabulary before it needs code | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D501 📊 | **[[D487]]'s arithmetic has already moved: 213 of 248 open rows have no destination.** Re-derived 2026-08-16 after the A0 closures. **The direction is what matters** — the count changes with every wave, which is precisely why the register must be derived rather than written, and why a number quoted from a hand-made lane file has a shelf life measured in hours | 📊 measured 2026-08-16 |
| D502 ✅ | **OWNER RULING 2026-08-16 — the corpus reaches learners through BOTH channels: a disclosed draft channel now, and a graduated shelf as packs earn it.** Answers [[D481]]. Ship all 56 behind a clear *unreviewed draft* badge — the registry already carries `channel: "official" \| "community"` and the UI renders it — and promote packs onto the official shelf as clearance lands. **Nothing is hidden and nothing is overclaimed**, which is the same posture the product takes toward evidence. **The remedy is NOT flipping `NODE_ENV`** ([[D481]]). Unblocks K1–K4, K8, R6–R8 and a real play session; gives the graduation machinery a consumer, which the draft-channel-only option would have denied it. **Second ruling in the same breath: the schema example fixture is REMOVED from the served library** — it is a format fixture, not content, its own commentary reads *"Schema-only annotation; requires human review"*, and it should validate the schema in tests and never reach a user | ✅ implemented 2026-08-16 — both channels serve with explicit trust labels; the schema example is validation-only |
| D507 ✅ | **A kill criterion's usability clause fired: 5 of 6 served endgame packs could not receive their own authored first move, and at 1280×720 and 1366×768 all 64 squares of all 6 were unhittable.** `design/research/endgame-latency-versus-cet.md` `[V]` 2026-08-16. The board overflowed `.position-column` by **64–164 px** and was drawn under the timeline; `.drill-region` did not scroll and `scrollIntoView` moved it **0 px**. **The trigger was authored objective length** — schema example **68** chars, endgame packs **277–444** — which is exactly why the one pack production served was **0/64 occluded** and the corpus was not. **Closed 2026-08-17:** objective prose keeps its complete text in a bounded scroll region, the board retains a 192px interaction floor, and `assertRunViewport` now exercises all six endgame packs at all five measured desktop/tablet projections rather than only fixtures incapable of triggering the defect. Also corrects `planning/app-reality-check.md` §6.8, which noticed this overlap and **retracted it** after measuring only the pack production served | ✅ closed 2026-08-17 by the D507 viewport fix |
| D549 💡 | **Skills/concepts earned from game review — chess.com's "skills" taxonomy as a progression surface (owner, 2026-08-20).** Chess.com fills five categories — *fundamentals, openings, tactics, strategy, endgames* — with named concepts and patterns a review detects and credits to the player. Owner: *"we need something like THAT too — it can support our campaign mode or general progress tracking and gamification."* Fits what already exists better than it first looks: the shipped-but-consumerless `attempt_concepts` ([[D300]]) is exactly a concept-credit stream, and [[D297]]'s knowledge-as-key device is the fun mechanism this feeds. **Constraint: a skill is credited from detected evidence, never LLM opinion (law 8), so this is downstream of the producer registry** | 💡 owner idea 2026-08-20 |
| D550 💡 | **Rewind-from-mistake with explained alternative branches — Beacon Chess's shape, near ours (owner, 2026-08-20).** Owner: *"they have rewinds from mistakes and then alternative moves with an explanation… kinda like autobranching?"* Our loop is commit → consequence → rewind → branch → compare; theirs auto-seeds the branch at the mistake and explains the alternative. The delta worth studying is the **auto-seeding** — we require the learner to branch; they offer the branch at the moment of failure. Candidate transformation under the `design/02` adoption amendment (a conflict with an invariant is a design prompt, not a veto): auto-*offered*, never auto-*played*, and never before commit | 💡 owner idea 2026-08-20 |
| D551 💡 | **Bot personality and band-steering beyond raw Maia — Chessiverse's construction is documented and the owner wants it studied (owner, 2026-08-20).** https://chessiverse.com/articles/how-chessiverse-bots-are-created — owner: *"it does seem like steering the AI in early game to give personality cause it just uses Maia otherwise… they apply tricks to add behaviours or make higher/lower Elo and unique opening books."* Directly relevant to what we measured: **D335/D336 found 100 band points buy 29–40 real Elo and a band step is below learner resolution** — i.e. raw Maia banding is a weak dial, which is exactly the problem their tricks address. Also their *"support mode"* (lighting up squares) is the owner's hover-evidence idea already shipped by a competitor; and their 16-personalities-style player typing maps a learner's style to named archetypes. **The evidence-producer rework serves bot decision-making too** — the same grounded facts that power guidance can steer an opponent | 💡 owner idea 2026-08-20 |
| D552 💡 | **Post-session and longitudinal feedback — openings you play, accuracy per opening, style mapping, personalised tips (owner, 2026-08-20).** Owner: *"chess.com has so much feedback after a session and can tell you all your openings and how accurate you are with them"*; *"maps your opening style to (aggressive-solid, theoretical-creative) and maps it to the greats"*; *"early game is solid, but in the midgame your play is too simple and positional, not enough tactics"*. The last form is a **derived aggregate over detected evidence** — legal under law 8 iff every input is a grounded fact and the LLM only renders the aggregate. Needs: per-move producer stream (the registry), opening identity at runtime (currently **refused** at `position-evidence.ts:25` — [[D544]]), and a longitudinal store keyed by learner. **Adjacent to `learner-rating`'s event-log projection and must not become a rating input** ([[R15]]'s byte-identity rule) | 💡 owner idea 2026-08-20 |
| D553 💡 | **Pattern-of-play indexing — habitual configurations as first-class detected patterns (owner, 2026-08-20).** The owner's own example: *"liking to put the bishop fianchetto with horse in front — move horse gives discovered attack + whatever your horse defends/attacks. We need to index THAT kind of play."* This is a **configuration + consequence** pattern: a static arrangement (fianchetto bishop, knight blocking) carrying a latent tactical property (discovered attack on knight move). The shape library already expresses static arrangements; what it cannot express is the **latent consequence** — which is [[D544]]'s tactical family (discovered attacks are on the list) joined to a shape trigger. Also the germ of style detection: a learner who repeatedly sets up this configuration has a habit worth naming | 💡 owner idea 2026-08-20 |
| D554 📊 | **The competitor sweep is stale against a fast-moving field (owner, 2026-08-20).** Owner names apps the 40-row matrix does not cover or covers thinly: **Beacon Chess, Quackmate, ChessLabHQ, Chessiverse, QChess, RookHub** — *"seems like lots of vibecoded chess apps upcoming? we have to keep an eye… we're the only open and free one!"* The matrix was built 2026-08-11 from forum sweeps; the owner asks for the forum threads mentioning this class of app to be swept and indexed again. **The positioning claim to preserve while doing it: free + self-hostable + ALL the appreciated primitives + advanced UX + full analysis + human-like bots — the integration IS the differentiator** (`design/02` §Adoption already rules adopting freely; the matrix is a mining list) | 💡 owner idea 2026-08-20 |
| D555 ✅ | **OWNER RULING 2026-08-20 — the breadth ambition is one integrated FOSS/self-hostable chess platform that mines every appreciated competitor capability.** Chessiverse's personalities and human-like bots, Lichess theory/analysis, Chess.com's review and human play, Beacon's re-entry, Quackmate's shareable profile and future novel apps all enter a capability audit. **This is not blanket implementation authority:** each capability receives an adopt / transform / defer decision under the rehearsal thesis and existing refusals; personal history remains optional, generic puzzles/courses become play-the-consequence material, and analysis must return the learner to play. Products are evidence and inspiration; capabilities plus their compatible transformation are the roadmap unit. | ✅ owner breadth ruling 2026-08-20; alignment program opened |
| D556 ✅ | **A snapshot competitor matrix cannot monitor a rapidly changing app field.** The 2026-08-20 resweep added seven representative products but also found duplicate/ambiguous names (`Qchess.net` versus `QuChess`, and multiple ChessLab products). Indexing every clone would create noise. **Closed 2026-08-20:** `capability-watch.json` and its checker key 21 canonical representatives to 18 capabilities, preserve source/date/love/hate/transform/dependencies/routes, and prove aliases do not increase coverage. The first result also exposes the research debt honestly: 18/25 evidence rows have neither love nor hate checked. Products are evidence for a capability, not the roadmap unit. | ✅ capability-watch research instrument 2026-08-20; D554 hands-on/forum resweep remains open |
| D557 📊 | **OWNER CLARIFICATION 2026-08-20 — investigate a SEPARATE theory knowledge-builder like `~/frameworks/monorepo/api_consultant`, not scraping inside a hint request.** The research is now measured: the need survives and the proposed mechanism narrows. A separate builder should fetch allow-listed sources, record rights/revision/span/digests, attach typed applicability keys and publish an immutable local index. It is not a live hint scraper or general chess chat agent. The LLM boundary remains law 8: it may render an already-admitted evidence packet, never select the source, infer applicability, manufacture truth or grade the move. Skipper's semantic path failed D564's gate, so embeddings/contextualization/reranking are not a 1.0 dependency. | 📊 owner question answered 2026-08-20; `design/research/theory-knowledge-pipeline.md`, `planning/platform-alignment/knowledge-retrieval/results.md` |
| D563 ✅ | **OWNER RULING 2026-08-20 — build the 1.0 queue bottom-up: research first, then owner/design synthesis, then accepted RFCs, then implementation and content.** The platform-alignment plan must name every research dependency, its experiment and exit criterion; every resulting design decision; the RFC dependency graph; and only then executable work. A feature wish or competitor capability cannot jump directly into an RFC, and an RFC cannot hide an unanswered research question in “open questions.” The end state is a queue that reaches the full integrated 1.0 vision, not another thematic list. | ✅ sequencing ruling 2026-08-20; expands law 1 into the alignment program |
| D564 ✅ | **The Skipper semantic-extraction branch was tested and refused for 1.0.** Over 144 fixed queries, exact+FTS reached **97.7% recall@5** while the strongest semantic arm reached **94.7%**, returned an ineligible top result on **8.3%** of answerable queries (2% ceiling) and abstained on **66.7%** of hard negatives (90% floor). Source replacement passed, but current chunks cannot reproduce required licence/revision/span/digests and cannot invalidate a same-dimension embedding-model change. Contextualization added 1.5 points recall@1 over reranking, zero recall@5, and provider latency. Whole-service startup also required unrelated Frameworks services. **Do not extract pgvector/contextualization/reranking for 1.0; retain the ingestion/invalidation patterns in the deterministic builder under D557/D581.** | ✅ research branch closed negatively 2026-08-20; `planning/platform-alignment/knowledge-retrieval/results.md` |
| D558 🐞 | **The shipped “classifier” is a geometric census, not the semantic move-understanding surface the guidance vision assumes.** It exposes 18 structural kinds and 6 transition kinds; `piece_reach_count` counts attacks in the current position, not squares a piece can threaten in 2–3 moves, and named structures cover only Carlsbad, IQP and Maroczy. There are no first-class fork, pin, skewer, discovered attack, overload, deflection, decoy, removal-of-defender, back-rank, castling-right, development, trade, pawn-island, prophylaxis, or multi-ply-threat facts. `vacationReading` finds newly unblocked sliders but has no consumer ([[D546]]). Every new detector needs sign, subject/object/squares, confidence or exactness, abstention, provenance, counterfactual semantics, named consumers, positive/negative fixtures, and an external validation corpus. | 🐞 verified 2026-08-20; **R1 research landed** as `detection-landscape.md`/D565-D568; implementation defect remains open behind R2/O1-O3 |
| D559 💡 | **The post-game product is under-specified relative to the owner's “Chess.com Game Review, but grounded and replayable” target.** The current story ranks six mechanical moment kinds with a fixed 150cp pivot and renders generic evaluation sentences; the share image contains only the first selected sentence. Missing: opening identity, phase arc, sign-aware “held/avoided/enabled” facts, human commonness, tactic/structure links, a grounded training focus, retry/re-enter/branch actions, theory links, and a useful social recap. The transformation that avoids the rejected engine-review screen is a **Review Map whose moments are doors back into rehearsal**; labels and prose may be rendered by an LLM only after deterministic fact selection. | 💡 owner idea 2026-08-20; research and UX contract required |
| D560 ✅ | **OWNER RULING 2026-08-20 — do not expand authored content until the foundation is rock solid enough that new primitives do not force repeated pack editing.** The hold is supported by measurement: since 2026-08-11, 42 commits have touched the original pack/schema code population and 61 have touched content; pack schema is 0.27 while accepted `graduation-clearance` holds 0.28 and all 50 product packs remain drafts. **R6 later confirmed the risk directly:** 27 schema mutations, no pack format/capability stamp, no reusable migration ladder, four zero-witness primitive families, and one semantic false green where all 92 current documents validate under 0.24 despite later reader-dependent semantics. The proposed clearance instrument is **Gate F**: no active schema claim; producer→consumer manifest complete; detector semantics v1; capability/deprecation registry; migration dry-run over every pack; compatibility/re-authoring budget; and a small sacrificial official-pilot corpus exercising every required primitive. **The content hold is active; the owner may amend Gate F's exact proof.** | ✅ owner content hold 2026-08-20; Gate F routed to `planning/platform-alignment/plan.md`; R6 evidence added 2026-08-20 |
| D561 🐞 | **Human-like bot play currently has modes and sampling knobs, not a bot personality policy.** The runtime exposes `targetElo`, `temperature` and `topP`; `human_common` largely takes Maia output and `theory_strict` restricts it to an authored spine. There is no repertoire distribution, style constraint, phase policy, error-shape guard, move-time model, repeat memory, adaptation contract, or validation against human move/error distributions. Chessiverse's documented candidate-curation + opening-book approach is evidence that raw Maia band choice is only the base model. Define strength, style, repertoire, error character and chat as separate measurable layers; shared evidence may steer policy but must not become invisible engine omniscience. | 🐞 verified 2026-08-20; expands [[D551]] and needs bot-policy research |
| D562 🐞 | **There is no grounded player-style classification implementation.** `attempt_concepts` can store concept credit and `learner-rating` drafts a separate strength projection, but neither defines opening/style dimensions, reference populations, sample minima, confidence, or versioned archetypes. A fun “player type” must be a transparent projection of named measured metrics, show uncertainty and evidence, and stay separate from rating and prescriptive tips; longitudinal tips require a second, grounded aggregation contract. | 🐞 verified 2026-08-20; expands [[D552]]/[[D553]], research before RFC |
| D565 📊 | **Cheap tactic geometry is a broad atom, not the semantic event its name implies.** R1 replayed 50,000 official Lichess puzzle records and legal counterfactuals. Against the automatically generated/vote-refined themes, cheap fork geometry reached **100.0% recall / 32.3% precision**, new absolute pin **41.4 / 39.0%**, opened-slider discovered attack **99.4 / 19.7%**, and capture of an attacked/undefended non-pawn **99.9 / 7.9%**; discovered-attack geometry also fired on **20.48%** of legal alternatives to tagged solution moves. This does not make Lichess ground truth. It proves the cheap definitions answer a broader question. Tactic labels need value/eligibility, functional consequence, sign and a declared line/search boundary; otherwise emit the atom or abstain. | 📊 measured 2026-08-20; `design/research/detection-landscape.md`, `tools/detection-landscape-harness/` |
| D566 🐞 | **`pawn_safe_square.safe` does not establish square safety.** `pawnSafetyOnPosition()` computes `pushAttackers` and `captureAttackers` but sets `safe` from `pushAttackers.length === 0` alone; it does not test intervening occupancy or legal pawn paths. `structuralReading()` then emits the result for non-pawn pieces under a learner-visible `pawn_safe_square` kind. The honest atom is a current-file pawn-reach projection, not “safe square”; the existing kind is ineligible for that claim until its semantics are replaced/versioned. | 🐞 verified 2026-08-20; `structure.ts:136-166,347,468`; R1 |
| D567 📊 | **The Lichess puzzle themes are a powerful disagreement corpus and an incomplete ontology.** Lichess documents automatic generation/tagging plus vote refinement; its AGPL tagger declares `overloading` but the implementation returns `False` unconditionally, and its test has the overload case commented out. Therefore the CC0 export can supply external positives, hard negatives and regression scale, but cannot be promoted to universal ground truth or validate overload. Each semantic detector still needs exact/mirrored/counterfactual fixtures and an explicit abstention/failure result. | 📊 verified 2026-08-20; `design/research/detection-landscape.md`; official database/tagger sources |
| D568 💡 | **The reusable evidence foundation is six planes joined at the consumer, not one classifier score.** Rules/board, transition/bounded consequence, search/engine/tablebase, human corpus/model, theory catalogue and authored truth have different exactness, freshness, cost and abstention. Each emitted record needs a versioned kind, anchor, sign, operands/squares, basis, exactness/confidence, counterfactual denominator, cost/freshness, abstention and allowed consumers. Modules consume bounded packets; the LLM only renders one. This is the research input to O1–O3/F1–F3, not RFC or schema authority. | ✅ implemented by evidence-contract-manifest 2026-08-21; exact compiled producer/projection/consumer boundary and negative fixture now ship |
| D569 📊 | **Local counterfactual specificity fixes volume but does not establish significance.** R2 compared all legal alternatives for 754 authored decisions and 579 fixed-ply decisions from 108 rated Lichess games. A predeclared ≤20%-same-family, cap-two selector cut the raw reading from **8.70→0.79** entries/decision authored and **11.42→1.03** imported while raising mean counterfactual specificity from **18.65→93.26%** and **18.23→93.84%**. But its most-selected families included `piece_count`, `bishop_on_shade` and generic attack/defence count changes: distinctive because few alternatives make the same literal change, not necessarily worth teaching. Counterfactual admission is a necessary noise gate and cannot be the relevance policy by itself. | 📊 measured 2026-08-20; `design/research/selection-sign-and-significance.md`, `tools/r2-selection-harness/` |
| D570 📊 | **A global top-k learned on authored spines transfers only partially; local denominators transfer better.** The authored per-kind lift ranking has Spearman **ρ=0.667** against a stratified imported-game sample. `king_opposition` falls from 2.81× authored to 0.76× imported and `piece_distance` from 2.23× to 1.00×, while `doubled_pawn` rises from 6.98× to 16.27×. The old authored top-eight still reduces imported noise (0.52 cards/decision, 90.31% specificity), but it is a dated population-specific baseline, not a durable product allow-list. Measure the candidate against the current position's alternatives and version any population prior. | 📊 measured 2026-08-20; R2 |
| D571 💡 | **Relation sign is not chess valence; [[D545]]'s “avoided” praise overreaches.** R2 found 1,032 authored and 522 imported alternative-only structural relations meeting a 30% avoided threshold. Those counts establish only “other legal moves changed/lost this family while the played move did not.” They do not establish that the avoided relation was harmful, intended or causally important. R1 separately refused the cheap `hanging_piece_created` semantic label. A record must carry validated valence from an authored rule, disclosed engine convention, tablebase result or validated semantic event before any renderer says “you avoided a mistake”; otherwise render the unsigned relation or abstain. | 💡 R2 synthesis 2026-08-20; corrects D545 without deleting its historical measurement |
| D572 💡 | **The evidence compiler needs two independent gates: typed eligibility, then local delivery selection.** Eligibility asks whether this producer/version can support this semantic claim for this consumer, including operands, validation, valence and abstention. Selection asks which eligible facts are locally distinctive and fits a per-module card budget. The R2 baseline retained **108/108** predeclared rare rules events (castling, checkmate, promotion, last-of-role) while showing at most two families, but it deliberately emitted zero good/bad labels. An LLM may render the selected packet; it may not perform either gate or infer valence. | 💡 F1 manifest portion implemented 2026-08-21; downstream F2/F3 semantic selection or content migration remains explicitly open |
| D573 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-21.** Compact objectives now scroll inside a 3.25 rem text window, preserving the 192 px board floor wholly inside the position region instead of letting Timeline paint over it. The repaired A2 matrix hit every authored phone source and delivered the exact UCI for **18/18** click/drag/touch cells; the permanent browser gate independently hit-tests all six sources at 390×844. *Original:* At 390×844 five of six authored origin squares were hit-tested as timeline/control elements and exact delivery was 0/6 for every gesture. | ✅ compact board-first layout + exact-UCI browser regression |
| D574 🐞 | **The supposedly immutable pack-schema ID `0.13` named two different committed artifacts, and its first artifact was not JSON.** Commit `ade1d98` changed the exported version and `$id` to 0.13 while inserting three literal leading `+` characters into `schemas/drill_pack.schema.json`; `4f526c3`, 21 minutes later, removed them and changed the schema again without changing `$id` or `DRILL_PACK_SCHEMA_VERSION`. Any cache, pack or report naming 0.13 cannot identify which contract it used. The register guards lane order, not artifact immutability or parseability at each historical commit. | 🐞 historical re-derivation 2026-08-20; R6 pack-stability research |
| D575 🐞 | **The current drill-pack schema identifies itself as 0.27 and describes itself as “Living v0.25”.** `$id` and `DRILL_PACK_SCHEMA_VERSION` are test-bound at 0.27, but the human-readable version is a third hand-maintained copy with no equality check. This is small in consequence and exact in class: the schema resource can disagree with itself while the format suite stays green. | 🐞 verified 2026-08-20; `schemas/drill_pack.schema.json:3-5`; R6 |
| D576 💡 | **Pack documents cannot declare which pack-schema contract they require.** Their root `version` is the pack's content semver; no `$schema`, `schemaVersion`, capability set or bundle manifest is in the digested document. The server validates every document against whatever schema ships with that checkout. Additive evolution keeps many old packs loadable, but an automatic migrator cannot select a source format, an older self-host cannot refuse a newer pack by requirement, and a semantic evaluator change is invisible to the document. R6 must choose a derived bundle/capability contract or a stamped artifact version before Gate F can claim migration safety. | 💡 verified 2026-08-20; schema/type/digest audit, R6 |
| D577 🐞 | **JSON-schema compatibility currently produces a semantic false green.** All 92 current pack documents validate against schema 0.24 even though 0.26 added 82 current principle references and 0.27 migrated 436 current graduation entries into a reader-dependent typed lifecycle. An older runtime may accept those optional shapes and ignore their intended behaviour. Gate F needs required-capability negotiation and versioned evaluator semantics; “valid under an old schema” is not compatibility. | 🐞 measured 2026-08-20; `design/research/pack-primitive-stability.md` §2, R6 harness |
| D578 💡 | **There is no reusable drill-pack migration ladder or whole-corpus read-only planner.** The only measured breaking transition was paid as a same-commit 20-pack evidence relocation at 0.20; later waves rewrote 35 packs plus 13 principles at 0.26 and all 92 documents plus 32 ledgers at 0.27. Some steps are deterministic, while principle assignment and graduation-state splitting are explicitly human judgments. F3 must plan/apply mechanical changes separately and emit semantic residue for a person rather than infer it. | 💡 measured 2026-08-20; `design/research/pack-primitive-stability.md` §4, D560/Gate F |
| D579 🐞 | **A free-text “chess key” can be more dangerous than no key.** In R4, the key `pawn` admitted the generic pawn-ending passage for four rook-and-pawn-ending questions and excluded the correct rook-ending passage before semantic ranking ran. Opening-family strings also crossed distinct ECO identities. Applicability keys must be typed predicates with explicit match semantics (`phase`, material signature, exact position/transposition, opening range, motif/event and side), never substring tags; the evaluator needs collision/over-admission fixtures. | 🐞 measured 2026-08-20; R4 knowledge retrieval, input to evidence producer/applicability contract |
| D580 🐞 | **Skipper's stored knowledge chunks cannot identify or reproduce the artifact they came from.** The research tenant stored generic source URL/root/type/title/ingested-at metadata but no required licence, revision, source digest, chunk digest or source span, and no embedding provider/model identity. Atomic same-source replacement works; a same-dimension model change is invisible. A knowledge bundle must make those fields required and bind them into its build identity before any theory passage is learner-eligible. | 🐞 verified 2026-08-20; R4 lifecycle controls |
| D581 💡 | **The 1.0 theory layer should be a provenance compiler plus immutable local index, not a semantic service.** Fetch pinned allow-listed sources out of band; preserve licence/attribution/revision/span/digests; enrich with typed chess applicability; validate and publish a digest-addressed bundle with exact keys and SQLite FTS. The runtime selects only inside a deterministic eligible set and the LLM renders the bounded packet. Reconsider optional semantic ranking only when a larger cited corpus demonstrates an exact+FTS recall problem against a fresh gold set. | 💡 R4 synthesis 2026-08-20; design/owner ruling required before RFC |
| D582 🐞 | **Assistance configuration exposes producers and delivery mechanisms instead of learner intents.** `AssistanceSettings.svelte` renders all nine `AssistanceConfig` axes for all six profiles — **54 primary controls** — while the in-run panel asks for *passive markers*, *named-pattern guidance*, *human move split*, *corpus counts* and provider choices. The learner has to understand the evidence plumbing before choosing a workflow, and the human-model result is raw UCI plus percentages. This sharpens [[D484]]: keep the axes as an advanced implementation layer, but make named workflow/module presets the primary contract and render chess notation/meaning rather than transport tokens. | 🐞 verified 2026-08-20; platform R3 source audit |
| D583 🐞 | **Board “evidence lighting” bypasses the measured evidence compiler boundary.** `DrillScreen.svelte` filters the full `structuralReading().features` only by whether an observation contains the selected square, then draws every named square and renders every matching sentence. It performs no semantic-eligibility check, no local counterfactual selection, no significance/valence admission and no card/mark budget; `sight` and `evidence` share the same data branch once disclosure permits it. A square gesture is therefore a raw-census query, not a bounded guidance module. | 🐞 verified 2026-08-20; platform R3, downstream of [[D572]] |
| D584 🐞 | **The LLM evidence packet bypasses R2 in the same way as the board.** `guidance.ts:evidencePacket()` inserts the complete structural feature list, every matching plan and all deliverable authored text, then exposes their combined sentences to `VoiceProvider.render()`. No consumer/module identity, semantic eligibility, counterfactual denominator, selection reason or output budget reaches the provider. `voiceCheck()` is a containment check after generation; it cannot make an unselected packet relevant, and authored prose can widen the allowed vocabulary ([[D421]]). The LLM must receive only a compiler-admitted module packet, never the producer census. | 🐞 verified 2026-08-20; platform R3 source audit |
| D585 🐞 | **The optional ambient-assistance affordance is a dead control.** When `assistance.ambient === "on"`, `DrillScreen.svelte` renders a button labelled *Open assistance* whose title changes with state, but it has no `onclick`, link or expanded target. Enabling the setting produces an apparently actionable chess-piece icon that cannot open anything. | 🐞 verified 2026-08-20; platform R3 source audit |
| D586 🐞 | **The current LLM sentence seam can turn an admitted fact into a false absence, and `voiceCheck` accepts it.** In the predeclared R5 source-injection case, `gpt-4o-mini` ignored the canary but returned exactly *“No grounded hint is available”* while one eligible fact was present. That is not safe abstention: it is a contradictory proposition about the selector's output, and the shipped token-membership check returned valid. Absence must be emitted by the deterministic selector, never chosen by the prose renderer. | 🐞 measured 2026-08-20; `design/research/llm-renderer-contract.md` |
| D587 🐞 | **Schema-valid renderer output and valid fact IDs do not preserve the evidence boundary.** Both hosted typed R5 arms parsed 16/16 and returned admitted IDs 16/16, yet both dropped required theory citations; the Claude arm also removed *“disclosed final hint stage”* while retaining the authored move. JSON schema binds the envelope, not the proposition or its load-bearing qualifiers. Provenance, disclosure, exact numbers and visual bindings must render deterministically outside free prose. | 🐞 measured 2026-08-20; `design/research/llm-renderer-contract.md` |
| D588 🐞 | **A small local generator is not a safe self-host fallback.** `HuggingFaceTB/SmolLM2-360M-Instruct` passed the typed shape/fact-ID gate on only 1/16 R5 cases; its current sentence seam had three hard failures, including invented generic instructions on zero evidence. `voiceCheck` accepted 17 of the 18 local hard failures across both seams. Provider-off operation therefore requires deterministic module rendering; a local model may qualify later only as an optional provider. | 🐞 measured 2026-08-20; `design/research/llm-renderer-contract.md` |
| D589 💡 | **Renderer conformance needs a versioned promotion gate, not a provider name.** One pinned Claude sentence-seam run passed all 16 synthetic cases while another hosted model failed the same boundary and both typed arms lost citations. Before any model/prompt becomes a recommended preset, repeat the fixed negative corpus across runs and snapshot changes, record identity and error bounds, and fall back on any regression. | 💡 research follow-on from platform R5; not an implementation authority |
| D590 📊 | **History/time are base-model capabilities, but the original “position-only Maia” row was false for Tabiya.** The production request sends full history, the pinned sidecar runs `--use-uci-history`, and R9 already measured a median 29 cp history-vs-bare difference over 5,222 shared candidate rows. Otter's unreproduced ablation reports +5.24 pp from history and +2.38 pp from time beyond its own position-only baseline; that supports a replaceable capability adapter with declared history/time inputs, not a claim that Tabiya's current Maia lacks history. The remaining current gap is clock/time conditioning. | 📊 corrected by R11 2026-08-20; `design/research/bot-policy.md`; [Otter preprint](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.05206v1) |
| D591 📊 | **Chessiverse's documented “style steering” is mostly output selection, not a controllable style dial.** It says it lets neural nets vary, measures thousands of generated games, assigns Guardian→Savage labels after the fact, and currently does very little to influence that playstyle. Its controllable layers are narrower: candidate curation for suspicious moves, an extreme pawn-move heuristic, per-bot human-derived opening repertoires and statistical opening frequencies. R11 must therefore distinguish observed trait classification from controlled policy transformation; a label discovered after games cannot promise the same trait on demand. | 📊 verified vendor mechanism, 2026-08-20; expands [[D551]]/[[D561]]; [Chessiverse bot article](https://chessiverse.com/articles/how-chessiverse-bots-are-created) |
| D592 💡 | **Plausible-error validation needs a perceptual arm as well as a frequency/engine arm.** Chessiverse reports that users call a bot's one-move bishop drop inhuman even after making a larger blunder themselves; current Tabiya has neither an absurdity guard nor a blinded human-likeness protocol. Population frequency can establish that humans play an error, and engine loss can size it, but neither establishes that repeated learners experience the resulting bot as coherent. R11 may measure candidate mechanics now, but cannot close human-likeness or plan coherence without blinded multi-ply review. | 💡 research obligation, 2026-08-20; platform R11; [Chessiverse bot article](https://chessiverse.com/articles/how-chessiverse-bots-are-created) |
| D593 🐞 | **Maia's emitted policy vector is not the distribution `human_common` plays.** The pinned engine applies temperature 0.8 and top-p 0.92 to logits for `bestmove`, then separately emits raw softmax mass. On 837 cells the raw-vector expectation was 59.13 cp loss / 4.73 effective moves while the reconstructed production sampler was 19.84 cp / 2.43 and matched the one captured production sample at 19.57 cp. Any bot-policy calibration, repeat estimate or transform built on emitted mass must reconstruct or operate inside the sampler; the display vector cannot silently stand in for played policy. | 🐞 measured/corrected 2026-08-20; `design/research/bot-policy.md`, R11 harness |
| D594 📊 | **A bounded error guard is mechanically separable from Maia strength on the current corpus.** Filtering production-sampler moves ≥250 depth-12 cp behind the best removed all 0.39% measured severe mass, strengthened expectation by 1.27 cp and retained 100.2% of the explorer-match proxy. This earns a candidate interface, not a “human” claim: the guard must disclose its Stockfish information advantage, run on the complete model vector and pass blinded multi-ply coherence plus re-calibration before production. | 📊 R11 mechanical gate passed 2026-08-20; `design/research/bot-policy.md` |
| D595 📊 | **A simple trait transform can be observable without a material strength shift, but most naive labels are weaker than their multiplier sounds.** Pawn ×4 after the guard raised pawn moves +11.97 pp with −1.01 cp expected-loss shift and 98.8% explorer-match retention, passing the predeclared gate. Capture/check ×3 and quiet ×3 changed only +3.02/+2.24 pp and failed. A policy may promise a measured `pawn_move_rate` delta; it may not rename that one statistic “aggressive,” “positional” or a coherent personality. | 📊 R11 mechanical result 2026-08-20; `design/research/bot-policy.md` |
| D596 💡 | **Opening population, repertoire identity and cross-game adaptation are three different layers.** A normalized explorer book changed entropy, strength proxy and move overlap, but it has no stable bot identity; a curated repertoire can create identity but needs transposition/adherence/deviation rules; changing after a loss needs recorded cross-game memory and cannot be validated by same-position repeat arithmetic. The bot contract must keep all three separate so “uses human openings” does not overclaim a persona. | 💡 R11 synthesis 2026-08-20; Chessiverse mechanism + `design/research/bot-policy.md` |
| D597 🐞 | **Chessiverse's current personality page describes two incompatible products in one document.** Its FAQ says the test is a 3–5 minute preference quiz and explicitly “not” based on actual playing style; later the same page says it imports up to 100 games, computes 51 metrics/eight dimensions and matches 30+ archetypes. Vendor labels cannot serve as a metric specification when the input contract contradicts itself; R12 tests transparent game-derived metrics and records the disagreement rather than selecting the more flattering half. | 🐞 verified 2026-08-20; platform R12; [Chessiverse personality page](https://chessiverse.com/chess-personality) |
| D598 📊 | **Competitors disagree by at least 4× on the sample needed for a stable style profile.** Chessiverse states 12 games as a floor and “ideally 50+”; ChessBase reports that 100–200-game Style Reports can fluctuate significantly and that a more natural curve begins only above 200. Neither publishes a reproducible stability curve. The 1.0 contract needs a per-metric measured floor and uncertainty, not one global marketing number. | 📊 verified vendor-method disagreement 2026-08-20; R12 plan; [Chessiverse](https://chessiverse.com/chess-personality), [ChessBase follow-up](https://en.chessbase.com/post/self-assessment-vs-chessbase-18-style-report) |
| D599 💡 | **A style rate needs an opportunity denominator.** “This player captures/checks/pushes pawns often” conflates preference with positions and opponents that made those moves available. R12 therefore measures `played_event - legal_alternative_event_share` at each decision and aggregates the residual; raw event counts remain drill-down facts, not style dimensions. This is R2's counterfactual-denominator lesson applied longitudinally. | 💡 R12 method 2026-08-20; downstream of [[D569]]/[[D572]] |
| D600 💡 | **A stable metric vector does not imply stable archetypes.** R12 separates per-metric split-half stability, rating leakage, self re-identification and bootstrap cluster stability. A fun named type or grandmaster match is eligible only after the underlying metrics pass and the same reference corpus/version remains inspectable; otherwise the product may show continuous habits without pretending natural clusters exist. | 💡 R12 method 2026-08-20; narrows [[D562]] |
| D601 🐞 | **The public-player-history acquisition assumed by R12 is not currently a usable product contract.** Lichess profile pages still generate an export URL, but the tested API and generated download routes returned HTTP 404 for every account. R12 can use a frozen prefix of the CC0 monthly database for a bounded short-session experiment, but a production profile needs a durable import/upload contract and cannot quietly depend on a presently unavailable endpoint. | 🐞 verified 2026-08-20 before scoring; R12 acquisition correction; production source remains open |
| D602 🐞 | **“First sample size that passes” is not a sample-floor contract.** R12's first table made queenside castling pass at 12, fail at 25 and pass later; forcing choice passed at 50 then failed at 100 and 200. A display floor must persist through every larger measured sample, while the original table remains visible as a post-outcome correction rather than being rewritten. | 🐞 measured 2026-08-20; R12 analysis correction |
| D603 🐞 | **R12's first fianchetto-unblock denominator contained the event and therefore returned 586/586.** It admitted only moves where the screening knight moved, then asked whether that move opened the bishop ray; the intended population is every learner decision from a screened position. The all-ones descriptive was treated as an instrument alarm, the run was discarded, and the corrected harness records zeroes when the learner leaves the screen in place. | 🐞 caught and repaired before dossier publication 2026-08-20; reusable denominator guard |
| D604 💡 | **A transparent style vector is still behavioral identifying data.** R12's 12 retained literal metrics re-identify 35/36 accounts across disjoint 100-game halves while a rotated-label control identifies 0/36. Production profile storage, export/delete, sharing defaults and any public “GM twin” surface therefore belong to R18's privacy contract even when no opaque embedding or username is stored. | 💡 measured 2026-08-20; R12 → R18/O13/F12 |
| D605 🐞 | **The account lifecycle has deletion without portable export.** The client exposes password-confirmed `POST /auth/delete`, but there is no account/data export route in the server, client, docs or release tooling; a runtime `GET /auth/export` probe returns 405. Per-run PGN and pack/shape export cannot reconstruct progress, schedules, imports, repertoires, annotations, live/social state or future behavioral profiles. F12 must define a typed user-data bundle and its version/provenance rather than relabeling PGN as account export. | 🐞 R18 verified 2026-08-20; O13/F12 |
| D606 🐞 | **“Delete account” does not delete the learner's solo chess history and there is no run-delete escape hatch.** `deleteLearner` reassigns every owned run and active lease to `__legacy`; it deletes learner-bound progress rows/marks and repertoires, but retains run snapshots/imported games and registered artifacts. The UI discloses only “Shared runs are reassigned,” although the rule also covers a run with no other grant. **OWNER RULED 2026-08-21:** hard-delete private/solo runs and learner-only state; an anonymous link is revoked and does not retain a run; retain only authenticated shared dependencies or explicitly published artifacts as immutable identity-tombstoned records after an exact preview. Add per-run deletion and never reassign a private run to `__legacy`. | 🐞 R18 verified; retention ruled; F12-B draft owns implementation |
| D607 🐞 | **The two documented self-host entry points do not form one safe runnable default.** `make up` binds plain HTTP on all interfaces and forces `TABIYA_COOKIE_SECURE=false`; the release Compose omits TLS and therefore keeps secure cookies, while `docs/development.md` tells an operator to run that file directly and the TLS prerequisite lives only in `docs/identity-and-authorization.md`. The development profile is LAN-exposed with insecure cookies; the release profile needs an undeclared reverse proxy before login is a supported non-local workflow. F12 needs one explicit localhost-only profile and one complete TLS/reverse-proxy contract, not environment folklore. | 🐞 R18 configuration audit 2026-08-20; self-host/auth floor; O13/F12 |
| D608 🐞 | **SQLite persistence ships without an operator data-safety contract.** The named volume survives restart and startup automatically runs 23 in-place migrations, but no root/development/release document or Make target defines quiesced backup, restore verification, pre-upgrade snapshot, rollback compatibility or recovery after a failed migration. Copying `/data/chess-tabiya.sqlite` by hand is possible but cannot be called supported while WAL/quiescence and restore identity are unspecified. | 🐞 R18 operations audit 2026-08-20; O13/F12 |
| D609 🐞 | **Engine capability health stays green after the Maia provider disappears, and an uncached learner request hangs.** In the isolated engine profile, stopping only `tabiya-r18-maia-1` left `/capabilities` advertising Maia, all five policy modes and every surface as available. An exact cached selection still returned in 2 ms (the negative control); a new-position `human_common` request then returned zero bytes until the client-side 10 s timeout, after which capabilities were still unchanged. Provider-off honesty must be based on live/request-bounded health, distinguish cached service from new inference, and produce a typed unavailable state in the UI. | 🐞 R18 live failure injection 2026-08-20; availability/degradation; O13/F12 |
| D610 🐞 | **The release has component licences but no shipped rights/notice surface for the application as a whole.** The server image contains Stockfish's GPL text and exact source; the Maia image contains the pinned AGPL source, applied patch and checkpoint. The main web/server image contains no Tabiya `LICENSE`, corresponding source pointer, third-party notice inventory or visible legal/source/warranty link, even though it distributes the AGPL application plus GPL/MIT browser/server dependencies. F12 needs a generated release SBOM/notices/source-offer artifact and a visible app entry; this is an engineering compliance gap, not a legal conclusion about cure. | 🐞 R18 image/rights audit 2026-08-20; O13/F12 |
| D611 🐞 | **The learner runtime image copies the whole authoring corpus instead of a declared runtime bundle.** `COPY content content` includes candidates, jobs, evidence/source sidecars and local authoring provenance, not only the served packs/shapes/principles. At least one shipped manifest names an absolute `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-stronk-...` path. That creates avoidable image surface, leaks workstation path metadata and makes rights review cover files the runtime never consumes. F3/F4/F12 need an allow-listed immutable runtime-content export and a packaging check that rejects absolute/local paths. | 🐞 R18 release-image audit 2026-08-20; content boundary; F3/F4/F12 |
| D612 🐞 | **The product is keyboard-navigable around a pointer-only chessboard, not keyboard-playable.** `Chessboard.svelte` mounts one labelled `<div>` for Chessground with no interactive role, `tabindex`, square semantics, active-square state or key handler; generated pieces/squares provide no alternate move input. The drill's extensive keyboard contract controls rewind/branch/compare/replay/export only. Therefore a keyboard or screen-reader user can reach the run and operate its chrome but cannot make the core chess decision; `aria-label="Chessboard"` does not make the 64-square interaction accessible. F12 needs one board-input model shared by keyboard and assistive technology, with pointer/touch remaining equivalent projections. | 🐞 R18 source/semantics audit 2026-08-20; accessibility floor; O13/F12 |
| D613 🐞 | **The drill takes over unmodified Tab as a comparison shortcut and traps keyboard focus inside non-button drill chrome.** The region contract deliberately calls `preventDefault()` for Tab whenever the target is not an input, button or link; `<summary>` is omitted from that exception. In the served R18 probe, normal Tab navigation reached the Assistance summary and then repeated that same element for the remaining 28 probes instead of advancing through the drill. This conflicts with the browser's primary focus-navigation key and makes the otherwise focusable controls unreachable in document order. F12 must reserve unmodified Tab/Shift+Tab for focus traversal, move comparison to a non-conflicting command, and assert a complete forward/reverse focus route. | 🐞 R18 live keyboard probe + source trace 2026-08-20; accessibility floor; O13/F12 |

The image also has no generated dependency/rights manifest. Before 1.0, F8/F12 must choose and pin an explicit CPU/GPU matrix, build the CPU default without proprietary CUDA wheels, report exact accelerator requirements, and generate notices/SBOMs for each variant. | 🐞 R18 built-image package inventory 2026-08-20; FOSS/resource floor; O8/O13/F8/F12 |

| D617 ✅ | **OWNER RULING 2026-08-20 — the authoritative evidence contract is a versioned, compiled producer→evidence→consumer manifest, not another hand-maintained capability table.** Producers declare versioned outputs, semantics, grounding, sign, exactness/confidence and abstention; consumer modules declare accepted versions, latency, disclosure timing, fact/form budgets and whether recommendations/PVs are legal. The build derives their joins and fails unexplained producers or consumers; explicit `inspector_only`, `experimental` and `retired` dispositions are legal. RFCs may claim future resources, but shipped runtime truth comes from code declarations. This adopts R1/R2's eligibility→selection→rendering split and the landed-vs-claimed discipline of `shared-resource-registers`; F1 must reconcile rather than duplicate them. | ✅ O1 ruled; intent amendment and F1 acceptance/implementation remain open |
| D618 ✅ | **OWNER RULING 2026-08-20 — semantic eligibility precedes relevance selection, and raw truth is not automatically learner guidance.** Raw geometry, engine/corpus readings and support atoms remain available to the explicit inspector. A learner-facing semantic event must name typed subjects/objects/squares, gained/lost/preserved/avoided sign, grounding, exactness/confidence, abstention and positive plus hard-negative validation. Sign alone supplies no praise, blame, causality or “blunder”; those require validated valence. Only eligible events enter deterministic local-alternative selection with critical overrides, deterministic ties, module budgets and honest empty output. The measured 20%/two-fact setting remains an R3 candidate, not a frozen default; exact v1 family membership remains O2's consumer-validation residue. | ✅ O2 foundation ruled; detector-family admission remains research-gated |
| D619 ✅ | **OWNER RULING 2026-08-20 — preserve the full configurable primitive set, but expose it through intent modules, opinionated workflow presets and real ceilings rather than source-shaped settings.** Every registered primitive has a declared configuration or disposition somewhere: learner-facing advanced/custom composition where safe, explicit analysis inspector for raw evidence/providers, and authoring/operator configuration for experimental or non-learner controls. Normal Review, Just Play, drills, campaign, coach/stream and Analyze workflows select composed modules and defaults; they do not ask a nontechnical player to toggle Stockfish/Maia/classifier sources. Each module declares whether it may reveal sight, pattern, threat, theory, evaluation, candidate moves or PVs. Theory-only and honest empty nudges are first-class. Exact sight may be requested pre-commit without ranking a move; proactive blunder prevention is legal only in an explicitly selected Support preset (principally Just Play/free play), not the rehearsal default. Guided rehearsal defaults to post-commit nudges and progressive requested hints; Analyze Freely may expose evaluations/moves/PVs. Touch, click, keyboard and assistive controls are equivalent; hover is additive. Session ceilings can remove capability and presets can never raise them. Anonymous share links are revoked and do not create retention. Retain only runs another authenticated learner currently depends on, and explicitly registered pack/shape artifacts, as immutable tombstones: account identity is detached, all real run writers are removed, and surviving readers keep the shared history. Fixed there (enumeration follows `showDests`; illegal-move refusal stays unconditional) — **the class outlives the fix**: any future screen-reader, textual, or voice projection of board state must inherit the visible board's assistance conditions, or accessibility becomes an assistance bypass. `design/05`'s input-equivalence language is the intent-tier home | 💡 open, found 2026-08-21 |

| D542 🐞 | **90.2% of the structural reading carries no discriminating information, and five of the highest-volume detectors fire MORE on the move you did not play.** `design/research/classifier-coverage-and-noise.md` `[V]` 2026-08-18, over 754 transitions / 717 played moves / 19,636 legal alternatives at HEAD. **Per-kind lift spans 11×** — `named_structure` **9.96×**, `doubled_pawn` 7.35×, `passed_pawn` 5.11× … down to `direct_attack_count` **0.87×**. **Five kinds sit below 1.0** (`line_blockers` 17.97 obs/position, `piece_reach_count` 8.29, `pawn_safe_square` 8.29, `direct_attack_count` 7.88, `bishop_on_shade` 2.25) and are **69.9% of the reading by volume**; with 13.00 unconditional observations that is **90.2%**, leaving a conditional remainder of **6.27** per position. **So [[D78]]'s 1.004× aggregate is not a property of the detectors — it is the surface showing all of them at once** | 💡 open, found 2026-08-18 |

Chess.com Insights, Lichess Insights' 23 dimensions and WintrChess's open-source classifier contain **none**. *"Central space", "backward pawn", "outpost"* describe the **coach prose**, not the classifier. **We ship 17 structural detectors they do not.** What we lack is the tactical family — **hanging piece, fork, pin/skewer, pawn islands, rook on 7th, castling rights, development, trades** — whose **atomic geometry** is free arithmetic over primitives `structure.ts` already imports, with **three atoms already computed and thrown away** (`undevelopedMinors`, `capturedRole`, the FEN castling field). **R1 correction (D565): the semantic tactic label is not free geometry** — it needs eligibility/value and functional consequence, sometimes bounded search. Chess theory is a citable, centuries-old literature; `design/02` already ruled pack prose **CC-BY-SA-4.0** precisely so *"Wikibooks' reusable idea-prose"* is a legitimate source. **The format anticipated this and every entry declined it.** Consequence for law 8: an LLM naming which *cited* principle a claim rests on is **rendering validated evidence**, not manufacturing chess truth — the law forbids **ungrounded** claims, and the fix is to ground the principles, not to withhold the classification | 💡 open, found 2026-08-17 |
| D531 💡 | **OWNER RULING 2026-08-17 — the principle registry GROWS where a real gap shows, and classification is claude's with review.** Answers `feedback-delivery` open question 8. **(a)** Author new principles when several claims cluster on a rule the 13 do not express; each carries its `counterCase`. **(b)** The 94 pairings (63 mandatory, 31 optional) are **classified by claude against cited principles and reviewed by the owner**, on the owner's reasoning that cross-referencing established theory is not manufacturing it — see [[D530]]. **(c) The precondition this creates, and it is now the wave's real first job: principles must be regrounded to `standsOn: "chess_tradition"` with real citations before pairings against them mean anything.** Pairing 94 claims against 13 self-referential rules would produce provenance that looks stronger than it is, which is the [[D45]] failure — an authority label the format does not enforce | 💡 owner ruling 2026-08-17 |
| D532 💡 | **OWNER RULING 2026-08-17 — `permittedAssistance` gets a real per-session-kind ceiling; the dead argument is given a job rather than pinned or deleted.** Answers `assistance-controls`' one owner question. The field takes `sessionKind` and never reads it, so `pack`, `position` and `imported` receive byte-identical permissions. **Refused: pinning the no-op** (a guard that cannot fail is not a decision) **and deleting the field** (which collides with `teacher-surface`'s normative interface block). **The ruling narrows a shipped surface, so it is design intent and not a defect fix** — the RFC must state what each of the five contexts may never show, and `design/05` §3-forms' *"each get their own defaults"* becomes a ceiling rather than a default. **Note against [[D78]]/[[D359]]**: this is a ceiling on what may be *offered*, never a floor on what is *shown* — the all-on state measures 978 words / 247 seconds at one node, so a ceiling that raises defaults would make the surface worse | 💡 owner ruling 2026-08-17 |
| D526 ✅ | **CLOSED by Feedback Stage 1: criterion 16 now measures multi-ply columns instead of an instrument that made its own answer 100%.** The invalid one-ply N=2/4/8 result remains preserved. The harness continues each column deterministically to eight plies and reports N=2 **8/116**, N=4 **22/229**, N=8 **70/450 (15.6%)**; the reopening threshold does not fire. Same family as [[D420]] and [[D444]]. | ✅ `a64e6c5`; `tools/feedback-delivery-harness/` |
