| D528 🐞 | **A column with ZERO plies past the fork makes `common = ∅` and silently disables CR1 for the whole comparison — a real narrow cannot-fire that CR3 does not name.** CR3 enumerates the degenerate cases deliberately (*"named, not discovered"*) and misses this one. It is not hypothetical in this corpus: **19 of 44** fork sets have a 1-ply column, so the neighbouring case is the common case | 💡 open, found 2026-08-17 |
| D529 📊 | **The authored corpus cannot exercise the surface its own comparison cap advertises: no fork is wider than 3 columns.** Measured over 44 real spine fork sets: **38 binary, 6 ternary, 0 at N ≥ 4**, against `MAX_COMPARISON_BRANCHES = 8`. The median fork's shortest column runs **2 plies** past the fork. **CR5's predicted direction is confirmed on real data** (N=2 86.4% → N=3 96.6%) — but the driver is **how early authored branches terminate**, not the intersection argument CR5 gives. So every claim about N ≥ 4 behaviour in this repo is synthetic, including criterion 16's threshold | 📊 measured 2026-08-17 |
| D522 🐞 | **A clearance kind's predicate already holds on all 16 entries assigned to it, so the migration's first run would have retired sixteen debts by doing nothing.** Found 2026-08-17 by running the predicate rather than reading it: for the 16 remaining rule-6 entries the D predicate (*"fires on at least one of this pack's positions"*) is **already satisfied today, at 2–24 firings each**. `open-centre-ruy-exchange` is the sharpest case — its blocking statement is *"fires on only three of its ten"* and **the predicate is satisfied by that very number**. It would also have reddened acceptance criterion 3 on `philidor-passive-rook-convert`. **This is [[D451]] reaching a specification instead of a criterion**: a test whose passing is guaranteed by the state it was written to describe. Fixed by a non-vacuity rule (`GRADUATION_CLEARANCE_VACUOUS`) that generalises to every kind | 💡 open, found 2026-08-17 |
| D523 🐞 | **Three of six clearance kinds join their evidence through a pointer grammar the shipped code enforces, and the RFC stated it for one.** At the symbol: **A** — `assessmentGrounding` reads `document.objective.grading.assessedBy` **directly and never looks at `subject`**, so any other subject is decorative; **C** — `validateClaimBindings` raises `CLAIM_POINTER_INVALID` on any pointer but `^/feedbackClaims/\d+/text$`, so **a 55-entry class could have been migrated with subjects whose predicate can never hold**; **D** — the census joins on a shape id or shape+plan pair. **[[D471]] found one instance of this and did not ask whether it was a class** — that is the lesson: a wrong-join finding should always be re-run across every kind that joins | 💡 open, found 2026-08-17 |
| D524 🐞 | **`content/drafts/` now ships to production and three documents still argue from the premise that it does not.** Owner ruling [[D502]] landed the draft channel: `.dockerignore` no longer excludes it, `verify-packaging.mjs` asserts the opposite, and `loadDefault` loads drafts unconditionally on the `community` channel. **`graduation-clearance` §0.1's whole motivation was refuted at HEAD** and is corrected to the narrower true claim (the official shelf is empty; graduation is the only route in). [[D246]] pins **versions**, not **premises** — a document can stay version-current and argue from a world that no longer exists | 💡 open, found 2026-08-17 |
| D525 🐞 | **[[D468]]'s fix pins the two paths cited today while the rule it protects admits four families.** `Dockerfile:32-33` now copies both cited sources and `verify-packaging.mjs:88-99` pins them — so `GRADUATION_RULING_UNCITED` correctly **stays whole** in `validatePackDocument`, and moving it would have deleted a guarantee. But §3.2a(1)'s allow-list admits four families, so **the first acceptance citing a design doc reproduces D468 as a boot failure**. Closed for today's corpus; open for the one `graduation-clearance` enlarges | 💡 open, found 2026-08-17 |
| D533 🐞 | **CI, the devcontainer, production, and host development executed four different Stockfish contracts.** `ubuntu-latest` installed Ubuntu 24.04's Stockfish 16, the production and devcontainer Bookworm images installed 15.1, and the host ran Homebrew Stockfish 18. Changing runner OS could not align the system: Ubuntu 26.04 is preview-only and packages Stockfish 17. Closed by pinning the official Stockfish 18 release commit and checksums through one installer consumed by Actions, the devcontainer and both production architectures; the real handshake now requires version 18 | ✅ closed 2026-08-17 by the Stockfish environment-parity fix |
| D534 🐞 | **One declaration-census test recomputed the full repository census five times and crossed its functional timeout only on CI.** It took 12.9 s locally and 24.0 s on the runner against a 20 s limit. Closed by combining the four namespace-disjoint mutations into one mutated census: every assertion remains, complete scans fall from five to two, and the affected test completes in about 5.5 s without weakening its 20 s timeout | ✅ closed 2026-08-17 by the declaration-census work bound |
| D518 🐞 | **The explorer wave is 0 of 60 mechanical, not 22 of 60 — and the wrong number is what made the whole content lane look tractable.** `attachExplorerEvidence` requires an `EXPLORER_RATIONALE` entry in `provenance.sources` and **0 of 50 packs carry one**; it writes exactly **one span**, while `CLAIM_ASSERTION_UNDECLARED` (**error**) refuses leftover machine tokens and **every one of the 60 claims carries 6–38 tokens**. So the largest job in the mechanical content lane has **no mechanical path at all**, and law 8 forbids generating the 60 authored sentences that would unblock it. **Claude reported *"27 mechanical rows, five jobs, three of them a shipped `make` target"* to the owner on the strength of the 22 figure** — that framing is withdrawn. Compounding it: **all 18 ledger-less packs declare `assessedBy.kind` of `(none)`/`authored`**, so `verifyDraft` refuses every one, and there is no mechanical route to those sidecars either. **What survives as genuinely mechanical: 21 text edits ([[D470]]), 4 denominators, and 7 engine claims across 2 packs** | 💡 open, found 2026-08-17 |
| D519 🐞 | **[[D227]]/[[D257]] closed by EXCLUDING the fixtures from discovery, and nothing was relocated — so one report now prints both denominators.** The six `*.browser.json` files are still in `content/drafts/`; `isPackDocumentName` merely skips them. Consequence measured: **`make expression-census` reports `corpus.packs: 56` alongside `totals.packs: 50` in the same report**, and `make graduation-report` still reports **56**. The rows are correctly closed — discovery *is* fixed — but *"the denominators are fixed"* is not what shipped, and every figure quoted from those two instruments must now say which of the two numbers it means | 💡 open, found 2026-08-17 |
| D520 🐞 | **`claim-binding.ts`'s `legalSuccessors`/`positions` omit promotion pieces, so they generate ILLEGAL FENs — and `tablebase.moveCensus@v1` is therefore unsatisfiable in 3 of the 12 tablebase packs.** A census that must cover *every legal successor* cannot be satisfied when the enumerator does not produce them. This is not a coverage gap, it is a **cannot-fire** ([[D428]]'s governing distinction), and it silently caps the one assertion family the corpus was expected to lean on | 💡 open, found 2026-08-17 |
| D521 🐞 | **`make graduation-report` WRITES `content/accepted-conditions.md`, so the repo's headline graduation instrument cannot be run in a measurement pass without dirtying the corpus it measures.** Exactly [[D446]]'s defect — which codex fixed for the Q8 harness at `5c66680` — surviving in the more load-bearing tool. The work-order pass had to recompute its numbers read-only instead (and reproduced them exactly: 56/220/30/43). **A measuring instrument that mutates its subject cannot be run by a reviewer, which is how its output goes stale** | 💡 open, found 2026-08-17 |
| D514 🐞 | **The only extant statement of who owns the binding wave is inside a FROZEN file, and it names itself.** `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md:1725` reads *"**answered.** This RFC owns it"* at HEAD; the archiving commit `5b65048` changed exactly **two lines** of that file (`Status`, `Planning`) `[V]`. RFC-0000 rule 3 and **law 2** make it immutable, so **the claim cannot be corrected where it lives**. This sharpens [[D476]] from *the owner was archived* to **the ownership statement is now unfixable at its only site** — which is why re-homing must happen **in the archiving commit** rather than afterwards. **Root cause found in the same pass:** ownership was recorded in an **Open question**, whose contract is *resolved before `accepted`* — so **closing OQ5 deleted the ownership rather than transferring it.** Five documents now say nobody owns the wave; zero own it | 💡 open, found 2026-08-17 |

So **a string-equality check reports 11 failures, 10 of them spurious**; a token check reports the one real failure. | D517 📊 | **An unblocking commit repaired the sentence it was looking at and left the same block standing four columns away.** `532c7e2` rewrote `feedback-delivery`'s status cell to *"UNBLOCKED 2026-08-16"* and left column 4 ending *"Cannot be accepted until the owner rules the withhold/deliver fork"* — **the block and its lifting in one row, in one commit** `[V]`. Record-only; the row is since rewritten. Worth keeping because it is the strongest available evidence that **a prose correction fixes the sentence a reader happened to open**, and it is the direct argument for structured re-homing over a *"remember to update the dependents"* clause. Related: [[D433]]'s window is **3 h 06 m, not "a day"**, and `5b65048` **already had both files open** — moving that remedy from *notify the dependent* to *one clause in the paragraph the archiver reads* | 📊 record-only, found 2026-08-17 |

Every one of our budgets is met comfortably (restart 37.9 ms perceived median, rewind 11.9 ms, opponent reply 128.0 ms, first Syzygy probe 33.0 ms), and **that is not a differentiator**. The project has assumed a latency advantage since the owner's field report of 2026-08-11; **the advantage has to come from the loop, not the clock** | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |

What is missing is the **disclosure**: the silence stated once, and presets that **name** today's default rather than move it. **These are two different claims — a correct invariant and a hostile first run — and nothing in the design tier separates them** | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |

| D487 🐞 | **REFRESHED 2026-08-20: the index that exists to answer *"what is unscheduled"* still declares itself complete and remains frozen at D487-era truth while the ledger now reaches D640.** The new audit parses **589 unique ledger ids: 234 closed/rejected and 355 open**. So the gates were closed against a corpus of 56 packs that production never loads ([[D481]]). **B1–B11 are not lies; they measure whether a CAPABILITY exists in the codebase, and the owner measured whether a PRODUCT exists on screen.** The deeper defect is that no gate criterion was written in a form a user could fail — B2's is *"each complete one fixture run"*, which is a test passing. **A gate that cannot be failed by a person is not a gate on the product** | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |

It would have passed, and its passing would have meant nothing.** Fixed there by requiring three response models with the published bracket taken as the **minimum** across them, including the saturating family the RFC itself names as its failure mode. **Every AC in this repo that validates a model by simulating under it inherits the defect** | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |

Measured cost: `maia-endgame-fidelity.md` has Maia converting only **88.1–91.9%** of won endgames, so the seal **over-credits at a measured rate** — and does so **inside the ≤10-piece regime the same RFC refuses to rate at all**. The defensible sentence is *"the journey is approximately the width of the estimable window"*; *"the journey fits"* is not, and the RFC was one clause from saying it. **A caveat that survives in one section and not in the summary is a caveat that will not survive the next reader** | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |

Every *refusal* code the format ships lives in a union a checker can walk; every *authoring* code is a string literal at a call site, so "which codes exist" is answerable only by grepping, and "which declared code has no emitter" is not answerable at all. That is exactly how `GRADUATION_RESOLVED_WITHOUT_RESOLUTION` and `GRADUATION_ACCEPTED_WITHOUT_RULING` could be specified in an RFC's §6 and be absent from the tree with nothing noticing. **Remedy proposed: a `PackIssueCode` union**, which makes the declaration census total over both halves instead of one. Found 2026-08-16 by the `dead-vocabulary` cross-review | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D463 💡 | **OWNER AMENDMENT 2026-08-16 — the live-marker ruling's *"permanently"* narrows to *"for the duration of live play"*, and the 2026-08-15 record is left intact beside it.** The 2026-08-15 ruling gated `human_divergence` behind the stronger `humanSplit` permission and accepted, in writing, that *"the marker leaves participants and spectators entirely, on every run, permanently"*. **A teacher reviewing a student's submitted game is mechanically a spectator**, so `permanently` caught a case nobody was ruling on. The narrowing follows from the 2026-08-16 *"ship no deferral"* ruling: a submission-granted teacher gets the **run host's own table** on a finished, disclosed run with no live session open, and the marker's arm is `permission.humanSplit === "free"`, so it arrives **with no change to `liveAdmitted`**. **Live play is unchanged** — the contamination concern the gate exists for is *structurally absent* once the game is over and disclosure has passed. **The third-value permission stays refused**; the reviewer reaches the marker on the host's arm, not through a weaker one. This is the reversal §6.2 recorded as *"cheap"*, spent on one conjunct. **Implementer note that matters more than the ruling:** criterion 6 needs *"non-reviewing spectator"* in **two** clauses, and its `liveMarkers` half is an `AssistanceContext` object-literal unit test with **no session layer in it at all** — so the compatibility claimed *"by construction"* is actually held by the `granted_via = 'submission'` conjunct, i.e. **by fixture convention**. Do not weaken that conjunct | 💡 owner ruling 2026-08-16 |
| D462 💡 | **OWNER RULING 2026-08-16 — the claim-delivery sequencing fork is answered with BOTH, in order: ship the surface, then run the binding wave before anyone plays.** The fork was *ahead* (deliver now, 97 of 98 claims reading *"author-declared, no machine record"*) versus *behind* (run the wave first, D77 stays open meanwhile and the wave has no surface to validate against). **Both arms were priced on losses that a single fact removes: nobody has played a run since 2026-08-12.** The *ahead* arm's cost was a first impression with no one to receive it; the *behind* arm's cost was a wave with nothing to validate against, which shipping first supplies. **This is the third fork this session that dissolved when the constraint was removed rather than chosen between** (after C6 and teacher mode), and the pattern is now explicit enough to be a rule rather than an observation. **Three obligations the ruling creates, recorded so it is not read as merely "do both":** Job 1 is **not complete when it ships** — the acceptance record carries the delivered share at the **end** of the wave, and day zero's 98/196 is a *starting* measurement; the wave's scope is **not only sourcing**, since **63 of the 98 withheld claims trip `CLAIM_AUTHOR_LABEL_REQUIRED` and need a pack edit**, so a wave planned as two instrument runs would miss two thirds of its own work; and **the no-learner-sees-it property expires** — if a play session happens before the wave completes, the ruling's premise is gone and the fork is live again  **Mechanical half measured 2026-08-16 and it comes back clean:** the 63 mandatory pack edits span **33 packs and all three phases**, and **all 13 principle entries list all three phases**, so `CLAIM_PRINCIPLE_OFF_PHASE` cannot refuse any of them. **The registry will not *refuse* the 94 claims — a human must judge each one**, which is why this is the wave's blocking question and not a lint, and why law 8 puts a floor under how fast the wave can go.| 💡 owner ruling 2026-08-16 |

| D451 🐞 | **An acceptance criterion fired and was scored a pass, so the RFC was one commit from archiving a paragraph its own criterion refuted.** A10 read: *"the won-root enrichment must not move… if it moves materially, §3.2's reading of the census is wrong — this criterion exists to be able to fail."* **It moved: 1.571× → 1.178×**, from an interval *excluding* the 9.34% uniform expectation (exact p = 0.0101) to one *covering* it (p = 0.414). Recorded as a pass because 11.01% sits **0.4 pp** inside the pre-fix interval. **The losing arm is the cleaner refutation and was never reported at all**: 14/223 → 2/223, 2.096× → **0.300×**, p = 0.0090 → 0.073 — and in a losing root DTZ orders *descending*, so the pre-fix enrichment ran **against** the DTZ gradient and could only have been the alphabet. **69% of the won-root excess over uniform was lexicographic, not DTZ.** The remedy is unaffected — §3.3 clause 2 already covered all three arms, so the winning side is fixed *by specification* — but the **diagnosis** was wrong, and D371's problem statement localised to drawn roots a bias that was never confined there. **A criterion whose failure clause fires and is read as a pass is worse than one that cannot fail, because it produces a record of having been tested.** Corrected in the RFC body before archiving | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |

The old `dtzTiedRoots: {win: 130}` remains unvalidated until a newly retained corpus is run; no closure is claimed |
| D458 ✅ | **A client-surface scan excluded the one client file that carried the figures it forbade.** The filter covered only 27 of 42 non-test sources, and its `orderingBasis…best` arm was vacuous. **CLOSED 2026-08-16:** the scan covers every non-test `.ts`/`.svelte` client source, client wire types no longer pin the measured numeric literals, and non-vacuity is established by first asserting the runtime contract really declares `orderingBasis` before proving the client never references it |
| D448 🐞 | **`RunService.evidence` serves rung-3 Stockfish to any granted reader on any disclosed run with no role check — so a LIVE spectator gets engine numbers that `/human-split` refuses on the same run.** Found 2026-08-16 by the `teacher-surface` author round while specifying the review rail. By `live-marker-quality`'s **own L4(b)** — *"a route that disagrees with the table is a defect in the route"* — this is a route defect, and it runs in the **narrowing** direction: the table is stricter than the route, so the refusal that exists is the one nobody can reach. `teacher-surface` §5.2 **deliberately does not take it**, because the surface belongs to the RFC that is mid-implementation on it. Note the shape: two RFCs each specified a correct rule for the surface they owned, and the hole is in neither — it is in a third route that predates both | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D449 🐞 | **`permittedAssistance` has no register for its inputs, and it now takes two that are neither `role` nor persisted in the run.** The `teacher-surface` review rail adds viewer-side inputs to a function whose input space nothing enumerates. **A third would collide the way migration numbers did before 2026-08-12** — which is the precedent worth citing, because that collision was fixed by adding a register rather than by being careful. Same species as [[D423]]: a shared resource with no single place that records who holds what | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D450 🐞 | **`docs/live-sessions.md` §Accepted limitation asserts a permission rule in prose that no test reads.** *"Role may cap it lower… never raise it"* is a normative statement about the assistance path, living in a doc, enforced by nothing. Same producerless-claim class as [[D257]]'s *"47 authored packs"* — a figure or rule computed by no code and asserted by no test — one tier up, on a **security-shaped** rule rather than a corpus count. **The distinction that makes this worse than D257:** a wrong denominator misleads us; a wrong permission rule misleads whoever implements against the doc | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D444 🐞 | **Two readers of the same ledger disagree by construction: `expression-census`'s `backedClaims` double-counts and never validates.** It is a **sum over `(pack, evidenceType)` citations**, so the 6 corpus claims carrying two machine labels count twice — and it **never calls `validateClaimBindings`**, so it counts what a ledger *declares* rather than what the checker would *admit*. The two numbers agree today only because the joined set is a singleton, which is the worst possible reason for two instruments to agree. `feedback-delivery`'s criterion 2a asserted equality between them and was therefore **unsatisfiable**; replaced with a subset assertion plus a named-refusal explanation per divergence. **The general shape: an instrument that reports on a validator without running it is a second opinion that has never read the case** | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D445 🐞 | **`authorSegments` locates spans with `indexOf`, so a repeated sentence is measured against the wrong window.** Where the same segment text occurs twice in one claim, every occurrence resolves to the first — so the attribution guard reads a span the author did not mean, and a duplicate segment can be attributed correctly at one site and silently misattributed at the other. Small today, and the class is the one this repo keeps paying for: a locator that assumes uniqueness over authored prose, which is the least unique input we have | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D446 ✅ | **The Q8 harness reruns against the current corpus, but its committed artefact is stale and the harness mutates the tree it measures.** `tools/q8-feedback-surface-harness/q8-output.md` still records 37 packs / 634 transitions, while the current run measures 50 packs / 754 transitions. The test writes the report with `writeFileSync`, so verification dirties the working tree and cannot safely join `make verify`. | ✅ closed by the Q8 harness integrity pass — normal runs compare generated output to the committed artifact without writing; UPDATE_Q8=1 is the explicit refresh path |
| D447 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-21 by shared-resource-registers.** Landed head 23 is derived from `storage.ts`; only declarations from current Active RFCs populate the live ladder, so a claimant leaving cannot remain silently counted. | ✅ closed by `shared-resource-registers` |
| D437 💡 | **R10 is REVERSED (owner ruling 2026-08-16) — cross-learner comparison ships as the cohort standing, and the limitation ships with it.** Owner: *"add leaderboards and cross-learner comparison… maybe local chess clubs want to use us at some point? add it properly, re-evaluate the refusal and why it was there and what it unlocks."* Designed in `learner-rating` §10a. **The cohort is an existing `classrooms` row** — no second grouping object, no second invitation flow, no new role or token scope. **Consent is transposed, not duplicated**: `teacher-surface` §2.1 says enrolment authorises *addressing* and *"does not authorise reading anything at all"*, and a standing **is** a reading surface, so it takes the submission shape instead — **no code path may derive a `standing_members` row from a `classroom_members` row alone**, and `{op:"publish"}` takes no handle argument, so R10(c) is enforced by the *absence of the parameter* rather than by a check. **Three layers — marks (default), record (opt-in), rating (opt-in, off).** The honour roll is the default on measurement, not deference: RD ≤ 60 needs ≈34 games at parity and ≈67 at the skirt, so **every rating cell in a new club is empty for weeks**. **Rank by results, group by rating** — the rating is never a rank, sort key, seed or tiebreak, because the resolvable step is 60 Elo against a ±300-band-point interval at the publication floor; AC-14 makes it falsifiable by requiring that **permuting every member's rating changes the returned order by zero bytes**. One deliberate divergence from the 4545 precedent: their shading is placement-derived, ours is event-derived, so **no mark is a function of anyone else's result**. **The self-cheating limitation is stated at four normative sites** — the standing view, the publish confirmation, §7.4 obligation 6 (so later surfaces inherit it), and `/capabilities` — and AC-16 fails if any is missing. R10 survives as three narrower clauses | 💡 owner ruling 2026-08-16 |
| D438 🐞 | **"A manufactured number versus a record of what happened" does not separate our rating from a leaderboard — and the document making that separation had already argued the opposite.** R10's refusal leaned on the rating being a manufactured claim. But `learner-rating` §1 argues at length that this rating *is not* manufactured — *"arithmetic over rules facts… says what happened, never what was good"* — and if that holds, manufacturedness cannot be what excludes it from a table. **The distinction proves too much.** What replaces it is **provenance**: 4545's standings were corrupted three times *under arbiters*, while ours are unwitnessed games in a tab we do not police, with six of nine assistance axes unrefusable. **Relocating the defect from law 8 to provenance is what made it addressable** — and it generated three design rules the old framing never could have (marks-first default, provenance stated at the point of comparison, witnessed play as a possible cohort requirement). Barth's *"the only thing a global leaderboard manages to tell you is that you suck"* narrows to **global**, his own word; the own-history ground narrows to a default; the self-cheating ground survives whole. **General guard: before refusing a surface because a number is manufactured, check whether the document doing the refusing has already argued that it is not** | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D439 💡 | **A campaign boss is a full game, not a pack (owner ruling 2026-08-16) — and the climax act is the one act that cannot carry a result.** Encoded in `learner-rating` §5.3a as a **`position` session played to `terminalOutcome`**, because `objective` and `checkpoints` are in the pack schema's top-level `required` list — so a pack **cannot exist** without an authored objective, which is R2's first refused input. Encoding the boss as a game makes the refused input **absent rather than ignored**, which is the difference between a rule and a hope. Consequences: the map gains **two verdict producers**, and which one seals a node becomes a property of the node; **Act II only** — Act I is refused twice (by R1, and because `THEORY_NEEDS_AUTHORED_BOUNDARY` + `BOUNDARY_NEEDS_PLY_HORIZON` make a `follow_theory` objective *structurally incapable* of a rules-terminal result) and Act III by R1 plus `PERFECT_TABLEBASE_OUT_OF_RANGE`'s seven-piece ceiling against R5's floor of 21 pieces. **So the campaign's climax cannot produce the outcome the campaign rates.** Material cost is zero where it applies (`plan` mode 14/14 at ≥21 pieces). **Six changes owed to `design/06-campaign.md`, named and not written (law 5):** §5's encounter vocabulary (an encounter not bounded by `plyHorizon`, plus which producer seals which object); §5's act ladder; §2a's difficulty-availability axis (its four labels describe a *position*, a result describes an *encounter* — needs a fifth label or a second axis); §2b's boss table; §5's corpus claim (*"36 of 37, median 12"* is now **50 of 56, median 11**); and §2c/§5's rewind ruling **iff** question 11 resolves toward R11 | 💡 owner ruling 2026-08-16 |
| D440 🐞 | **26 of 56 packs declare `resolveAt: "terminal"` and 25 of them also declare a `plyHorizon` — 20 at 7–13 ply — so the corpus already claims a terminality it cannot deliver, and nothing validates the pair.** Found while encoding [[D439]]: the boss-as-game ruling exists because a pack cannot reach a rules-terminal result, and the corpus turns out to be **asserting that it does**, at scale, today. A pack that resolves at terminal and stops at ply 11 resolves at *whatever ply 11 holds*, which is not a terminal position. **One lint rule fixes it**, and until it exists every `resolveAt: "terminal"` figure quoted from this corpus is unreliable in the same way the browser-fixture denominators are ([[D227]]) | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D441 🐞 | **`learner_marks` and milestones are the same object class, and rendering them into one surface would falsify a doc by a UI decision that looks like tidying.** `docs/return-and-progression.md:48-49` currently forbids a learner rating; marks and milestones are structurally alike enough that a designer merging the two lists would be making a normative change while believing they were reducing clutter. **Recorded as a refusal in advance**, because this is the class of change nobody reviews as a change | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D442 🐞 | **[[D420]]'s fix was not coherent as written, and the arm that decides whether an ordinary learner ever publishes has still not been run.** Two defects in the repair: *"the minimum across three response models"* is **undefined over intervals** — corrected to the **intersection** (a grid point enters the bracket only if it clears the null under *every* model, grid pinned at ±50). And the period structure carried **no arrival rate**, so *"12 games or 7 days"* was **unsimulable** — a period closes on whichever comes first, and without a rate neither arm is determined. AC-7 now requires **two rates**, one count-closing and one clock-closing, reporting periods-to-publish per rate. **The clock-closing arm is the one that matters** — it is the ordinary learner who plays slowly — **and it is unrun**. A falsifier that cannot be executed is the same species of defect as one that tests its own assumption | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D443 🐞 | **[[D424]] had a fourth site, and it was the most dangerous one — inside the document that recorded D424, one round later.** The gauge caveat survives at §2, §4.2 and §7.3 q2 as recorded. **§7.3 qualification 3 reported *estimable window ÷ journey = 1.09* with no caveat at all** — and a ratio above 1 reads as *"it fits"* to every reader, while the same arithmetic puts the journey's floor **6 points outside** the window. A ratio is a caveat's worst enemy: it compresses two numbers and their uncertainty into one figure that looks like a verdict. Fixed there; recorded because **the row warning that a caveat will not survive the next reader was itself the next reader** | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D434 🐞 | **An unpublished ruleset manufactures its own residue — the "12 unclassifiable entries" were 17, and the four cited as evidence were never residue at all.** `graduation-clearance` reported a 12-entry residue against a classifier that existed only in the author's head. Published as a literal seven-rule keyword list in §5.1a and re-run over all 220 blocking entries, the rule output is `unbuilt 29 / corpus 46 / citation 54 / engine 37 / tablebase 5 / shape 22 / authored 10 / residue 17`. **The reviewer's ruling survives** — 8 of the 17 are genuinely not `unreachable`, which was the point at issue — **but its supporting sample does not**: all four statements it cited (fifty-move counter, `variants` encoding, `follow_theory` friction, `PLAN_SIGNATURE_INLINED`) are matched by rule 1 and were never in the residue. So a correct ruling was reached on four wrong examples. Downstream: six of eight cells moved, `clearable` is **179 / 41** not 180/40, and the pack split **inverts** to **27 instrument-bound / 23 authoring-bound**. **The general form: a hand-classification whose rules are not written down cannot be audited, and its leftovers are an artefact of the classifier rather than a fact about the corpus.** Found 2026-08-16 by the `graduation-clearance` author round | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D435 🐞 | **The acceptance back door survives all three tightenings proposed for it, and one of them is unpayable.** Against [[D425]]: **T1** (`git log --diff-filter=A` on the cited file) **never fires** — `planning/exploration/log.md` was added 2026-08-09 in `8fb62692` and an author *appends* to it. **T2** (read the cited line and find the date there) **passes the attack**, because an appended entry carries its own `## 2026-08-16` heading — payable 40/40, and kept. **T3** (extend date containment to every clearance kind) is **unpayable**: all three `permanent_property` rulings are undated and `docs/tablebase-grounding.md` contains zero date strings, so it would redden criterion 6 on landing. T3 withdrawn and replaced by `GRADUATION_RULING_UNANCHORED`. **What actually closes it is a line-level `git blame` arm** on `GRADUATION_RULING_SELF_MINTED`, rejecting both the commit under review and the all-zero `Not Committed Yet` sentinel — verified payable (line 1231 blames to `ee34f1e5`, 2026-08-13). **The floor is stated rather than hidden:** this forces a ruling into a *prior standalone commit*, and **without a second party nothing closes further** — which is the honest boundary of a no-reviewer design | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D436 🐞 | **The community channel reaches production, and the real bound is an empty-evidence constructor rather than an absent one.** `PackStudio.hydrate` → `addCommunity` means registered packs **are** served. But `addCommunity` hard-codes `assessmentGrounding: "unverified"` with empty `positionEvidence`, `boundClaimIds` and `claimBackings`, and **cannot shadow an `official` id**. So the exposure is a **false blocker list, never false evidence** — a learner can be shown a pack whose graduation state is wrong, never one whose claims are wrongly backed. **This changes [[D417]]'s fix from a storage-sidecar problem to a surface question**, which is a much cheaper shape. Found 2026-08-16 by the `graduation-clearance` author round | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D430 🐞 | **`explorer_frequency` is a dead alternative inside a live map — a vocabulary that is declared, reachable on paper, and unreachable in fact.** `MACHINE_LABELS` maps `corpus_observed` to `["explorer_frequency", "explorer_position_census"]`, but `evaluate` resolves *every* `explorer.*@v1` assertion through `uniqueRecord(…, "explorer_position_census", …)` and returns that kind **unconditionally** — so an `explorer_frequency` record can never back a claim through a binding, whatever an author writes. Mirrored the same way in `check.ts` and `expression-census.ts`. **Harmless today at 0 records and actively misleading the moment the corpus wave runs**, because it offers an author a choice one arm of which silently fails. Exactly the class [[D428]]'s census exists to enumerate, and a demonstration that the map being *complete* says nothing about it being *reachable*. Found 2026-08-16 by the `feedback-delivery` author round  **Sharpened 2026-08-16: the map is not only triplicated, it is module-private.** So the cheapest path for the next consumer (`earnedEvidenceTypes`) is a **fourth copy carrying the same dead alternative**. The fix is to export it, not to copy it again — a private constant that three modules already need is a shared resource without a register, the same shape as [[D449]].| 🐞 partial — MACHINE_LABEL_EVIDENCE_KINDS is now one exported map consumed by binding validation, sourcing checks and the expression census; removing the unreachable explorer_frequency arm still needs the dead-vocabulary RFC |
| D431 🐞 | **31 claims reach delivery through a map entry that is never written.** `pack-registry.ts` writes a `claimBackings` entry only when a binding exists *or* `author_principle` is carried without a machine label — so a claim carrying `derived_feature` **alone** gets no entry at all. That is 31 claims and **11,256 chars, 32% of the day-zero admitted set**, delivered on the *absence* of a record rather than the presence of one. `feedback-delivery` fixes the consequence with an explicit C4 default (criterion 18), but **the registration asymmetry is the row**: a delivery rule that reads a map must be told what an unmapped claim means, or it inherits whatever the writer happened not to do. Found 2026-08-16 by the `feedback-delivery` author round | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D432 🐞 | **`RECORDED_READING_DISPOSITIONS` freezes a corpus FACT as a runtime disposition, and its own completeness assertion cannot catch the rot.** `explorer_position_census` is marked `refused` because *"no loadable pack producer emits this kind"* — true at the moment of writing, **false the moment the corpus wave runs**, and `assertRecordedReadingDispositions` checks that the table is *complete*, which it stays either way. A disposition is supposed to state a decision; this one states a measurement, and measurements expire. Same family as [[D368]] and the four stale graduation blockers in [[D406]]: a claim about the corpus, written once, never re-derived. Found 2026-08-16 by the `feedback-delivery` author round | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D433 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-21 by rfc-lifecycle-completion.** The completion checklist now requires clearing or re-homing every discharge row owned by the archiving slug and names lifted/re-homed edges in the commit body. | ✅ closed by lifecycle documentary landing |
| D429 🐞 | **A refusal emitter is not a consumer, and `FORMAT_DISPOSITIONS` cannot tell them apart — so every `refused` row self-reports as healthy the moment its warning ships.** `/retryVariants` gained a refusal emitter at HEAD. Counted naively, that emitter is a "consumer" of the very vocabulary it exists to refuse, so the census would read the field as live. The general hazard: **the act of documenting that something is dead makes it look alive to any instrument that counts references.** Fixed inside `dead-vocabulary` by splitting `refusalSites` into a fourth column, but the hazard belongs to `FORMAT_DISPOSITIONS` itself and will recur for every future `refused` disposition. Found 2026-08-16 by the `dead-vocabulary` cross-review | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D416 🐞 | **The completion protocol describes one agent and the repo runs two lanes.** Verified over the last 25 commits, and the split is perfectly clean: every `feat:`/`fix:`/`docs: archive` commit is **untrailered** (implementer lane) and every `ledger:`/`rfc:`/`research:` commit carries the session trailer (claude's lane). The protocol says the flip rides *"in the same commit"* — **a sentence only one agent can satisfy.** Three consequences, all of which fired: a closeout landing in the other lane is **untraceable from the RFC's own commits** (the exact search a reconciliation runs, which is why [[D400]] and [[D401]] both got it wrong); it can land **ahead of the code** (for 69 seconds the ledger asserted sixteen closures against a tree containing none of the implementation); and it lands **under an unrelated subject**, so the commit log has no record either. **The cheap fix, in the spirit of the log clause: require the closeout commit to NAME the rows it flips in its subject or body.** `3b16127` flipped 18 status characters and named 2 — one line would have made this a `git log --grep`. **Fired again in the next commit after this row was written, which is the strongest possible argument for it.** D370, D371 and D382 — `opponent-contracts`' three rows — were flipped in `3c7d278`, a **claude** commit whose subject is *"rulings: teacher mode ships complete, band is provenance, leaderboards admitted, bosses are games"*, naming none of them and nothing about opponent contracts. The implementing commit `6ba0736` changed **zero** ledger characters. **Fourth instance of claude absorbing codex's flips**, and the first *after* the rule existed. Note also that codex's own report named the wrong absorbing commit (`1037004`, which touched only `log.md`) — **when the flip lands under an unrelated subject, even the party whose work was absorbed cannot find it**, which is the defect stated more sharply than this row originally managed | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D417 🐞 | **The rate refusal is decimal-only, so an integer percentage routes as authored judgement.** D166 says a rate is refused inside an author-attributed segment because it *"normalises under the percentage or `displayCp` entries of the frozen table"*. The shipped guard is `RATE_TOKEN = /(?:[+-]?\d+\.\d+%?)/` (`claim-binding.ts:167`) — **decimal-only**. Probed against the shipped validator: *"f5 scores 90.9% for White"* → `CLAIM_READING_UNATTRIBUTED`; ***"f5 scores 91% for White"* → nothing**; *"ninety-one percent"* → nothing. An integer percentage is the ordinary way to write a rate, and an author-declared span strips it from the `MACHINE_TOKEN` residue too — **so the one refusal the routing must not lift is lifted by dropping a decimal point** — **and the second net cannot catch it either** (confirmed 2026-08-16 by the `feedback-delivery` round): `CLAIM_ASSERTION_UNDECLARED` sweeps the *remainder*, and declared `authored:true` spans are removed from the remainder first. Live in the corpus **today** — `anti-scandinavian-white/just-take-it` carries **74%** alongside `best` and `advantage`. Also escaping: *"ninety-one percent"* and *"44,467,486"*. **`feedback-delivery` §3.6a leans on this guard and its criterion 19 ASSERTS the hole rather than assuming its absence**, which is the right handling for a dependency you cannot fix in your own scope | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D418 ✅ | **`pack-graduation` shipped with ZERO ledger flips, and nothing has audited it.** `db243f5` and `b6b5d9a` changed **no status characters**: D203–D212, D237–D246 still read 🐞/💡, and D138, D141, D162 likewise. [[D400]] recorded it as flipping 13 rows correctly — that was wrong, and it means **the D400 cluster's real remainder is pack-graduation's, not claim-backing's**. Fifteen rows in an unverified state, and the RFC is archived | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D419 ✅ | **The disposition column is not a status and three readers in two days have taken it for one.** `design/BACKLOG.md`'s defect table is `\| <id> <status> \| <description> \| <disposition note> \|`; column 3 holds pre-implementation provenance that **is not updated when the status flips**, so a ✅ row can carry *"🔨 fixed in …"* or even *"💡 open"*. That misread produced [[D400]] and [[D401]], both of which entered the ledger as false records. **D97 and D112 read ✅ and "open" in the same row.** Either the column updates on flip, or it carries a marker saying it is historical | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D410 🐞 | **Imported broadcast feeds carry third-party move grades, and nothing asserts we strip them.** Lichess broadcast PGN ships inline `{ [%eval 81.15] } { Blunder. Rf8 was best. }` — **another product's verdict on a move, entering our corpus as authored-looking text.** `comments=false` strips it (measured: 43,593 → 11,870 bytes, 8 → 0 `%eval`) and `import-source.ts:73` already sets `evals=false&literate=false` — **but that is reliance on an upstream default we do not control, with no test.** Propose an assertion, not a hope. Found 2026-08-16 by `design/research/live-relay-as-drill-source.md` | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D411 💡 | **Assistance must be lockable on "the source game is still live".** A learner drilling a position from an in-progress broadcast could otherwise read our evidence rungs while the real game is still being played — which is why organisers ship delays of up to 3600 s and Lichess a 3-move stream delay. **This would be the first consumer of `permittedAssistance`'s declared-and-unread `sessionKind`**, strengthening the existing `DESIGN-GAP:` against `design/05:147` rather than adding a new mechanism | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D412 💡 | **Disambiguate the parked events row: *team relays* ≠ external tournament relay.** Two independent agents have now derived from scratch that the events row's *"team relays"* means a roster-with-calendar, and that live broadcast import is a different object entirely. One clause in that row saves the next derivation | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D413 💡 | **`docs/game-import-and-story.md`'s chess.com refusal is better-founded than its own sentence claims.** It says chess.com URLs are refused *"because no supported per-game public fetch contract exists"* — now verified as **the general case**, not a gap in our research: nothing live or in-progress is publicly exposed, four probes 404, and their terms prohibit the use anyway. The doc should say so, since the current wording reads like a limitation we might one day overcome | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D414 💡 | **No broadcast has been round-tripped through `pgn-import.ts`.** Parse-compatibility with real broadcast PGN rests on **shape inspection, not execution** — the one unverified link in an otherwise all-`[V]` dossier, and the obvious next hands-on step (~20 lines under `tools/`) | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D404 🐞 | **`clearedBy` is populated in exactly zero of 436 graduation entries, so the only clearance lint fires zero times.** `pack-graduation` shipped the slot as optional free text and `GRADUATION_CLEAREDBY_UNRESOLVED` lints it at warning — but its guard is `typeof row.clearedBy === "string"`, and **0 of 293 draft entries and 0 of 143 candidate entries carry the key** (`rfc/graduation-clearance.md`). **Not a descope:** that RFC's §0.3(a) scoped out *paying* the debt, and specifying what payment looks like is not paying it | 🔨 owned by `graduation-clearance`, found 2026-08-16 |
| D405 🐞 | **The rubber stamp already exists, and it is `resolved`, not `accepted`.** All **30** `resolved` entries carry one **byte-identical** `resolved.by`: *"The recorded work was completed; the original statement remains as history."* `accepted` was hardened against assertion with a required `rulingRef`; **`resolved` never was**, so it is an author's sentence about their own pack that no checker re-evaluates. The fix that follows: **`resolved` must be a standing predicate a checker re-runs**, with a stale resolution demoted back to blocking | 🔨 owned by `graduation-clearance`, found 2026-08-16 |
| D406 🐞 | **Four blockers are stale and nothing detects staleness.** `mate-k-q-technique`, `mate-k-r-technique`, `mate-two-bishops` and `philidor-passive-rook-convert` each declare *"not ledger-verified; no sourcing sidecars were produced"* **while holding 25–27 `tablebase_result` records and passing `sourcing-check` at strict**. Counterweight measured in the same pass: all 16 *"no engine pass"* entries are **true** (0 `engine_eval` each), so this is not a general rot — it is the absence of a re-check. Same family as [[D368]]: a claim about the corpus, written once, never re-derived | 🔨 owned by `graduation-clearance`, found 2026-08-16 |
| D407 🐞 | **`unbuilt` is being filed as work, and it is not work.** Five fixtures record a **permanent refusal** as a blocking entry someone could theoretically clear — while `out_of_scope` has **zero instances**. The taxonomy needs `unbuilt` (names the RFC that owns it; **can never be `accepted`**) separated from `unreachable` (no predicate can ever clear it; the only kind eligible for acceptance). Conflating them means a pack waits forever on a row nobody can act on | 🔨 owned by `graduation-clearance`, found 2026-08-16 |

This is `planning/content-era/plan.md` §3b's existing structure written down, and it reinstates **no reviewer, front door or back** — the sign-off struck 2026-08-13 stays struck | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |

The mode is not the problem | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 **EVIDENCE ARRIVED 2026-08-16 (D333/D381): the band transfers ~0.07 in reduced endgames against 0.40 at full material, so the twelve packs' declared 1150–1900 span is unsupportable at endgame material rather than merely unmeasured.** The owner call narrows accordingly: not *"is the dial real"* (it is, at ~0.29 overall) but *"should an endgame pack declare a band at all."* |

| D371 ✅ | **`perfect_tablebase` degenerated to alphabetical order in drawn roots.** The deterministic UCI tie enriched irreversible simplifying moves 2.6× in the exact mode used to hold | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-16 by `rfc/opponent-contracts.md`** — every residual tie now uses position-pure `sha256(fen\0uci)` order; drawn roots record `orderingBasis: "none"`, won/lost DTZ ordering is unchanged. **Post-fix census on the original 507 positions, and the second number is the one to keep:** drawn roots **3/66 = 4.55%** against a 4.02% uniform expectation — the 2.6× enrichment is gone. **Won roots moved too, from 1.57× to 1.18× (24/218 = 11.01%)**, which nobody predicted, because the fix was specified for drawn roots only — 130 won roots had **tied optimal DTZ**, so the residual tiebreak carries far more traffic on the winning side than the defect report implied. The check was **non-vacuous**, and the won-side movement is recorded rather than smoothed over: it means `localeCompare`'s simplification bias was never confined to the case that was measured |
| D367 ✅ | **A content wave has no completion protocol, so it ships its fixes and loses its rows.** Cluster F ran on 2026-08-15 (`41afe00`) and `planning/work-register.md` still called it *"queued, never launched"* a day later, because its five ledger rows never flipped. The RFC lifecycle has a completion protocol — *the ledger flip rides in the implementing commit, the log entry rides in the archiving commit* — and **content waves have neither half**. They are the only tier of work in this repo that can complete invisibly. Found 2026-08-16 by re-running a wave that had already run. The fix is a stated content-wave closeout, not more diligence | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-16** — `AGENTS.md` now states a content-wave closeout beside the RFC one: the ledger flip and the `planning/content-era/log.md` entry ride in the commit that ships the content. Added by claude on measured evidence and the owner's to veto, in the same form as the 2026-08-15 log clause |
| D368 🐞 | **Measured claims are prose, so staleness has no detector.** Nine shape entries and two packs carry claims of the form *"N of 694"* — a numerator, a denominator, and **nothing recording what corpus they were measured against or when**. The corpus grew 43→56 packs and 694→827 positions and **four became materially false**: three entries declare themselves orphans *"0 of 694"* while all three are referenced and firing (8, 7 and 10 of 827), and `fianchetto-g7` claims trigger 44 / mirror 0 / g7–d4-clear 0 where the instrument now reads **95, 10 and 5**. If a measured claim carried `{measuredAt, packs, positions}` this entire class would be a lint rule instead of a re-measurement pass. **Same subject as [[D103]], and it argues D103's `triggerNote` should be a measurement RECORD rather than free text** — both blocked on the same `additionalProperties: false` | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |

| D366 ✅ | **Our most-used endgame opponent is the one instrument never measured in endgames.** Owner 2026-08-16: *"isn't that THE place where humans play so different from agent?"* Measured on the corpus: **12 of 14 endgame packs declare `human_common`** (Maia) and 2 declare `perfect_tablebase`. **Every instrument we own is mismatched exactly here.** (a) `perfect_tablebase` orders winning moves by **shortest DTZ** — distance-to-*zeroing* — so it provably beelines for the next capture or pawn move; "simplifies happily" is a property of the shipped code, and no human plays that way. (b) The explorer has **no endgame coverage at all** — R9 measured usable data ending at ply ~20 and zero by ply 27, while endgames begin far later. (c) **Maia is a policy network with no search**, which is precisely the regime where search matters most and a policy head is weakest — and it is our default. **The product question the owner's framing exposes:** a human-like endgame opponent is simultaneously the **most valuable** thing we could have (it is where technique gaps actually decide games) and the **least verified** thing we ship. The measurable form is not "is Maia strong" but *"is Maia wrong the way a human at that band is wrong"* — plausible technique failures — **or wrong arbitrarily**, which is noise wearing a human's name. The tablebase gives exact ground truth for result-preservation at ≤7 pieces, so this is answerable | ✅ **ANSWERED 2026-08-16, `design/research/maia-endgame-fidelity.md`** — 1,095 probes, 0 errors, 507 tablebase-probed positions from 11 of the 14 endgame packs, at the **0.7/0.9** temperature those packs actually declare rather than the code default. **Human-shaped, decisively:** the 84 errors live on **6 of 45 positions and 5 distinct moves**, modal error share **0.95–1.00** against **0.58–0.64** if the erring move were uniform over the same dropping moves. **All 84 are `win→draw`; zero `win→loss`, zero `draw→loss`** — where a uniform-random legal move concedes the full point on 10.7% of winning and 27.6% of drawn roots. `argmax(policy)` preserves at the identical rate, so the errors are **the model's belief, not the sampler's tail** — lowering temperature would not remove them. **Band-calibrated not at all:** 88.1 / 88.9 / 91.9% at 1100 / 1500 / 1900, **43 of 45 positions tied** between the extremes (sign p=0.5), with band application verified per probe (810/810 sent `Elo` after the `SelfElo`/`OppoElo` defaults; 45/45 positions produced three distinct policy vectors, zero collisions). **So the mode is sound and the `targetElo` on those twelve packs is the unsupported claim** |

**Live collision, 2026-08-16: `D203` is used twice.** The `pack-graduation` cross-review was
issued the block **D203–D212** and may use no other id; while it was measuring, the selection-
integrity commits (`fc99ba1`/`5835dfc`) took **D203** as *"the next free id above the highest
issued block"* — which it was not, because this block had been issued in a brief but not yet
registered on this line. Both rows are below. **The convention has a hole and it is this one:
"the highest issued block" is only knowable from a file that the issuing brief does not write
to.** Resolution is the owner's: renumber the selection-integrity row to **D233** (the first id
above the highest block now registered), or renumber this cross-review's first row. Fix for the
hole: a block is registered **on this line the moment it is issued**, not when its rows land.

| # | Defect | Status |
|---|---|---|
| D148 🐞 | **The D126 ruling does not reach a single `cost: unmeasurable`, and the reason is the schema, not the corpus.** `$defs/deviationCost` admits exactly three shapes — `cp` (basis `engine` or `material`), `mate` (basis `engine` or `tablebase`) and `unmeasurable` — so there is no corpus-basis cost, and a result split cannot be written into the field at all. The semantic block is stronger than the structural one: *cost* is a quality claim by definition, so converting a split into one is precisely the conversion the ruling refuses. Measured on the corpus rather than argued: the four deviations carrying `unmeasurable` are played **30 of 10987** (`kid-mar-del-plata-white` c4-c5), **2 of 158** (`iqp-white-panov-attack` d4-d5), **0 of 742** (`nimzo-doubled-c-pawns` c4-c5) and **0 of 730** (`grunfeld-exchange-fianchetto` …Bxd4) at ratings 1400/1600/1800, rapid+classical, 2023-01..2025-12, retrieved 2026-08-15 — all four below the 100-game floor at which `explorer.ts:91` abstains. **A move worth authoring as a deviation is a move the band does not play, which is the same property that denies it a split.** Two corrections fall out: D126's own summary says the ruling unblocks *"five packs carrying `cost: unmeasurable`"* and the middlegame-wave correction says all five `concept_violation`s carry `unmeasurable` — there are **four**; `maroczy-bind-white-squeeze`'s c4-c5 carries `{cp, 194, engine}` from that wave's single engine pass. The four reasons now state the measured count and the abstention instead of *"no corpus evidence bears on this"* | 💡 open (core), **headline stale** (D400). `$defs/deviationCost` now admits **four** shapes, not three — the fourth (`category`/`basis: "tablebase"`) added by `18d2832 feat: implement engine leverage`, **by none of the three RFCs this row was attributed to**. Semantic core untouched: still no corpus basis, and a result split still cannot be written into `cost` |
| D149 ✅ | **`explorerUrl` hard-coded `moves=12`, so an authored deviation outside the twelve most-played could be bounded but never counted.** `ExplorerQuery.moves` now accepts a positive safe integer and canonicalization defaults it to 12; the URL, priority artifact, and job digest all record the applied depth. Tests pin both the unchanged default and a 40-move request. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the authoring-instrument batch |
| D150 ✅ | **No evidence record kind at HEAD can carry a result split, so every split this wave authored is unbound prose.** Three gates close in series: `EVIDENCE_KINDS` (`sourcing/types.ts`) has no census kind; `explorer_frequency`'s values are validated **key-exact** against the eight move-share fields (`sourcing/check.ts:128-132`, `exactKeys`), so adding `white`/`draws`/`black` fails `EVIDENCE_VALUES_INVALID`; and the supported text must be **byte-equal** to `renderExplorerFrequency` (`check.ts:144`), which renders a share and no outcomes. `check.ts:202` then maps `corpus_observed` to `explorer_frequency` alone, so a split sentence labelled `corpus_observed` is `EVIDENCE_TYPE_UNBACKED` the moment its pack acquires a ledger. The eleven packs edited this wave have **no `.evidence.json` at all**, which is the only reason the label passes today — the same hole D124 names from the band side. `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md`'s `explorer_position_census` is the named fix and is not landed, so this row is the standing statement of what the ruling can and cannot be bound to | ✅ **closed 2026-08-16 by `claim-backing` (`5a63225`)** — found by the D400 pass, which caught this row asserting the fix was *"not landed"* when all three gates it names had already fallen. `explorer_position_census` is in `EVIDENCE_KINDS`; `check.ts:288-293` validates the census shape; `check.ts:199` maps `corpus_observed` to it; `explorer.ts:268` emits it. **Residue is content, not contract** — zero census records exist, which is [[D128]]/[[D141]] |
| D151 🐞 | **The 100-game abstention floor sits exactly where timing windows live, and two of the four shipped windows are entirely beneath it.** A window is declared several plies into a spine, and band data thins with depth. Measured at the wave's population: `maroczy-bind-white-squeeze` completes readiness after b3 at **80 games** (one ply earlier, after Rac1, 101 — the floor is crossed *inside* the window); `iqp-white-panov-attack` completes readiness at **29** and its arrival close fires at **12**; only `kid-mar-del-plata-white` (639 at readiness, 644 at arrival) and `dragon-yugoslav-race` (239 at readiness, 439 at arrival) stay above it. So the tempo layer's authored budgets and deadlines are unreachable by corpus evidence in half the cases before the conditional-instrument problem (D155) is even reached. Worth recording as a **content-planning** fact, not only a defect: window depth and evidence availability trade off against each other, and nothing warns an author | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the explorer-grounding content wave |
| D152 ✅ | **`make expression-census` was blind to corpus grounding.** Its canonical report now includes a per-pack evidence census: label-to-rung mapping, exact claim counts, pointer-matched ledger backing, and machine-readable explorer populations where records provide them. Missing sidecars remain visible as zero backing and an empty population rather than being inferred from prose. A synthetic backed corpus record pins the positive path. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the authoring-instrument batch |
| D153 🐞 | **[[D123]]'s 400-character `timingWindows[].note` cap now blocks the D126 ruling specifically, measured.** A split is only evidence with its population attached — game count, rating band, speeds and window — which is ~120 characters in this repo's own phrasing. The four shipped window notes are **337, 394, 372 and 359** of 400 characters, so the headroom is 63, 6, 28 and 41: **no window can state the population of a split in the field that exists to justify its thresholds.** The wave routed the sentences into `feedbackClaims` and `provenance.sources` instead, where the runtime shows the first and never shows the second. This is D123 with a number on it and a second independent cause, which is the argument for lifting the cap rather than trimming prose | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the explorer-grounding content wave |
| D154 🐞 | **The D126 row's illustrative split is a manufactured chess number sitting in the ledger next to a real game count.** The row reads *"At this position 1400–1800 players scored 47/31/22 over 5,069 games"*. **5,069 is real** — it is `maroczy-bind-white-squeeze`'s start position at the middlegame wave's band, re-queried and reproduced exactly this wave. **47/31/22 is not**: the measured split there is **2629/371/2069 = 51.9/7.3/40.8%**, and a 31% draw rate does not occur anywhere in the eleven positions this wave censused (range 4.7–9.8%). The illustration is doing its job and no one has been misled, but a plausible-looking split paired with a real count is exactly the shape [[D110]] describes one tier up — *the number was typed and no instrument produced it* — and this ledger is the document every agent reads first. Remedy is one edit by the owner or claude-on-a-ruling: either mark the numbers illustrative or replace them with the measured Maroczy split | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the explorer-grounding content wave |
| D155 💡 | **The instrument the tempo layer needs is a game-level corpus, and calling it "the explorer" is why four waves have re-derived that it does not exist.** Every timing-window graduation blocker in the corpus asks for the same thing — *"a corpus measurement relating the arrangement's completion to results at this band"* — and the explorer cannot answer it for a structural reason, not a coverage one: it aggregates **per position**, so no query can condition an outcome on whether an event happened earlier or later in the same game. D126 does not touch this; admitting result splits changes what may be *said* about a position, not what may be *asked* of the index. The distinct capability is per-game traversal (replay games at the band, test a predicate over the move sequence, partition outcomes) — the Go corpus pipeline `design/04` §4 already names for endgame mining, pointed at middlegame timing. Proposal: name it as its own instrument class wherever the content map lives, so *"the explorer cannot do this"* stops being rediscovered and starts being a queued capability | 💡 open, raised 2026-08-15 by the explorer-grounding content wave |
| D156 💡 | **Two disposable authoring instruments landed under the exploration gate and one should be promoted; this is [[D121]]'s class exactly.** `apps/server/src/split-probe.ts` prints a position's W/D/B split and every returned move's split at the wave's stated population, live and uncached, with the 100-game floor flagged and a `MOVES=` override for [[D149]]; `apps/server/src/fen-walk.ts` resolves a pack's start, every spine node and every deviation's before/after position to FENs so a probe can be aimed at them. Both are headed DISPOSABLE and neither is imported by production code. The promotion case is measured: **no shipped command answers "what did the band score here"** — `make candidate-attach` writes a move-share sentence and nothing else — so the next content wave rebuilds this, which is the D121 pattern (three authors, three identical evaluators, one missing print statement). `fen-walk` has a second use the wave did not expect: it is the only way to get the FEN of an authored deviation's *result*, which every deviation-grounding question needs | 💡 open, raised 2026-08-15 by the explorer-grounding content wave |
| D157 🐞 | **`carlsbad-minority-attack` — the pack `design/04` §8 names as the middlegame exemplar — carried no corpus evidence of any kind until this wave, and nothing flags it.** Its four `provenance.sources` entries are an opening-name citation, a chessops verification, an UNGROUNDED disclaimer and an authoring note; there is no rating band, no game count, and no explorer query anywhere in the file. Every one of the ten packs the middlegame wave authored states its band in prose; the pack they were written to match does not. It now carries the position census (815 games, 390/60/365) as its first corpus fact. The general defect is that **a pack quoting no population is indistinguishable from a pack quoting the wrong one** — D124 measures the second and nothing measures the first. A single required field, or a check that warns when a pack has no band-bearing source, closes both | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the explorer-grounding content wave |
| D110 🐞 | **No position in the corpus has a complete legal-move tablebase census, and thirteen claims assert one.** Enumerating the legal successors of the root and of every authored spine node across the 12 `tablebase_exact` packs and matching them against the packs' `tablebase_result` FENs: **0 of 241 positions is fully censused** — root coverage runs from **1 of 28** (`mate-two-bishops`) to **5 of 11** (`pawn-breakthrough-convert`), and `philidor-passive-rook-convert` covers **4 of 21** while its `one-move-wins` claim reads *"All twenty-one legal moves were enumerated and queried: thirteen draw … and seven lose."* `mate-bishop-knight`'s and `trajectory-mate-bishop-knight`'s `stalemate-is-the-default` assert *"nine win, nine draw, and eight of those nine draws are stalemate"* against a 2-of-23 root. Either the queries ran and were never recorded, or the counts came from elsewhere — this is *the author ran the query, typed the number, and the ledger never learned about it* generalised from one worked example to a measured population. **The tablebase evidence that IS recorded is complete and live** (root FEN in 12 of 12 packs, 229 of 229 authored spine positions, 0 of 341 manufactured at HEAD); it is the *enumerations* that are missing. Remedy is a query wave, not a contract change **Independently verified 2026-08-15 by claude, and the core holds:** `philidor-passive-rook-convert`'s ledger carries 25 `tablebase_result` records at **25 distinct anchor FENs** — a walk *down the spine*, one record per position — and **exactly one** of them anchors at the root the claim enumerates. So *"thirteen draw, seven lose"* is a precise cardinal breakdown no instrument in this repo produced. The claim may well be TRUE; truth is not the standard, traceability is, and this is law 8 failing from the **authoring** side rather than the rendering side — the product manufacturing chess truth in its own content. **Two different scopes are in play and both are recorded rather than one being chosen:** a phrase-level sweep for asserted enumerations finds **4 assertions across 3 files** (`philidor-passive-rook-convert` ×2, `mate-bishop-knight`, `trajectory-mate-bishop-knight`); the drafting agent's claim-level detector finds **13**. They measure different things. A first sweep by claude reported *23 packs* — that count was **wrong and is withdrawn**: 22 of the 23 hits were the **opposite**, the honest disclaimer *"all legal moves were not enumerated"* that the engine-pass note carries by design, caught by a regex that missed the negation. **ROW TITLE OVERSTATED — corrected 2026-08-16 by `claim-backing`'s cross-review, which re-derived both detectors and found BOTH RIGHT about different things.** The lexical sweep's **4** is the count of explicit *"all N legal moves were enumerated"* assertions. The draft's **13** is neither that nor a census count — its own list mixes genuine censuses with off-tree positions and one subset quantification. **The actual law-8 escalation is 7 full-set census claims.** And the headline *"no position has a complete census"* is false as stated: **36 positions are fully censused** — all of them single-legal-move links, where the census is trivial. The true and **stronger** figure is **0 of 199 choice-bearing positions**, which is the population the claim actually concerns. Escalation stands at 7; the number was wrong in the alarming direction and is corrected here rather than quietly. | 💡 open — **RE-DERIVED AT HEAD 2026-08-16 (D400) and it reproduces exactly: 0 of 277 choice-bearing tablebase-anchored positions is fully censused, and the 36 that are censused are all single-legal-move links** `[V]`. The four quoted claim texts are byte-identical. **`claim-backing` never scoped this row** — its criterion 10 lists D110 as *"opened by the drafting commit"* — but it landed the instrument the remedy needs: `CLAIM_ASSERTION_KINDS` now carries `tablebase.moveCensus@v1`, so a census claim is bindable for the first time and no pack binds one |
| D111 🐞 | **Five claims assert what no instrument in this repository measures**, and they are the permanent residue of D97 once the debt is made payable. `mate-bishop-knight/opponent-plays-the-spine` and `trajectory-mate-bishop-knight/spine-is-the-opponent` cite *"a reimplementation of the shipped rule [that] reproduced all nineteen of them"* — an instrument that left no artifact; `trajectory-mate-bishop-knight/wrong-corner-is-forced` cites *"took twenty plies"* against *"the most resistant defence available at every turn"*, a search nothing recorded, and carries no self-declared label to fall back on; `philidor-passive-rook-convert/sibling-drill` cites a record in **another pack's** ledger; `philidor-passive-rook-convert/why-the-skewer-works` carries a `tablebase_exact` label for a legality/geometry fact no tablebase record states. **Each remedy is authoring** — add a self-declared label and mark the span authored, copy the record, or re-label — **and none is tooling.** That the residue is 5 of 131 rather than 0 is the law-8 check working; a binding mechanism admitting all 131 would have stopped checking | 💡 open — **re-checked at HEAD 2026-08-16 (D400): four of five unchanged.** `philidor-passive-rook-convert/sibling-drill` gained `principles` in `5a63225` but already carried `author_principle`, so the label half was never the missing part — the cross-pack record is still uncopied. `why-the-skewer-works` still carries `tablebase_exact` for a geometry fact. Never scoped |
| D112 ✅ | **`$defs/feedbackClaim` is `additionalProperties: true`** — uniquely among the shapes the claim work touched (`$defs/spineNode`, `$defs/deviation` and `$defs/reasoningKeyPoint` are all `false`; only `$defs/provenance` shares the openness, which is D25's hazard one object over). So any future field on a claim — an anchor, a binding, a trust marker — **validates silently and unchecked before it is specified**, and absence can never be proved from the schema, only counted from the corpus. Noted inline by `rfc/feedback-delivery.md` §2.1 as the reason its key-set measurement rather than the schema is what its C1 rests on; ledgered here so it is destinated rather than living in one RFC's parenthesis. `claim-backing` routes around it entirely by putting the binding in the evidence ledger, which is why it stays open | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 |
| D121 ✅ | **`make shape-check PROBE=<fen>` computed the probe and printed nothing.** The CLI now prints `PROBE FIRES` or `PROBE DOES NOT FIRE` with the checked file and `/trigger` pointer for every probe; unit coverage pins both outputs and a real CLI invocation observes the result. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the authoring-instrument batch |
| D122 ✅ | **A unit test pins two content facts, and authoring correct content turns `make verify` red.** `apps/server/src/expression-census.test.ts:28-41` asserts that `knight-vs-bishop/black-anchor-the-knight` fires **0** times in the corpus and that all five `opposite-castling-race` subjects carry `IN_SHAPE_DENOMINATOR_EMPTY`.
