Nothing is wrong with the content or the instrument — the test encodes a census snapshot. Same class as **D47** (pin tests encode content facts, queued on `rfc/fixture-realism.md`); recorded separately because this instance blocks the required gate rather than merely embedding a fact. The wave deliberately did **not** edit the test: a content wave rewriting a server test to match its own output is the failure mode the pin was meant to catch | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-15 by `aee7c64`** — `expression-census.test.ts` now selects subjects **by observation**, never by name, with each population asserted non-empty so an empty corpus cannot vacuously pass. Row stood open for a day because claude fixed it inside a content commit and never flipped it — the same defect-vs-doctrine mismatch that left D59 open, found 2026-08-15 by the middlegame content wave |
| D123 🐞 | **`timingWindows[].note` is capped at 400 characters — the one field that must justify an authored threshold is the most constrained in the format.** Spine annotations, deviation notes and provenance strings are unbounded; the window note, which is where a luxury budget of 1 and a deadline of 4 have to be explained, truncates. All four window-declaring packs of the 2026-08-15 wave hit or worked around the cap (three authors reported it independently, ~4 minutes each), pushing the rationale into `provenance.sources` where the runtime cannot show it. Either lift the cap or give a window a `rationale` sibling that is not learner-facing | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the middlegame content wave |
| D124 🐞 | **The corpus quotes two different explorer populations and no pack states its band in a machine-readable field.** The opening wave and `content/candidates/priority/priority.json` used ratings 1400/1600/1800, **blitz+rapid, 2024-01..2026-07**; the 2026-08-15 middlegame wave used ratings 1400/1600/1800, **rapid+classical, 2023-01..2025-12**. Both are honest and both are quoted in prose inside `provenance.sources`, so nothing can compare them, detect the mismatch, or re-derive a percentage — the checker's byte-equality rule for `explorer-move-share/v1` applies only to attached candidates, not to hand-authored packs. A corpus-evidence block (population, speeds, window, retrieval date) beside the claim would make band a fact rather than a sentence | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the middlegame content wave |
| D125 💡 | **A named structure family can have no band-attested tabiya, and the content map has nowhere to say so.** `design/04` §3 lists **hanging pawns** as one of ten middlegame families. Measured 2026-08-15 on the standard QGD Tartakower route at ratings 1400/1600/1800 (rapid+classical, 2023-01..2025-12): the pre-break node has **39 games** and the first position where the shape entry's trigger can fire has **14** — below the 100-game floor the explorer client itself uses to abstain. So the wave shipped nine of ten families and skipped this one on evidence rather than on effort. The gap is invisible in `04`, in the ledger, and in `content/shapes/hanging-pawns.json`, which reads as an ordinary unreferenced entry. Proposal: record attestation per family (position, band, count, date) wherever the content map lives, so "not authored" and "not reachable at this band" stop looking the same | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the middlegame content wave |
| D126 ✅ | **Owner-gated: are explorer W/D/B result splits admissible as `corpus_observed` evidence?** The explorer returns a white/draw/black split per move, and the wave's instrument printed it on every call. **All three authoring agents independently refused to use it**, reading a win-rate at a rating band as a graded move assessment under law 8, and the exemplar refused it too. The cost of that refusal is measurable: roughly half the wave's "could not ground" list — *is this recapture better*, *does ...h5 stop the storm*, *is the symmetric isolani balanced* — becomes partially groundable at **zero additional API cost** if the answer is yes, with a stated population and no engine. The argument against is that outcome share at a mixed-Elo band is confounded by everything except the move. This needs a ruling, not an authoring convention, because the corpus is already full of packs that quote frequencies and refuse results | ✅ **OWNER RULED 2026-08-15: ADMISSIBLE as `corpus_observed` (rung 4).** The boundary is now written so authors stop self-refusing: **the split may be stated; it may never be converted into a move verdict or a quality claim.** *"Over 5,069 games at this position the outcomes were 2,629 White wins, 371 draws and 2,069 Black wins (51.9/7.3/40.8%)"* is what happened — rung 4 is defined as *"says what happened, not what is good"*, and the ladder's named failure is reading **popularity as quality**, which a result split is not. *"…so Black is better here"* stays refused. Unblocks roughly half the ungroundable list at zero extra instrument cost, including five packs carrying `cost: unmeasurable` and four authored tempo budgets. Owned by `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md` (the `explorer_position_census` record kind) , raised 2026-08-15 by the middlegame content wave |
| D127 🐞 | **The shape library can author a plan success signature the pack layer refuses to grade on.** `queenless-middlegame/white-king-into-the-game`'s signature is built from placement/quantified nodes, and a pack that copies it verbatim into a `structural_feature` success condition is rejected with `STRUCTURAL_CONDITION_HAS_NO_FEATURE` ("derives no rules evidence reference"). The two layers therefore disagree about what is expressible: `shape-check` accepts the entry, `pack-check` refuses the pack that uses it. `berlin-queenless-press` works around it with an implied `piece_distance` conjunct and discloses the substitution in its objective summary and provenance — i.e. the authored content now differs from the library text it claims to grade. Either the shape validator should refuse un-gradeable signatures at authoring time, or the objective compiler should accept them where a checkpoint already may | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 by the middlegame content wave |
| D128 🐞 | **Fifteen packs carry claims and no evidence ledger at all — a debt kind `claim-backing` §4 has no bucket for.** At `d2f34f9` this was 5 packs / 1 machine-checkable label; `aee7c64`'s ten middlegame packs took it to **15 packs / 55 claims / 15 machine-labelled / 5,353 chars** at `67f6ee0` [V]. §4's Bucket 2 is defined as *"the instrument exists and is wired, but the query was never recorded"* — for these there is no sidecar to record into, so the debt is a sourcing run **plus** a ledger **plus** a binding. `feedback-delivery`'s C7 fails them closed correctly; what is wrong is counting them inside a wave that does not pay them | 💡 open — **WORSE THAN WRITTEN, re-derived at HEAD (D400).** Same fifteen packs; the debt inside them is not: **71 claims and 31 machine-labelled**, not 55/15 — and 31 is exactly the masked figure [[D207]] reports from the other side. **Zero backed.** Opened by `claim-backing`, never scoped |
| D129 ✅ | **Ordinals are numerals the residual sweep cannot see, and this corpus writes ranks and files as ordinals.** `claim-backing` §3.4's frozen table was *"an English cardinal 0–999"*; **24 of the 75 machine-labelled claims at HEAD carry an ordinal** [V] — *"every rook slide along the **sixth** rank"* (`tempo-is-the-lesson`), *"rook to the **fourth**"* (`bridge-not-squeeze`), *"the **third** rank"*, *"queen-versus-**seventh**-pawn"*. Omitting them did not make them safe, it made them undeclarable and unswept. Fixed in the draft by extending the table to ordinals 1st–99th; the residue (fractions, *"twice"*, *"a third"*) is 4 occurrences and stays `authored` | 🔨 fixed in `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md` §3.4 by the cross-review 2026-08-15 |
| D130 ✅ | **An assertion's `fen` argument need not name a position the pack reaches.** In `claim-backing` §3.3's registry v1, only `tablebase.lineUniformCategory@v1` checked its FENs against the authored spine; the other eight entries accepted any FEN holding a record in that ledger. `philidor-third-rank-hold`'s `philidor-is-drawn` opens *"This **exact position** is a tablebase draw"* and its ledger holds 23 `tablebase_result` records, so the span binds to a different position and validates. This also falsified Open question 3's premise, which borrows `feedback-delivery`'s *"author-supplied and machine-checked against the pack's own spine"* verbatim. Fixed by a standing precondition + `CLAIM_FEN_OFF_PACK` | 🔨 fixed in `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md` §3.3 by the cross-review 2026-08-15 |
| D131 🐞 | **The residual sweep polices numerals; the owner's D126 boundary polices inference from numerals — and the second is not mechanically checkable.** D126 admits explorer W/D/B splits *"the split may be stated; it may never be converted into a move verdict or a quality claim"* and refuses *"…so Black is better here"* by name. That clause carries **no cardinal, no SAN token, no result word**: appended to a claim binding one percentage it survives the sweep with an empty residual and ships `ledger_bound`. Four families sit in the gap — qualitative, causal, comparative, scope — and three corpus claims already contain no digit and no spelled cardinal at all [V]. **Not fixable by any check short of reading English.** Recorded, tested as a ceiling (criterion 4), and answered only by §3.6's paired rendering | 💡 open — **the ceiling stands (D400).** `claim-binding.ts:203` still tests a token regex; nothing reaches inference. **The routing this row named as the only possible answer DID land** — `CLAIM_AUTHOR_LABEL_REQUIRED`, `authorSpans`, `CLAIM_READING_UNATTRIBUTED` — but it fires only inside a claim binding, and **there is one binding in the entire corpus**. `claim-backing` asked for 💡→🔨; that flip never ran (see [[D400]]) |
| D132 ✅ | **`feedbackClaims[].id` uniqueness is refused nowhere, which reopens D98 through the digest.** `validatePackDocument` builds `const claimIds = new Set(...)` (`apps/server/src/pack-validation.ts`) to resolve `stated_reasoning` references and never compares its size to the array length; the only `DUPLICATE_*` codes in the file are for timing windows and trajectory legs [V]. Give claims 0 and 5 the same id, bind claim 0 honestly, and every `claimBindings` check passes — while an id-keyed backing test admits claim 5's unrelated prose as `ledger_bound`. Fixed by `CLAIM_ID_DUPLICATE` plus resolving the backing test by pointer rather than by id; **0 committed packs affected today** | 🔨 fixed in `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md` §3.2 by the cross-review 2026-08-15 |
| D133 ✅ | **The `EVIDENCE_OVERREACH` template exemption is record-level, not pattern-level, so narrowing it for one pattern widens it for four.** `check.ts`'s guard reads `(!isExplorerTemplate && !isEngineTemplate && PROSE_POINTERS.some(...))` — a record satisfying either template is exempt from **all five** prose patterns at once. `claim-backing`'s drafted §3.2(5) struck the exemption only for `/feedbackClaims/…` while also deleting the templates' must-be-a-claim-pointer clause and their byte-exact prose comparison, leaving a templated `engine_eval` free to support `/objective/summary`, `/planClasses/<i>/description`, `/deviations/<i>/note` or `/spine/…/annotations/<i>` with nothing checking it. Fixed by striking the exemption for all five patterns | 🔨 fixed in `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md` §3.2 by the cross-review 2026-08-15 |
| D134 ✅ | **Three measurements in `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md` were falsifiable and one criterion was vacuous.** (a) *"0 of 241 positions is fully censused"* — **36 are** [V], all single-legal-move links of the mating spines; the true and stronger statement is **0 of the 199 choice-bearing positions**. (b) Centipawn `1` listed as appearing nowhere — it is present among 157 distinct `candidates[].centipawns` values, as is `52`; only `109`, `−106`, `−120`, `−170` are genuinely absent, and `−107`/`−110` are present, which shows value-membership is a weak instrument. (c) The Summary attached prose-**character** shares (49.0 / 64.5 / 95.4%) to claim-**count** ratios (70/131 = 53.4%, 90/131 = 68.7%, 126/131 = 96.2%). (d) Criterion 9 required *"Bucket 3 must remain non-empty"* — a threshold of 1, sitting on the boundary the owner's own DTZ-floor ruling forbids — and pinned it to *"131 of 131"*, unfalsifiable against a corpus of 166. All four corrected in place; the §1.2 and §4 baselines and the root-coverage row reproduced **exactly**, which is what shows the method was sound and only the totals slipped | 🔨 fixed in `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md` by the cross-review 2026-08-15 |
| D135 🔨 | **The `author_principle` escape is closed, but relabel pressure remains unmitigated.** `pack-validation.ts` now requires every self-declared principle to resolve into the 13-entry registry with `standsOn`, `counterCase`, and provenance. The cheaper relabel remains structurally available: 0 of 52 `corpus_observed` and 0 of 8 `engine_validated` claims carry a self-declared label, while 16 of 37 `tablebase_exact` claims do. | 🔨 partial: registry fence shipped; relabel pressure remains measured and open |
| D136 ✅ | **`attachExplorerEvidence` mutates the pack twice, so deleting the prose overwrite does not stabilise `packDigest`.** Two lines apart, `explorer.ts` assigns `pack.feedbackClaims[i].text = renderExplorerFrequency(values)` **and** `pack.provenance.sources = [...new Set([...])]`, and `digestDrillPack(pack)` then runs over the mutated object. `claim-backing`'s drafted §3.5 and criterion 6 name only the first, so a faithful implementation would still move the digest and rewrite `pack.json`. The second assignment is also the only route by which the Lichess attribution reaches `provenance.sources`, so dropping it silently is a licence defect. Fixed by removing both and refusing up front with `ATTACH_SOURCE_LINE_MISSING`; §3.5 also missed that the flat-file write branch — the one `content/drafts/*.json` uses — bypasses `atomicCanonical` entirely | 🔨 fixed in `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md` §3.5 by the cross-review 2026-08-15 |
| D105 🐞 | **A `retryVariants` note names a pack that does not exist, and nothing can see it.** `content/drafts/queen-vs-pawn-seventh-convert.json`'s `opposite_side` entry defers its save-side sibling to a pack nobody has written — *"The companion c2 query in provenance is the seed for that hold pack"* — and because the referent is a substring of a free-text `note`, no validator can resolve it. Measured 2026-08-15 in the `format-surface` cross-review, which built the set of **79** real pack ids in `content/` and matched every one of the **11** committed `retryVariants` entries against it: **8 name a real id, 3 name none** (this one, plus the two entries in `schemas/drill_pack.example.json`, which carry no `note` at all). This is the concrete cost of *"names no referent"*: a dangling cross-reference is indistinguishable from a live one. **`format-surface`'s `RETRY_VARIANTS_NOT_EXECUTABLE` warning does NOT discharge this row** — it fires on the field, not on the unresolvable name inside the note. Closing it needs the `variantOf` array widening (that RFC's Open question 4), which is `VARIANT_PACK_UNKNOWN`-checked already (`pack-validation.ts:670-679`). **Correction landed here 2026-08-16 by the `dead-vocabulary` cross-review, because its home is immutable:** `format-surface` §3.3 states *"`variantOf` has zero users in `content/`"* — **stale, two committed adopters** since `caa8afa` (`vocabulary-wiring`). The archived RFC cannot be edited (`rfc/0000-rfc-process.md`), so the correction lives in this row and in `vocabulary-wiring` §7. **The widening premise survives the correction** — a field with two users is still the successor, not a dead one — but any future reader arguing from "zero users" is arguing from a fact that expired within a day | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 |
| D106 🐞 | **`targetElo` is accepted beside `strong_engine` and silently dropped.** `$defs/opponentPolicy` admits `targetElo` for all seven modes, but `appliedTargetElo` returns `undefined` whenever `health.identity.eloHonored !== true` (`apps/server/src/engine-band.ts:72`), and the shipped predicate naming the band-honouring modes — `policyUsesMaiaBand` — admits `human_common`, `theory_strict` and `practical_resistance` and **not** `strong_engine`. So an author may declare a Stockfish Elo, the format takes it, the engine ignores it, and `eloApplied` never records it. Textbook declared-vs-executable, and it cannot be fixed by honouring the value: weakened Stockfish is rejected doctrine (`AGENTS.md` §Rejected). Found 2026-08-15 in the `format-surface` cross-review while testing that RFC's per-leg narrowing, which now refuses the pair at leg level (`LEG_POLICY_ELO_UNHONORED`) — **the pack-level instance is untouched and is this row** | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 |

Provider matrix tests include the empty, judge-only, opponent-only, and opponent-plus-tablebase cases. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the selection-integrity batch |

| D104 🐞 | **The served-Najdorf browser walkthrough is nondeterministic after a branch move.** On an unchanged post-archive tree, one zero-retry `make test-browser` run timed out waiting for `Active line 4 plies` after `clickMove("d1", "d2")` (**23 passed, 1 failed, 1 baseline skip**); the immediately preceding and following full runs both passed **24 with the same skip**. This is not the retired stale-bounds D14 failure: it is the run state failing to advance or render after the move. Retrying in configuration remains forbidden; the race needs its own diagnosis. **2026-08-16 negative reproduction:** the exact test passed 20/20 isolated zero-retry repetitions plus one full-suite run; no patch was inferred from the absence. | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-15; not reproduced in 21 fresh runs on 2026-08-16 |
| D113 💡 | **Hint DISTANCE as an assistance axis — the owner's 2026-08-15 idea, and the mechanism for it already ships unused.** Today a hint is all-or-nothing: rung 0 states a fact, rung 5 states an authored claim, and neither has a *dial*. The proposal is a hint whose vagueness is **derived rather than chosen** — point at a square, a piece, a ply-distance or a move, four increasing disclosures over **one** piece of evidence. `bestline` records carry the full PV (`movesUci`) and the plumbing is end-to-end — `api.ts` → `rest.ts:1354` → `evidence-queue.ts:394` → `compare.ts:204` — yet **0 of 764 committed records are `bestline`** (391 `engine_eval`, 341 `tablebase_result`, 32 `position_legality`, zero bestlines). Same shape as the tempo layer and `arrows`: shipped, plumbed, never authored against. **This is what the campaign's loadout slots want to be made of** — a slot that buys *how close to the answer* you may look is opportunity cost that does not price retrying | 💡 open, owner idea 2026-08-15 |
| D114 🐞 | **Eval swing ALONG a principal variation is the wrong instrument for "where does it get sharp", and it is wrong by construction.** The PV is the line where the evaluation *does not* move — that is what makes it the PV — so score fluctuation along it detects search instability, not a threat. The signal the hint idea actually wants is the **MultiPV gap**: best minus second-best at a ply. A large gap is mechanically *"one move holds here and everything else drops X"*, which is a rung-2 fact about the position rather than a judgement about the learner. Recorded because the intuition is right and the instrument is subtly wrong, and building the obvious version would produce a hint that fires on search noise. `MultiPV` is already plumbed (`strong-engine.ts:30,55`, `evidence-queue.ts:338`) and `api.ts:925` requests `bestline` at `multiPv: 1` — the one setting that cannot produce the gap | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 |
| D115 🐞 | **A PV-derived hint is a CONDITIONAL presented as a prediction unless its scope is carried.** *"Something happens on f7 in three moves"* is true only under best play by both sides; the learner deviating voids it, and the learner is the one being hinted. `design/05` §2's rung-0 scope corrections already establish the rule — *"b5 is denied **while the a4 pawn stands**"* — and this is the same failure one rung up. Any hint drawn from a line must say **under whose play**, or it is the `outpaced` trap in a new location | 💡 open, found 2026-08-15 |

So every pack-attached capability in flight — bound `deviation.cost`, engine conditions, claim bindings, recorded readings — measures its payoff against a corpus a production deployment does not load, where the only pack is `schemas/drill_pack.example.json`. Not an argument against any of them; an argument against quoting the number without the contingency. Found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/evidence-at-runtime.md`, whose §8.4 states it rather than assuming it away | 💡 **SHARPENED 2026-08-16 by running `make graduation-report` — the mechanism landed today and NOTHING can use it.** `rfc/pack-graduation.md` archived (graduation is a *move*, not a copy); the report now reads **documents 56, blocking 220, resolved 30, accepted 43** and, at the bottom, **"Graduable drafts and packs: (none)"**. Zero of fifty authored packs. **And the reason is uniform: every blocker ends `clears via (unspecified)`.** The corpus is authored, the mechanism exists, and **the bridge between them is undefined** — so this is not a content backlog, it is a missing clearance vocabulary. `content/packs/` holding only `.gitkeep` is the symptom; 220 unclearable blockers is the defect. **Nothing this product has authored can reach a non-development registry today** |

No `category`, no `dtz`, no `dtm`, no piece-count abstention contract, no probe of any kind. Its honest rung is **0** — a material census is arithmetic over the position. The packet's true map at HEAD is therefore **rungs 0, 3 and 5**, with **1, 2 and 4 all absent**, which makes the ledger's 341 `tablebase_result` records a genuine gain rather than a duplication. Escalated as a row rather than written into `design/05`, per law 5 | 🔨 **restated (D400) — half this row is now false and the half that matters is still true.** The `endgameReading` finding stands verbatim: `endgame.ts:10/40`, no `category`/`dtz`/`dtm`, honest rung 0. **The headline is superseded** — the packet gained `readings` carrying admitted `tablebase_result` (rung 1) and `engine_eval` (rung 2), so *"rungs 1, 2 and 4 all absent"* is false; rung 4 alone is absent. **`42680cf` rewrote D116/D118/D119/D120 to say exactly this and left this row stating the old map** |

Same shape as [[D125]] one tier down: *not authored* and *not reachable* look the same | 💡 open — **re-derived at HEAD and unchanged (D400): the same fifteen packs, 32/47 = 68.1% grounded, `AssessmentGrounding` still the binary `"ledger_verified" | "unverified"`** `[V]`. No `.evidence.json` added anywhere since 2026-08-15. **Its own owning RFC corrected this row's population and the correction never flowed back** — `evidence-at-runtime` §9 states 21 of **53** registry documents, six of the difference being browser fixtures |
| D142 💡 | **The runtime already opens every pack's evidence ledger and throws it away — [[D118]]'s cost is a discarded object, not a missing contract.** `PackRegistry.loadDefault` resolves `<stem>.evidence.json` through `sidecarPaths`, reads it with `optionalJson`, and hands it to `fromDocuments`, where exactly one call consumes it: `assessmentGrounding({document, ledger, manifest})`, which returns a **two-valued string**. The ledger object then falls out of scope; `PackRecord` has seven fields and none of them is the ledger. So the file read, the parse and the digest-linkage check are already paid for every pack, on every start, and **one bit of 764 records is retained**. This is why the fix needs no pack version, no run version and no migration — the join is keeping an object that is already in hand | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/evidence-at-runtime.md` |
| D143 🐞 | **No position in the corpus carries both an engine reading and a tablebase reading, so nothing can corroborate one instrument against the other.** Across the 32 committed ledgers: **569** distinct anchor FENs, of which **281** have an `engine_eval` and **288** have a `tablebase_result` — 281 + 288 = 569 exactly, with zero overlap. The instruments partition the corpus **by pack**: 17 packs are 100% Stockfish (openings), 15 are 100% Syzygy (endgames), none mixes. Consequences worth having written down: no cross-instrument agreement check is possible on committed data; any design assuming a position can be read two ways is assuming a corpus that does not exist; and the tablebase/engine split is a property of the authoring pipeline (`verifyEngineDraft` vs `verifySyzygyDraft` each stamp only their own kind), not of the positions | 💡 open — **re-derived exactly (D400): 569 distinct anchor FENs, 281 with `engine_eval`, 288 with `tablebase_result`, 0 overlap, 0 mixed packs** `[V]`. **One figure was already corrected by its own RFC and never flowed back**: the partition is **20 packs Stockfish-only / 12 Syzygy-only**, not 17/15 — `evidence-at-runtime` §9 published that and noted 17/15 was wrong at `bc3cdc1` too |
| D144 💡 | **158 FENs are recorded in more than one pack's ledger, and single-ledger resolution refuses all of them.** Of 569 distinct anchor FENs, 396 appear once, 159 twice, 8 three times, 4 four times and 2 five times — opening transpositions across sibling repertoire packs. `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md` §3.3 established the rule that makes them unusable: *"a binding that reaches outside its own digest is unverifiable"*, so a pack may resolve only against its own ledger. That is correct for prose binding and it is inherited by any runtime projection for the same reason — but it is also the single cheapest coverage widening available to the pack-attached evidence work, and admitting it would make a pack's evidence depend on which other packs happen to be loaded. An owner call, not a tooling one. Sibling of `anti-scandinavian-white`'s split backing in [[D110]]'s neighbourhood: the same position is measured in one pack and unavailable in the next | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/evidence-at-runtime.md` |
| D145 🐞 | **`voiceCheck` validates only `packet.sentences`, so any packet field that produces no sentence is invisible to the renderer AND to the check.** The check builds `const source = packet.sentences.join("\n")` and tests renderer output against that string alone; `ExternalHttpVoiceProvider.render` transmits `{personaPrompt, sentences, scope}` and nothing else. `EvidencePacket` has nine fields and eight of them are server-local bookkeeping. So [[D116]]'s *"widen the evidence packet"* means, mechanically, **widen `sentences`** — via a frozen deterministic renderer in the `renderEndgameReading` / `renderPivotalMarker` mould — and a structured field added without one is decoration that no learner and no validator will ever see. Recorded because it is the first thing any implementer of D116 or D118 will get wrong, and because it is also the reason the widening is law-8-safe: a sentence is what the check binds | 💡 open — **re-verified verbatim (D400)**: `voice.ts:110` is still `packet.sentences.join("\n")` and the provider still transmits sentences only. **`EvidencePacket` is now ten fields, not nine — and the tenth, `readings`, is precisely the no-sentence field this row predicted.** The implementer handled it as the row prescribed: frozen renderer, appended *after* the check. The mechanism statement stands as the standing constraint for the next packet field |
| D146 🐞 | **`voiceCheck` is token-membership, not proposition-checking — and the residual gets more expensive with every rung added to the packet.** It extracts squares, UCI, SAN, `CHESS_LEXICON` nouns, `BANNED_JUDGEMENTS` and `PRESCRIPTIVE_VERBS` from the renderer's output and refuses any that is not a substring of the packet's sentences. It cannot refuse a *join*: two admitted facts recombined into a claim neither record makes passes, because every token was present. Pre-existing and structural, but the cost rises sharply with rung 2, because a centipawn number is exactly the token that invites a causal join ("+0.54, so the knight on e5 is strong" — where `+0.54` came from a record and `knight`/`e5` came from the rung-0 structural sentences). The two things that blunt it are not fixes: past tense with authoring attribution in the frozen sentence, and the banned-judgement vocabulary. This is the ceiling of any mechanical check over free prose — the same ceiling `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md` §2 names for its span binding (*"it does not guarantee that the sentence composed from those fragments is true"*) | 💡 open — **re-verified verbatim (D400)**: `voice.ts:109-116` compares squares, UCI, SAN and three frozen word lists against `packet.sentences`, and nothing else. **No proposition check exists anywhere in the tree.** Same shape as [[D131]] and [[D266]] |
| D147 💡 | **Rung 4 reaches a live surface but never the evidence packet, so corpus frequency can be shown and can never be worded.** `GET /runs/:id/corpus` ships: a disclosure-gated, ephemeral, explicitly LLM-free explorer panel with closed sentences and the byte-fixed `CORPUS_GUARD` line, gated by the same `permittedAssistance` predicate as the human split. `EvidencePacket` has no rung-4 field and no rung-4 sentence, so `renderVoice` cannot voice it, `voiceCheck` does not bind it, and the six `VoiceScope` surfaces are blind to it. The two rung-4 paths are unjoined, which means [[D119]]'s *"rung 4 absent"* is true of the packet and false of the product — and joining them is not a plumbing job, because the packet path would need an `explorer_position_census` record kind (`rfc/archive/claim-backing.md` §3.7) rather than a live query, to keep the packet free of instrument calls | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/evidence-at-runtime.md` |

The owner's primary want is the opposite thing. **Legs (1) and (2) shipped in `board-annotation`:** learner-drawn marks persist outside the assistance ladder, and a lease-holder's marks relay to viewers with attribution outside matches. **Leg (3), system-drawn directed marks, remains open:** `boardOverlays` is derived from `boardLighting`, not `arrows`, and emits square highlights with no `dest`; the structural reader emits square sets, not vectors. | 💡 **PARTIAL 2026-08-16:** learner and attributed-human marks shipped; system-drawn directed marks still need an honest producer. `format-surface` may not retire the field |

| D160 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-16 by `vocabulary-wiring`.** `docs/engine-workers.md` now states the superseding clause: `record` covers every instrument, stateful or not, and persists what it actually applied and returned. No fourth attest law was created and the archived RFC remains immutable. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by `vocabulary-wiring` |
| D161 ✅ | **Claude fabricated a result split inside the ruling that admits result splits, and paired it with a real count.** D126's illustration read *"At this position 1400–1800 players scored **47/31/22** over **5,069** games."* The count is real — the Maroczy root, reproduced exactly by a live re-query. **The split is invented.** The measured value is **2,629 / 371 / 2,069 = 51.9 / 7.3 / 40.8**. Caught by the explorer-grounding wave and **corrected in place**. This is **D110's exact shape one tier up**: a plausible cardinal breakdown attached to a genuine population, in the document every content agent reads first, teaching the boundary it violates. Two things make it worse than a typo and both are the lesson: the number was **not plausible for the band** — a 31% draw rate occurs **nowhere** in the eleven positions censused, whose range is **4.7–9.8%** — so it was generated from a wrong mental model (GM draw rates) rather than mistyped; and it survived because **an illustration reads as prose, not as a claim**, so no instrument, validator or reviewer was pointed at it. **Consequence: worked examples in ledger and design tier are claims and must carry their provenance or be marked synthetic.** `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md`'s residual sweep would not catch this either — it governs pack prose, not process documents | ✅ **corrected 2026-08-16**. Paired with **D154**, which is the wave's own filing of the same finding; this row carries the correction and the standing rule, that one carries the measurement; the standing rule it produces is open |
| D162 🐞 | **A production deployment serves ONE pack — the schema example — and none of the 47 authored ones.** `PackRegistry.loadDefault` builds `productionPaths` as `[schemas/drill_pack.example.json, ...jsonFiles("content/packs/")]`, and **`content/packs/` contains only `.gitkeep`**. `content/drafts/` — all 47 authored packs, including tonight's eleven middlegame packs and every graded endgame — loads **only in development mode**, and `draftFile` outside development is a hard `TypeError`. So the entire content era has produced **nothing a deployed instance would serve**. Found 2026-08-16 while checking `evidence-at-runtime`'s claim that its payoff is zero in production; that claim is correct and this is the reason, but the consequence is far larger than that RFC — it applies to every pack, every ledger, every shape wiring and every gate that reasons about "the corpus". **Nothing can graduate today either: 0 of 47 packs has an empty `provenance.graduationBlockers`**, so the drafts→packs path is not merely unused, it is unmet by every candidate. This is not a bug in the loader — the split is deliberate and the gate is honest. It is a **product-state fact nobody had stated**: the deployment story and the content story have never been connected, and `design/02`'s hosted-multi-user ruling makes that gap live rather than theoretical | 💡 open, **owner-gated** (D400). `pack-registry.ts:323/331` still builds `productionPaths` from `content/packs/`, which holds only `.gitkeep` and has had no commit since the repo rename; `:328-330` still throws for a `draftFile` outside development. Sibling of [[D138]] from the loader side — **both are the same undefined bridge** |
| D163 ✅ | **Owner ruling 2026-08-16: route the claim, do not forbid the sentence — an instrument label is earned per *segment*, and an unbound assertion demotes the label rather than deleting the prose.** [[D131]] escalated the residual sweep's ceiling as unfixable and the cross-review proposed shipping with it stated; the owner refused, on the ground that the boundary was never *"never say Black is better"* but ***"never say it AS CORPUS OBSERVATION"***. `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md` §3.4a specifies it: `text` is cut at a frozen boundary set (sentence terminators, `;`/`:`, dashes, and the inference markers `so`/`therefore`/`thus`/`hence`/`which means`/`because`/`since`), a segment holding a validating instrument span is instrument-attributed, **every other segment is author-attributed**, and there is no unattributed remainder. Two refusals follow — `CLAIM_AUTHOR_LABEL_REQUIRED` (authored segment without `author_principle`) and **`CLAIM_LABEL_UNEARNED`** (an instrument label with zero instrument-attributed segments), the second being the only refusal in the RFC that fires on the absence of everything and therefore the only one that reaches *"Black is better here"*, which has no span to bind and no token to sweep. `CLAIM_AUTHORED_SPAN_UNLABELLED` is withdrawn: a refusal of the sentence becomes a routing of the label | 🔨 owned by `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md` §2.1/§3.4a, ruled 2026-08-16 |
| D164 ✅ | **The unbound-proposition hole is 76% of the machine-labelled corpus, not a corner of it.** Cutting the **91** machine-labelled claims at HEAD (`ab662f9`) on `claim-backing` §3.4a's frozen boundary set, **69 carry at least one segment with no cardinal, no ordinal, no SAN or square token and no result word** — **126 such segments**, provenance 53 sentence-initial after a terminator, 31 claim-initial, 26 after `;`/`:`, 9 after a dash, 7 after an inference marker [V]. [[D131]] was filed as a stated ceiling; it is the load-bearing wall. Corroborating shape: eleven `da77c56` claims hand-write the mechanism's job into the prose (*"Stated as what happened, per the owner ruling … No move, plan or side in this pack is graded from these numbers"*), which is an author compensating for a missing disposition. The measurement is what turned the cross-review's *state-and-ship* into an owner refusal. **Framing corrected 2026-08-16 by the `feedback-delivery` author round, verified by symbol:** the `author_attributed` *disposition* emits **no issue at all**. What is publication-gated is `EVIDENCE_TYPE_UNBACKED` (`published ? "error" : "warning"`); the routing's own three refusals — `CLAIM_AUTHOR_LABEL_REQUIRED`, `CLAIM_LABEL_UNEARNED`, `CLAIM_READING_UNATTRIBUTED` — are **`error` at every review status**. The distinction matters because "it only warns on drafts" was the reason this hole read as tolerable, and it is true of exactly one of the four | 🔨 measured and answered by [[D163]]'s routing, found 2026-08-16 by `claim-backing` round 2 |
| D165 ✅ | **Owner ruling 2026-08-16: `author_principle` becomes a resolving reference against a principle registry, because the rung the routing targets has to be real first.** [[D135]] found the label gates nothing — one member of a seven-member enum, with nothing checking that a claim carrying it contains a principle — and the cross-review offered measure-and-escalate. The owner ruled build. `content/principles/<id>.json`, loaded by `PrincipleRegistry.loadDefault` mirroring `ShapeRegistry.loadDefault` member for member, validated against a new `schemas/principle_entry.schema.json` at `urn:chess-tabiya:schema:principle-entry:0.1`. An entry carries `id`, `version`, `name`, **`statement`**, `phases` (the `SHAPE_REFERENCE_NEVER_PRESENT` analogue, raising `CLAIM_PRINCIPLE_OFF_PHASE` at warning), a frozen `standsOn` enum (`chess_tradition`/`authors_practice`/`instrument_pattern`), **`counterCase`** (the `typicalMistakes` analogue — *when the principle is wrong*, required and non-empty, because `design/05` rung 5 says an authored claim *"can simply be wrong"* and an entry that cannot say when is a slogan) and `shape_entry`'s `$defs/provenance` verbatim. `official` channel only | 🔨 owned by `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md` §3.10, ruled 2026-08-16 |
| D166 ✅ | **A rate hides its denominator and a count carries it — the one refusal [[D163]]'s routing must not lift.** Routing an unbound assertion to the author is honest for a proposition and is **laundering** for a measurement: attributing *"…f5 scores 90.9% for White"* (Grünfeld, **11 games**, below the 100-game floor at which `explorer.ts` abstains) to the author converts an inadmissible reading into an admissible opinion. `claim-backing` §3.4a step 3(3) therefore refuses a **rate** — a token normalising under the percentage or `displayCp` entries of the frozen table — inside an author-attributed segment with `CLAIM_READING_UNATTRIBUTED` at error, **and no label lifts it**. Raw counts are *not* refused: *"d4-d5 scored 1/0/6 over seven games"* states its population in the same breath, so a learner reading it at rung 5 under the author's name can discount it. This asymmetry is the whole difference between the ruling and `claim-backing` §2's rejected candidate (E) | 🔨 specified in `claim-backing` §3.4a, found 2026-08-16 by round 2 |

C9 (`authoredText` silent on `kind: "claim"`) already closes it and **must not be weakened**; the successor rule for anyone widening the packet later is *instrument-attributed segments only*, which `claim-backing` §3.4a's cut is the first mechanism in the repo able to express | 💡 open (a standing constraint, and it is holding) (D400). `guidance.ts:26-30` — `authoredText` is still silent on `kind: "claim"`; C9 was not weakened. **`evidence-at-runtime` widened the packet the safe way this row prescribed**: `readings` never enters `packet.sentences` and `appendRecordedReadings` runs *after* `voiceCheck`. Measured at `a7e700d` by evaluating the shipped function over all 32 committed ledgers: **32 of 32 return `ledger_verified`, including all 5 whose `packDigest` is stale** — `mate-bishop-knight`, `mate-k-q-technique`, `mate-k-r-technique`, `philidor-passive-rook-convert`, `trajectory-mate-bishop-knight`, the same five [[D179]] found at `1b89123` — carrying **186 records, 181 of them machine readings** [V]. So the one bit the runtime keeps out of 764 records attests **one** record, not the ledger, and it is published on `GET /packs/:id` as `objective.grading.grounding`. `rfc/evidence-at-runtime.md` §2.1 adds a digest conjunct for its own consumer; the general defect is this row | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review |
| D224 🐞 | **`transposeKey` collides inside a single pack's ledger, so "one authored position, one record" is false.** `lucena-bridge-convert` records `8/1P2k3/2K5/8/3R4/8/8/1r6 b - -` **twice** — halfmove clock **9** supporting a spine pointer, clock **13** supporting `/deviations/7/moveUci` — with byte-identical values (`category: "loss"`, `dtz: -8`, `precise_dtz: -8`, `dtm: -28`). Across all 32 ledgers, **732 admitted records occupy 731 distinct `transposeKey`s**; it is the only collision, and there are zero among the 391 `engine_eval` records [V]. Any position-keyed index over a ledger must be multi-valued: a scalar `Map` drops one record and which one depends on iteration order. Pointer coverage is unaffected (the two records back two different pointers) | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review |
| D225 🐞 | **A transposition index measured on the authored tree measures the wrong set, and hides an 11.6% refusal.** `evidence-at-runtime` §2.2 admits a `tablebase_result` only when the node's halfmove clock equals the record's, and expected that to cost nothing "because records are produced by replaying the same moves" — true, and true only of the tree. Measured over the **one-ply frontier** (every legal successor of the 497 spine-line positions of all 32 packs): **372 frontier positions land on a key carrying a `tablebase_result`, and 43 of them (11.6%) arrive with a clock no record at that key holds** [V] — all transpositions, differing by 4–6 halfmoves (`mate-bishop-knight` node clock 7 vs recorded 3, 13 vs 9, 15 vs 11, 19 vs 15). The rule is right and is kept; the generalization is that **coverage claims about a transposition index must be measured on the frontier, never on the tree the records were written from** | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review |
| D226 🐞 | **`voiceCheck` is a token filter, and every RFC that treats it as a proposition binder over-claims it — demonstrated twice, by execution against the shipped function.** (1) **Absence is speakable.** Against a packet whose only sentence is `"This pack declares: opening."`, `voiceCheck` returns `valid: true` for *"No reading was recorded at this position."*, *"The engine is silent here."*, *"Nothing was recorded for this spot when the pack was authored."* and *"No measurement exists here; elsewhere on this line there was one."* [V] — none contains a square, a move, a `CHESS_LEXICON` noun, a `BANNED_JUDGEMENTS` word or a `PRESCRIPTIVE_VERB`, which is the whole of what the check inspects. An entirely invented recorded-reading sentence passes the same packet for the same reason. So *"packet membership is a speaking licence"* holds only for chess **tokens**; its converse — non-membership as prohibition — is false for all other prose, which is [[D146]] at full strength with an executed counter-example instead of an argument. (2) **The token admission is `String.includes`, not a word-boundary match:** `source.toLowerCase().includes(token)` means a sentence containing `sf-a1x` licenses the square `a1`, while a `\b`-anchored regex over that same sentence finds nothing to object to — so every acceptance criterion that tests *"the sentence does not **match** `SQUARE`/`UCI`/`SAN`"* is testing the wrong direction. Both are pre-existing and orthogonal to any one packet field; both become load-bearing the moment a frozen sentence carries a machine-stamped identity string, or a design leans on `voiceCheck` to make absence unspeakable | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review |
| D227 ✅ | **`content/drafts/` holds 53 pack documents and `PackRegistry.loadDefault` loads all 53 in development, six of which are browser test fixtures.** `jsonFiles` excludes only the four `SIDECAR_BASENAMES` suffixes, so `immediate-guard.browser.json`, `line-boundary.browser.json`, `outcome-hold.browser.json`, `outcome-resist.browser.json`, `stated-reasoning.browser.json` and `trajectory-legs.browser.json` are indexed as ordinary packs [V]. Every corpus figure quoted as "of 47 authored packs" — [[D138]], [[D141]], [[D162]] — is silently excluding those six, and **no code performs that exclusion**, so no test can assert the number the prose uses. 21 of 53 documents carry no ledger. Either the fixtures move out of `content/drafts/`, or "47" stops being a denominator | ✅ closed by catalogue fixture exclusion — default discovery rejects *.browser.json while Playwright injects all six fixtures explicitly |
| D228 💡 | **Two capability registers now describe the same instruments and nothing makes them agree.** `a7e700d` shipped `CAPABILITY_DISPOSITIONS` and `assertAdvertisedCapabilityDispositions` (`apps/server/src/capabilities.ts`): 32 rows, a four-valued `reached`/`refused`/`unmeasured`/`impossible`, a `reason` on every row, an `experiment` required on every `unmeasured`, and a coverage gate that throws when a ready engine advertises an option no row dispositions. `rfc/evidence-at-runtime.md` §5.1 proposes a **second** registry — admitted evidence-record kinds, published as `Capabilities.recordedReadingKinds` — computed independently. They overlap on Stockfish, Syzygy and Explorer and already differ in emphasis: the shipped register says `Explorer / position white / draws / black → reached (corpus panel)` while the RFC refuses rung 4 as having no producer. Both are true of different layers, which is exactly how two registers drift | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review |
| D229 ✅ | **`packages/runtime` cannot see `apps/server`, so any evidence-packet field typed off a server constant is unbuildable — and the runtime already exports the type each attempt reaches for.** The runtime's dependencies are `@chess-tabiya/schema` and `chessops`, and nothing else [V]. `EvidencePacket`, `voiceCheck` and every `render*Reading` live there; `ASSESSMENT_CATEGORIES`, `TABLEBASE_CATEGORIES` and `EVIDENCE_KINDS` live in `apps/server`. `evidence-at-runtime` §3.4 typed a packet field as `(typeof ASSESSMENT_CATEGORIES)[number]` with the note *"there is no `AssessmentCategory` type in the repo"* — but `packages/runtime/src/branch-scale.ts` exports `AssessmentCategory = "win" \| "loss" \| "draw" \| "cursed-win" \| "blessed-loss"`, exactly the five determinate values, re-exported from the runtime index [V]. The rule is always **type in the runtime, narrow in the server** (`apps/server/src/tablebase.ts` already imports `transposeKey` from the runtime on line 1), and it should be stated once rather than rediscovered per RFC | ✅ closed with D258 — AssessmentCategory is declared once in the schema package and imported/re-exported by runtime while the server tuple is checked against it |
| D230 💡 | **[[D194]] has a general shape and no general guard: a type-keyed barrier in front of a payload passthrough.** `publicEvents` allowlists event **types** (`engineFeedbackEvent` matches `evidence.attached` and machine-ref `objective.state_changed`) while `GET /runs/:id/events` forwards whole event objects — so a new **field** on an unlisted type egresses by default, and nobody reviewing the field is looking at the barrier. Pack egress does **not** have the defect and is the contrast worth naming: `projectPackDocument` and `PackSummary` are built field by field, so a new `PackRecord` field is invisible to `GET /packs/:id` and `GET /packs` by construction [V]. The general rule: anything reaching a passthrough must itself be projected by enumeration, or the barrier must key on payload shape rather than on type | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review |
| D231 🐞 | **Rung 4's refusal is now a refusal of a *specified* record kind, and no single row says so.** Four rows hold the pieces: [[D126]] ruled explorer W/D/B splits **admissible** as `corpus_observed`; `da77c56` authored **16 such claims across eleven packs**, its own message calling them *"the first position-level rung-4 evidence in the repo"*; [[D150]] measures that all 16 are unbound prose and pass only because those eleven packs have no `.evidence.json`; `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md` §3.7 specifies `explorer_position_census` as the fix. Re-derived at `a7e700d`: **zero `explorer_frequency` records exist anywhere** — 0 in the 32 draft ledgers, 0 in the 36 candidate ledgers, whose 129 records are 52 `opening_identity`, 27 `position_legality`, 26 `puzzle_provenance` and 24 `engine_eval` [V]. So every clause-1 refusal is still correct; what no reader of any one row can tell is that the refusal has become a **sequencing** state with a named owner rather than an absence of demand | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review |
| D232 ✅ | **Nothing pins that a new evidence-packet construction site arrives with its disclosure gate.** `a452abb` ("fix: gate reasoning review evidence") closed [[D140]] by **adding the missing call**, not by adding a guard: the disclosure gate is four hand-written `requireGuidanceDisclosure(...)` lines in `rest.ts` sitting beside four hand-written `evidencePacket({...})` calls, and the failure mode is a fifth site. The fix's shape is worth keeping — it adds a second, independent `service.guidanceAccess` call rather than widening the role-blind `reasoningReviewAccess` — but a structural test (every `evidencePacket(` preceded by a gate in the same block), or a constructor that takes the access object, costs one test and removes the class | ✅ closed by evidence-at-runtime — `guidance.test.ts` enumerates all four sites and requires a preceding disclosure gate |
| D257 ✅ | **The corpus figure the whole project quotes — "47 authored packs" — is computed by no code, so no test can ever assert it.** `PackRegistry.loadDefault` walks `content/drafts/` with `jsonFiles`, which excludes only the four `SIDECAR_BASENAMES` suffixes; `*.browser.json` is not one of them, so the registry indexes **53** documents in development, six of them browser fixtures [V]. "47" is an **editorial** exclusion applied in prose by every RFC and ledger row that quotes it ([[D138]], [[D141]], [[D162]]) and performed by nothing in the tree. This is the general form of [[D227]] and it earns its own row because the defect is not the number: **a figure with no producer cannot be pinned by an acceptance criterion**, so it drifts silently and every consumer re-derives it differently. Two cheap fixes: move the fixtures out of `content/drafts/`, or export the exclusion as a predicate the prose and the tests share | ✅ closed with D227 — isPackDocumentName is the exported discovery predicate and the default-catalogue test pins that browser fixtures are absent |
| D258 ✅ | **`AssessmentCategory` is declared three times, and each RFC picks a different one.** The identical five-member union `"win" \| "loss" \| "draw" \| "cursed-win" \| "blessed-loss"` appears in `packages/schema/src/drill-pack/types.ts`, in `packages/runtime/src/branch-scale.ts` (re-exported from the runtime's `index.ts`), and as the value-level tuple `ASSESSMENT_CATEGORIES` in `apps/server/src/tablebase.ts` [V]. [[D229]] found the second and concluded *"type in the runtime, narrow in the server"*, which is right for `evidence-at-runtime` — but it is a choice among three, and the **schema** copy is the only one both other packages already depend on, so it is the only declaration that could be single. Not consolidated by any in-flight RFC because none has a consumer asking for it; recorded so the next one reads a row instead of discovering three declarations | ✅ closed with D229 — the schema package is the single AssessmentCategory declaration consumed by runtime and server |
| D259 🐞 | **`voiceCheck` has exactly one production call site, and `/reasoning-review` is not behind it.** The check runs only inside `renderVoice` (`apps/server/src/guidance.ts`) [V]. `/reasoning-review` calls `voiceProvider.render(packet, voicePersona, prompt, "reasoning")` **directly** and filters the *response* with `reasoningMatchCheck`, so `packet.sentences` — including its rung-3 `human_divergence` sentences — reaches an external provider with **no `voiceCheck` in front of it at all**. [[D140]] fixed the *disclosure* gap on that route at `a452abb`; the *check* gap is a different hole and is still open. It matters wherever an RFC reasons that "the packet is bound by `voiceCheck`": on one of the four packet construction sites it is bound by nothing | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D260 🐞 | **A persona-prompt constraint cannot make a concept unspeakable when the packet names the concept.** The `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review moved absence-honesty onto a criterion reading *"the persona prompt must never mention recorded readings, their absence, or coverage"*, on the reasoning that a renderer never told the concept exists has no reason to word it. The reasoning is sound; the premise fails **inside the same RFC**, because every frozen sentence arm opens *"Recorded reading at this position"* — so the provider is taught the concept by the payload at every node that carries one. **General form: constraining the prompt is worthless whenever the data carries the vocabulary.** Answered in that RFC by §3.8 (the provider never receives a reading at all); recorded here because the reasoning error is reusable and the next design to reach for a prompt constraint should check what its payload says first | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D261 💡 | **`voicePersona` is a deployment option, so every persona-prompt criterion pins a default and nothing more.** `ApplicationOptions.voicePersona` flows through `createRestHandler` and defaults to *"Clear, concise Tabiya voice. Do not add chess claims."* [V]. A test can assert that string; it cannot assert what an operator configures. Any RFC treating a persona constraint as a guarantee is treating a default as an invariant. Either the persona becomes a frozen constant with an assertion at construction, or persona-based constraints are documented as defaults — the second is honest and free, the first is the one that would actually bind | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D262 🐞 | **A `transposeKey` index over the corpus has three different "position counts" and the RFCs have been mixing them.** Measured at `0241a98` over the 32 draft ledgers [V]: **732** admitted records occupy **731** `(pack, key)` index entries — one intra-pack collision, `lucena-bridge-convert` at halfmove clocks 9 and 13 ([[D224]]) — over **568** distinct keys corpus-wide, because **158** anchor FENs recur across packs. The same three-way split runs through the frontier: **11,464** per-pack-distinct successor positions against **7,589** corpus-wide, a 34% difference. No number is wrong; quoting one under the other's label is, and the cross-review's *"731 distinct positions"* did exactly that. **Rule: any corpus figure derived from a per-pack index must say "summed per pack" or "corpus-wide" in the same sentence as the number** | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D263 💡 | **Publishing a pack moves its digest, so the corpus is designed to go stale under its own ledgers — and the only runtime consequence today is a CLI warning.** `rfc/pack-graduation.md` §4.5 establishes that setting `reviewStatus: "published"` moves `digestDrillPack(document)`, because `provenance` is inside the digested document; that RFC accepts a 32-of-32 `packDigest` re-stamp as a **landing-order obligation** shared with `claim-backing`, whichever lands second. Meanwhile the repository's only staleness check is `EVIDENCE_DIGEST_STALE` in `sourcing/check.ts` at severity **warning**, in the authoring CLI ([[D223]]). So the drift is **scheduled, recurring and invisible at runtime**. `evidence-at-runtime` §2.1 fences its own consumer with a load-time refusal; nothing fences the others. **Demonstrated live during the author round, in both directions:** the stale set was **5 packs / 181 machine readings** at `0241a98` and **3 packs / 104** at `c55b9cf`, because `caa8afa` (*"feat: wire pack vocabulary reach"*) added a `variantOf` block to two packs and **re-stamped both ledgers in the same commit** [V]. The in-commit re-stamp is the discipline working; the fact that the number moved 43% within hours is why any figure quoted about staleness has a shelf life and why criteria should report it rather than freeze it | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D264 🐞 | **The `CORPUS_GUARD` "one constant, one render site" pattern has already forked, which is what any criterion written against it must catch.** `apps/web/src/lib/corpus-sentences.ts` declares `CORPUS_GUARD` = *"These counts say what this population played, not what is good."*; `apps/server/src/repertoire.ts` declares `REPERTOIRE_CORPUS_GUARD` — the same sentence **minus its trailing period**, in a different package — and interpolates it into two generated strings [V]. Two near-identical population guards already exist, so a test asserting *"one reference to the constant"* is satisfied by a **copy of the string**, which is the failure that actually happened. Any guard-sentence criterion must assert the sentence is not **declared** twice, not that the constant is referenced once | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D265 💡 | **The digest-conjunct pattern generalises: a two-valued grounding verdict is not an admission gate, and every consumer of one needs its own second predicate.** `assessmentGrounding` returns `"ledger_verified" \| "unverified"` and attests **one** record; `evidence-at-runtime` §2.1a adds `ledger.packDigest === digest` for its own consumer and converts an authoring-time **warning** into a load-time **refusal**. The shape is reusable and the reasoning is: a summary verdict computed for one purpose (does the *assessment* have a backing record?) is being read for another (does this *ledger* describe this *document*?). Three properties made the fix cheap and are worth naming as a checklist — both operands were already in scope one line apart in `PackRegistry.fromDocuments`; the cost was measurable before writing any code (5 packs, 181 readings); and the failure direction is silence rather than a false statement. [[D223]] is the defect, this row is the pattern | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D266 🐞 | **Three instruments guarantee tokens while being described as guaranteeing propositions, and the description error is the recurring bug rather than the instruments.** [[D234]] names `voiceCheck` (token membership described as a speaking licence); [[D131]] names `claim-backing`'s residual sweep (numeral policing described as inference policing); the third is the shape they share — **an allow-list over a lexicon cannot bind a claim, and every design leaning on one must argue its own safety separately.** All three were described correctly *once* and cited loosely afterwards, which is how the error propagates: the citation is shorter than the caveat, so the caveat is what gets dropped. Proposed convention, cheap and checkable in review: **an instrument's description must name what it inspects, not what it is used for** — *"`voiceCheck` compares square/UCI/SAN tokens and three word lists against the packet"*, never *"`voiceCheck` binds the sentence to the packet"* | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `evidence-at-runtime` author round |
| D267 🐞 | **No instrument in this repo can see a citation, so the one step `rfc/pack-graduation.md` §7 prices as the cheapest on the ladder moves no measured number at all.** Run rather than reasoned, at `c55b9cf` and again after this wave's 22 packs were cited: `checkSourcingFile` reports **32 of 47 clean at draft severity and 4 of 47 at published severity BEFORE**, and **32 / 4 AFTER** [V]. The 4 are `anti-caro-advance`, `opening-principles-black`, `opening-principles-white`, `opponent-intent-early-queen`; the 15 draft failures are all `EVIDENCE_READ_ERROR` + `MANIFEST_READ_ERROR` (no sidecars), reproducing [[D238]]. Every remaining published-severity error in the corpus is `EVIDENCE_TYPE_UNBACKED`, which demands an **evidence record of a named kind**, and a bibliographic source produces none. §7's *"10 after a citation pass"* is a hand audit against the **blocker gate**, which is prose — so it can be true while the checker is unmoved, and both statements are now measured | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D268 🐞 | **A retrievable, licence-compatible source cannot enter the sourcing manifest, because `linkage` demands every manifest entry be consumed by an evidence record or abstention and `EVIDENCE_KINDS` has no bibliographic member.** The six kinds are `opening_identity`, `position_legality`, `explorer_frequency`, `tablebase_result`, `engine_eval`, `puzzle_provenance` (`apps/server/src/sourcing/types.ts:57`) [V]; `linkage` raises `MANIFEST_ENTRY_UNUSED` for any entry no record or abstention names (`ledger-validation.ts:383`) [V]; and `evidenceSupports` raises `EVIDENCE_OVERREACH` for any non-template record supporting a `PROSE_POINTERS` target [V]. So the only encodable home for a citation at HEAD is `provenance.sources` (free text) plus `provenance.attribution` (licence-checked only). This is [[D150]]'s exact shape one field over: **the wave produced attribution that no instrument reads.** An abstention was considered and rejected as dishonest — the sources are available; what is missing is a record kind, not a source | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D269 ✅ | **Three evidence sidecars stored a `packDigest` that did not match their pack at HEAD, before this wave touched anything.** `mate-bishop-knight`, `mate-k-q-technique` and `mate-k-r-technique` — 3 of the corpus's 32 sidecars — fail `digestDrillPack` against their stored digest at `c55b9cf` [V], i.e. their evidence was confirmed against a different version of the file and `checkSourcingFile` has been emitting `EVIDENCE_DIGEST_STALE` ("re-confirm evidence") on them unnoticed. It is a **warning**, so nothing fails and nobody looked. This wave did **not** re-stamp them — that would erase a signal it did not create — and instead wrote the finding into each pack's `graduationBlockers` so it survives the next re-stamp. Directly relevant to [[D209]]: `pack-graduation` §4.5 plans to re-stamp all 32 ledgers, which would silently retire these three | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D270 🐞 | **Two of the 22 packs cited this wave now hold a source that argues AGAINST the objective it was fetched to support, and that is the pass's most valuable output.** (1) `berlin-queenless-press` wants *"the king-activity plan for queenless middlegames"*; en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berlin_Defence (CC BY-SA 4.0, read 2026-08-16) calls the position an **endgame**, calls Black's king **"misplaced in the centre"**, and says *"Black can hold the endgame with accurate play"* — the reverse valence, and it names no king-activity plan. The pack's own corpus measurement already said nobody plays it, so **two independent signals now lean against that objective**. (2) `french-advance-chain-white` premises that *holding the base census is the White plan*; en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pawn_structure §e5-chain gives White's themes as *"Kingside mating attack, f2-f4-f5 break"* and mentions d4 only as what **Black** attacks. Neither pack was edited beyond recording this: a citation that refutes is evidence, and law 6 says it gets escalated | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D271 🐞 | **`carlsbad-minority-attack`'s three-plan taxonomy — the pack's self-declared foundational claim — is two-thirds citable and one-third contradicted in shape by the same source.** en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pawn_structure §Carlsbad formation gives *"Themes for White: Minority attack on the queenside. e3-e4 break, sometimes prepared with f2-f3. e5 outpost, sometimes supported with f2-f4"* and *"Themes for Black: e4 outpost, kingside attack"* [V, section wikitext, 2026-08-16]. So plans 1 and 2 are cited; the pack's third White plan, a **kingside attack**, is what the source assigns to **Black** in this structure, and White's third theme there is an e5 outpost. Two routes out and both are authoring, not measurement: re-cut the plan class to the outpost the source names, or find a source that states a White kingside attack in the Carlsbad. The blocker was split rather than retired | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D272 🐞 | **The residue a citation pass structurally cannot clear is *order*, and it is the majority of the middlegame class.** Five of the ten objective-premise blockers reduce to *arrangement before X* — `maroczy-bind-white-squeeze` (arrangement before lever), `kid-mar-del-plata-white` (before the lock), `dragon-yugoslav-race` (before the h-pawn arrives), `iqp-white-panov-attack` (Re1 and Be4 before ...Nce7), `carlsbad-minority-attack` (implicitly) — and **every source consulted lists themes without sequencing them**. Wikipedia's structure articles are theme inventories by construction. So these are not *"no source exists"* and not *"no compatible source exists"*: they are **claims of a kind no encyclopedic source settles**, and per [[D155]] the instrument that would is a game-level corpus that does not exist at HEAD. This is the third wave to arrive at the same missing instrument from a different direction | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D273 🐞 | **One claim in the corpus has a genuinely empty compatible-source shelf, and it is worth distinguishing from the other two failure modes.** `pawn-breakthrough-convert` asserts the three-against-two breakthrough mechanism and that its sacrifice order is not interchangeable. Checked 2026-08-16: en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breakthrough_(chess) **does not exist (HTTP 404)** [V]; en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passed_pawn covers **piece** sacrifices clearing a promotion path, not the pawn breakthrough [V]; en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Chess/The_Endgame/Pawn_Endings covers the opposition and the rule of the square and has no breakthrough section [V]; the MediaWiki search API returns no article for it on either wiki [V]. The only partial hit is en.wikibooks.org/wiki/Chess_Strategy/Queenside_pawn_majority (*"without kings, a pawn breakthrough becomes easier"*), which backs the premise and not the mechanism. The order claim is separately tablebase-grounded; the technique is not, and no CC-BY-SA/CC0 text found states it | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D274 💡 | **Wikipedia's rendered page truncates before the section a citation needs; the MediaWiki `action=parse&section=N` API does not, and that is the method every future citation wave should use.** `en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pawn_structure` carries 20 structure sections and a plain fetch was truncated before *Carlsbad formation* — the exact section three packs needed. `action=parse&page=Pawn_structure&prop=sections` lists the indices and `&prop=wikitext&section=18` returns that section alone, in full, in one small request [V, 2026-08-16]. This turned one article into the single highest-yield source of the wave: §8 Maróczy Bind, §13 e5-chain, §15/§16 the two Isolani formations, §18 Carlsbad and §19 Panov each carry an explicit *"Themes for White / Themes for Black"* list, which is exactly the shape a pack's `planClasses` wants. Cheap, retrievable, CC BY-SA 4.0, and section-addressable | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D275 🐞 | **`provenance.attribution` is validated for its licence string and nothing else, so an attribution chain can be complete, well-formed and entirely fictional.** `licenceObligations` (`apps/server/src/sourcing/check.ts:318`) does two things: it demands an attribution row for every **manifest** entry that is CC-BY-SA-4.0 *and* contributes prose through an evidence record, and it refuses any attribution row whose `licence` is not `"CC-BY-SA-4.0"` [V]. Since [[D268]] means a bibliographic source can never be a manifest entry, the first check is vacuous for this wave's 19 sources, and the second only reads one field. No `url` is fetched, no `sourceId` resolved, no `noticeText` checked against anything. The 22 packs this wave cited pass `sourcing-check` identically to how they would with invented URLs — which is why every source here was fetched and quoted in `provenance.sources` by hand, and why that discipline is currently a convention rather than a gate | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D276 🐞 | **Editing a pack with a JSON round-trip destroys the corpus's hand-formatting, and editing a sidecar with one destroys its single-line form — a 22-pack pass produced 2,101 insertions before it produced 431.** `content/drafts/*.json` mixes pretty-printed and hand-compacted arrays (`berlin-queenless-press.json` keeps `movesSan` on two lines and structural expressions inline), and `*.evidence.json` files are single-line; `json.dump(indent=2)` reformats both, turning a one-field edit into a 293-line or a 1,222-line diff [V, measured both ways this wave]. The fix that worked and should be shipped rather than re-invented: **splice only the block you own** — locate `\n  "provenance": {`, brace-match its span, re-render that object alone; and for a digest, replace the digest literal by string substitution. There is no `make` target that edits a pack field, so every content wave writes this by hand | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the citation pass |
| D204 ✅ | **All four graduation-blocker emitters now produce typed entries accepted by the current schema.** Distillation, opening, position-seed, and Syzygy outputs carry stable `id`, `state`, and `statement` fields and validate their emitted documents. | D205 ✅ | **A `state: "accepted"` blocker is reachable by assertion — `kind` has no per-kind required field and `ruling` is checked against nothing.** `rfc/pack-graduation.md` §1.2 makes `accepted` the one state that can graduate a pack that should not, then guards it with a single lint on *non-blank `accepted.ruling`*: `permanent_property`'s "stated impossibility" and `out_of_scope`'s "named boundary" require no field `owner_ruling` does not, so all three collapse to one free-text string. The fallback guard — a printed corpus-wide page — has no criterion requiring anyone to read it. The repo can afford better: **21 dated owner rulings are greppable in `design/`**, and the 2026-08-13 no-review-workflow ruling is recorded in five living-tier files. Fix is law 3 applied to a field that exempted itself from it: a `rulingRef` that must resolve, plus a committed page so each new acceptance is a diff | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by pack-graduation 0.27 |
| D206 ✅ | **Three of the seven packs recorded as clean at published severity fail `sourcing-check` today, at draft severity.** `conversion-up-a-piece`, `rook-4v3-same-side` and `trajectory-qgd-exchange-minority` have no `.evidence.json` and no `.sources.json`, so `checkSourcingFile` raises `EVIDENCE_READ_ERROR` + `MANIFEST_READ_ERROR` at the default severity, which `issue()` defines as `error` (`sourcing/ledger-validation.ts:38`). Measured by **running** the checker over all 47 packs at both review statuses: **4 clean at published, 43 fail, and 15 of 47 fail at draft severity now.** The general rule this cost: **the absence of evidence scores as clean in any derivation that counts unbacked labels instead of running the check** | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by pack-graduation 0.27 |
| D207 ✅ | **The corpus's 97 unbacked machine-checkable claims are 66 reported + 31 masked.** 66 sit in packs with a ledger and surface as `EVIDENCE_TYPE_UNBACKED` across 28 packs; the other 31 sit in the 12 ledger-less packs that carry machine labels, where `checkSourcingFile` fails on the missing sidecar first and never reaches the label check. 66 + 31 = 97 exactly. The number is right and it is not what the gate says | ✅ closed by the graduation-emitter residue batch — missing ledgers now emit EVIDENCE_TYPE_UNBACKED for every machine-labelled claim in addition to EVIDENCE_READ_ERROR |
| D208 ✅ | **`make verify` never runs `sourcing-check`, and after a pack graduates it would run on the wrong root.** `verify` is `typecheck test schema-check` (`Makefile:20`); `sourcing-check` is a separate target requiring an explicit `DIR=`/`FILE=` (`Makefile:60-63`), and every caller of `checkSourcingDirectory`/`checkSourcingFile` in the repo is an emitter's internal re-check or a unit test over a temp directory — **no test runs it over `content/drafts/` or `content/packs/`**. So the published-severity escalation that `pack-graduation` §5 makes load-bearing is enforced by a command someone must remember to type, aimed at `DIR=content/drafts` — **the one directory a published pack has just left**. Same family as [[D175]]: a maturity step that exits its own gate | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `pack-graduation` cross-review; floor set by that RFC's criterion 14 |
| D209 ✅ | **Two in-flight RFCs each assert a digest-freshness criterion the other breaks.** `pack-graduation` §4.5 moves all 47 pack digests and re-stamps 32 ledgers; `claim-backing` round 2 §3.10/§5.1 moves 35 packs and 29 ledgers over an overlapping set. Neither names the other, and each pins *0 `EVIDENCE_DIGEST_STALE`* as an acceptance criterion. **A criterion that asserts a repo-wide state rather than an end-of-commit state is one two RFCs can both pass and jointly violate** — the fix is to make the re-stamp a landing-order obligation | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by pack-graduation 0.27 |

REFUTED` entries record refutations; `anti-caro-advance`'s `ENGINE-CHECKED` entry **opens** a new defect (*"the spine's model answer is the third-ranked of its own four candidates"*) in the corpus's most graduation-ready pack; four entries use ALLCAPS `UNGROUNDED` to mark debt; and `maroczy-bind-white-squeeze` records a **completed** engine pass in lowercase with no marker at all. | D237 ✅ | **The cheapest moment to build a gate is while the thing it guards does not exist.** `content/packs/` holds only `.gitkeep`, so a strict `checkSourcingDirectory` sweep over it inside `make verify` costs **zero** today and can never be red without someone having graduated a pack that should not have. Added after the first pack lands, the same gate is either redundant or under pressure to be weakened on its first use — by the author who most wants that pack to pass. The general form: **a gate added before its first subject is free and uncontested; the same gate added afterwards is a negotiation** | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by pack-graduation 0.27 |
| D238 ✅ | **A ratchet is the honest form of a gate you cannot afford to close.** **15 of 47** `content/drafts/` packs fail `sourcing-check` today at *draft* severity [V], and `pack-graduation` §0.3(a) refuses to pay content debt — so it asserts **≤15**, prints the failing set, and names the wave that drives it to `0` as the owner of the strict flip. **A criterion that reddens the build for debt the RFC declines to pay is a criterion that gets waived, and a waived gate reads as an enforced one.** The ratchet number is the destination that *"a later wave"* was standing in for | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by pack-graduation 0.27 |
| D239 ✅ | **Three of the four `graduationBlockers` emitters validate their own output and throw, so an un-migrated emitter does not emit a bad document — it stops emitting.** `sourcing/openings.ts:120`, `sourcing/position-seeds.ts:252` and `sourcing/syzygy.ts:190` each call `validatePackDocument` on the document they just built and raise `SourcingError("EMITTED_PACK_INVALID")` when it fails; `valid` is `!issues.some(severity === "error")` and schema violations are error-severity (`pack-validation.ts`) [V]. Only `distill.ts` lacks the self-check, and it is the one whose test carries the assertion. Sharpens [[D204]]: the two red tests were the visible half of the break, and the run-time half is larger | ✅ closed by the graduation-emitter residue batch — distillRun validates its completed document and refuses EMITTED_PACK_INVALID before returning |
| D240 🐞 | **Emitter blocker ids are stable literals, but no shared template registry keeps emitters and migrated content aligned.** `sourcing/syzygy.ts` interpolates `options.opponent` into two of its three blocker texts, so the same blocker renders differently per caller [V] — a text-derived id moves with the call site, and the migration's ids then disagree with the emitter's. The general form: **an identity derived from rendered output is an identity that moves when the caller changes** | 🐞 partial: ids are stable literals; the shared emitter/migration template registry remains absent |
| D241 ✅ | **A citation lint that cannot resolve its own anchor form fails open on every typo.** `pack-graduation` §1.2's worked `rulingRef`, `planning/exploration/log.md#2026-08-13-owner-rulings`, is **not** the slug of that heading — the GitHub-style slug carries the whole title through *"…not-verdicts"* [V]. What makes the line form safe is a rule already on the books: **law 7 makes `planning/exploration/log.md` append-only, so it is the one living-tier file whose line numbers are a stable citation target.** `#L<line>` is permitted only into append-only files | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by pack-graduation 0.27 |
| D242 ✅ | **A citation requirement is a real guard only when the honest case can pay it cheaply and the dishonest case cannot pay it at all — so verify payability before ratifying the lint.** [[D205]]'s `rulingRef` was checked against its own migration before acceptance: the 37 no-review-workflow entries quote **one** ruling in seven wordings, so they take **one** reference (`planning/exploration/log.md#L1231`, whose file contains `2026-08-13`) [V], and `GRADUATION_RULING_UNCITED` checks exactly path existence plus date containment. **A lint that blocks the migration it exists to enable is worse than no lint**, and that is a check the author owes before ratifying any reviewer-proposed requirement | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by pack-graduation 0.27 |

| D244 ✅ | **`content/candidates/` is inside the pack schema and outside graduation, and one `readdir` conflated the two questions.** Its 36 `pack.json` documents migrate because `packages/schema/src/drill-pack.test.ts` validates them under the closed policy [V]; they are **not** graduation subjects, because the gate is a `content/drafts/` → `content/packs/` move. The alternative — excluding them from the closed-schema policy so the schema change stays cheap — is declined on the same RFC's own argument: **buying a cheap schema change by narrowing a corpus sweep is the failure shape *"a gate you graduate out of is not a gate"* names.** The candidate→draft promotion path owns their entries | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by pack-graduation 0.27 |
