| D245 🐞 | **A proxy built to be loose was quoted to the unit and read as a bound.** `pack-graduation` §4.3's residual-clause proxy over the 48 resolution-marked entries returns **45** at HEAD with the nine tokens as written and **47** only with a looser tokenization [V]; the cross-review published 47 as a reproducible floor under a hand-counted 42. Both runs are correct and neither is the answer. The general form: **a proxy that moves by two on a word-boundary choice cannot also be cited to the unit — quoting its exact value repeats the error it was built to expose.** Companion to [[D212]] on the other axis: that row is about names recalled versus numbers scripted; this one is about scripted numbers whose script is a guess | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by pack-graduation 0.27 |
| D246 🐞 | **Two cross-RFC lane facts moved under an RFC mid-review, in opposite directions, and both had been cited `[V]`.** `engine-leverage` **landed** 0.23 (`18d2832`), so the register's *"implementing 2026-08-16"* is stale; and an uncommitted `vocabulary-wiring` implementation carries `DRILL_PACK_SCHEMA_VERSION = "0.24"` and `$id … :0.24` in the working tree [V]. Neither touches the 0.27 lane. The general form: **an RFC round that measures a moving tree must pin its commit and re-check every cross-RFC fact, because a lane citation is true when written and can be false when read** — and the same round found `sourcing/check.ts` and `pack-validation.ts` had moved since the cross-review's commit, which is why its §5 numbers were re-run rather than re-read | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by pack-graduation 0.27 |
| D233 🐞 | **The selection measurements fixed at the public event projection still cross the wire earlier through direct selection responses.** `/select-move`, `/runs/:id/prediction`, and `/runs/:id/group-reply` return `OpponentSelection` before disclosure; the browser must then echo ordinary/group selections into `/moves`, which is how the same full object becomes durable. Redacting those responses would also delete the RFC's required production record. Closing this honestly needs a server-owned selection/opaque-receipt contract (or an equivalent atomic select-and-commit route), not another projection filter invented during a defect patch | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 while implementing D194; requires an RFC/API lane |
| D183 🐞 | **`projectRun` enforces adjacency invariants over the run event stream, so a new `DrillRunEvent` member is only safe if its emission instant is controlled — and a user gesture's is not.** Three checks constrain what may sit *immediately* next to what: `outcome.reached` must directly follow its `move.committed` on the same node (`events.ts`, *"must immediately follow its move.committed"*), `segment.completed` must directly follow its ending checkpoint (`segmentFromEvent`'s `events[event.seq - 2] !== end` guard), and every opponent `move.committed` must be directly preceded by an authoritative `opponent.move_selected` (`opponentMovesFromEvents`, `replay.ts`). Because `RunStorage.read` rebuilds every run through `readBackReplay`, an event interleaved at the wrong instant does not degrade the run — it makes `projectRun` throw, and **the run becomes permanently unloadable**. Generalizes well past the RFC that found it: *"just add an event type"* is not a cheap move, and any proposal to add one must name **when its event can fire** and prove that instant is controlled. Compounding costs: `appendEvents` re-runs `projectRun` over the whole log on every append, and `RunStorage.save` rewrites the entire `snapshot_json`, so a high-frequency event is also a per-emission full replay | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/archive/board-annotation.md` |
| D184 ✅ | **Move authorship now reaches the granted live-session viewer.** `LiveSessionService.detail` projects the already-specified `deriveMoveAuthorship` result from the durable run and possession journal. The browser renders each committed move with the current granted handle, or honestly labels an identity that no longer resolves as `former member`. Server and real two-learner browser regressions pin White/Black attribution without adding persistence or exposing it outside the grant-scoped session detail. *Original:* `deriveMoveAuthorship` was specified, implemented, exported and tested, but no REST route served it and no component rendered it. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the D184 live-attribution batch |
| D185 ✅ | **Live-session kind has one shared declaration.** `LIVE_SESSION_KINDS` and `LiveSessionKind` now live in the runtime package; the server aliases its validator/DDL source to that constant and the browser API aliases its public type to the same union. Widening only one package no longer compiles cleanly, and an exact vocabulary test pins the three shipped members. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the shared live-session vocabulary fix |
| D186 ✅ | **The shipped PGN exporter now uses chessops' `%csl`/`%cal` codec for board marks.** The persisted brush vocabulary is closed at chessops' four exportable colours, `makeComment` writes one batched shape comment per position, and round-trip tests pin circles and arrows. Import remains deliberately deferred because imported mark authorship is unresolved. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by `board-annotation` |
| D187 ✅ | **Board-local learner state survives the deliberate `Chessboard.svelte` remount.** `DrillScreen.svelte` owns the fetched mark set, debounce, scope and persistence callbacks, and passes only the active projection into each keyed board instance. The remount and its stale-bounds fix remain intact. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by `board-annotation` |
| D188 💡 | **PGN export filters learner marks, but future per-author or per-viewer fields still have no general export contract.** Any spectator, any granted teacher, anyone holding a `session_join`-minted grant can `GET /runs/:id/pgn`. Today that is correct and not a defect: the PGN contains moves, branch labels and headers, all of which the same principal can already read through the run projection. **The standing rule the observation produces is the point** — any per-author or per-viewer content added to the PGN must carry an explicit principal filter, because the route's gate will not supply one, `exportPgn`'s signature suggests nothing of the kind, and a leak through an export is invisible to every test that reasons about the run projection. Sibling of the disclosure-surface class that `run_grants`' nine-reader enumeration exists to police, one artifact over. `rfc/archive/board-annotation.md` §3.2 is the first case and closes it by construction (own marks only, with the withheld count stated in a `TabiyaMarks` header rather than silently omitted) | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by `rfc/archive/board-annotation.md` |
| D213 ✅ | **The run event log's adjacency invariants are held open only by every emitter's synchronous bracketing, and nothing asserts it.** Amends [[D183]], which recorded the invariants correctly and their consequence wrongly. Measured at `a7e700d`: `commitMove` pushes `move.committed` and `outcome.reached` **in one `appendEvents` drafts array** (`runtime.ts:346-351`), `reachCheckpoint` emits its checkpoint then its segment in two back-to-back calls (`:454`, `:461-473`), and `commitMove` deliberately emits any `branch.forked` *before* the opponent selection (`:298-322`) — so all three gaps are empty by construction and **no existing event type is tolerated in any of them**. Two corrections follow. (1) *"The run becomes permanently unloadable"* is wrong for the normal path: `appendEvents` re-runs `projectRun` over the whole log **before** anything persists (`events.ts:377-385`), so a bad-adjacency event throws at the append and never reaches `snapshot_json`; the unloadable state is reachable only from a hand-written or migrated log. (2) The real hazard is subtler and is not written down anywhere: the three gaps stay closed **only because every emitter happens to bracket them inside one synchronous call**, a property no test asserts, no type enforces and any future `await` between two `appendEvents` calls silently breaks. A user-gesture-timed event would make that accidental bracketing load-bearing forever, and its failure mode is a `TypeError` surfacing as a 500 that discards the learner's gesture. Also weakening the read-time framing: `RunStorage` serves a live run from `#snapshots` without replaying, and `listRuns` reads `summary_json` only | ✅ closed by the runtime-invariant batch — one real checkmating run now exercises and pins selection→commit, checkpoint→segment, and commit→outcome adjacency |
| D214 🐞 | **`EvidencePacket.sentences` is the `voiceCheck` allowlist, which makes it a law-8 boundary rather than a projection — and `rest.ts` already splices derived sentences into it at two sites.** `voiceCheck` (`packages/runtime/src/voice.ts:32-33`) builds `source = packet.sentences.join("\n")` and then permits **any** square, UCI, SAN token, chess noun, judgement word or prescriptive verb in the model's output that appears in that string. So whatever reaches `sentences` defines what an LLM is licensed to assert about the position. `rest.ts:1178` and `:1191` already do `Object.freeze({ ...basePacket, sentences: [...basePacket.sentences, suggestTitle(story), ...] })` — the precedent for appending arbitrary derived text at the REST layer is established — and `rest.ts:1168` replaces `sentences` wholesale with comparison narrative. The general rule: **any feature adding learner- or author-controlled text near the packet must be checked against `sentences`, not against `DrillRun`**, because a `DrillRun`-shaped isolation argument cannot see this door. Board annotation is the first case (one line — *"the voice should acknowledge what the learner circled"* — would put learner-chosen squares into the allowlist and let the model say `e5` about a position where nothing validated mentions `e5`), and a byte-identity test over the un-plumbed state cannot detect it | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` cross-review |
| D215 🐞 | **`Cursor.branchId` is history-dependent, so any key built from it is a function of the visit rather than of the tree.** `rewind` (`packages/runtime/src/runtime.ts:398-400`) selects `isAncestor(run, nodeId, run.activeCursor.nodeId) ? run.activeCursor.branchId : target.branchId` — rewinding to an **ancestor** keeps the current branch id; rewinding anywhere else adopts the target node's. `Node.branchId` is fixed per node and `Cursor.branchId` is not, so the same node in the same tree presents different `(branchId, nodeId)` pairs on different visits: fork from N to B′, then rewind to N, and the cursor reads `B′:N`, not `B:N`, until a rewind lands off the current path. Nothing is wrong with `rewind` — the behaviour is what makes "the fork clears the board" work at the fork node itself, which `Node.branchId` could not deliver — but it means **branch-keyed persistence is conditionally invisible**, and no existing consumer had noticed because nothing else persists against the cursor's branch id. Recorded for every future feature keyed on "the line I am on" | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` cross-review |
| D216 🐞 | **A `story_read` token passes `requireRead` as the token's *creator*, through a fabricated principal — the scoping is a hand-narrowed projection, not an authorization scope.** `Principal` (`authorization.ts:12-15`) is `{learnerId, handle}` and has **no token variant**, so no anonymous caller can be represented. `RunService.publicStory` (`service.ts:570`) therefore resolves the token hash, loads `learnerById(record.createdBy)`, **builds a principal out of the sharing host**, and calls `this.story(...)` — which passes `requireRead` on the host's own grant. What limits the anonymous reader is the hand-written narrowing that follows (`moments.slice(0, 8)`, five fields per moment), not the authorization layer. `session_join` is the safe sibling: it is `authenticate()`-first and *materializes* a real `run_grants` row on redemption (`storage.ts:1771`), so the joiner is a genuine principal thereafter. The standing rule: **any new route must take its principal from `authenticate()` and never inherit one from a token path**, or an anonymous link holder acts as the host on a surface nobody scoped for them. Sibling hazard on the same page: `RunService.pgn`'s optional-principal overload (`service.ts:1430-1432`) falls back to the auto-created `__legacy` learner, which also passes `requireRead` — unreachable from REST today (`rest.ts:1035`), in-process only | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` cross-review |
| D217 🐞 | **`exportPackRunPgn` exports a *synthesized* run with regenerated ids, so anything keyed on a run's node or branch ids silently vanishes on the pack export path.** `RunService.pgn` sends every pack session to `exportPackRunPgn` (`service.ts:1451`) — the **default** arm — which does not export the run: it returns `exportPgn(combinedRun(pack, run, branchIds))` (`packages/runtime/src/pack-pgn.ts:205`), and `combinedRun` (`:122-152`) builds a fresh `DrillRun` through `createRun`, splicing the authored spine together with the played paths. Node ids, branch ids and the branch list are all regenerated, and the final `exportPgn` call is passed **no `branchIds`** at all. FEN-derived keys (`transposeKey`) survive because the positions replay identically; id-derived keys cannot match anything. The first `exportPgn(run, branchIds)` at `:204` is a legality validation whose output is discarded, so a feature that plumbs only "the `exportPgn` call" has a 50% chance of plumbing the wrong one. Recorded because the asymmetry is invisible from `exportPgn`'s signature and from `RunService.pgn`'s three arms | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` cross-review |
| D218 ✅ | **Filtered PGN export states the filter without measuring another author.** Every export carries `TabiyaMarks: own (N); other authors' marks are not exported`; the second clause is constant regardless of whether another principal has zero or hundreds of marks. Match-seat regressions pin the absence of the live side channel. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by `board-annotation` |
| D219 ✅ | **A skipped migration number is a one-way door, not a gap waiting to be filled.** The migration loop is `for (const migration of migrations) { if (migration.version <= version) continue; … }` (`apps/server/src/storage.ts:2197-2199`). The forward direction is fine — applying 23 to a database at 21 runs cleanly and sets `user_version = 23` — and it is the half that gets checked. The reverse direction is fatal and is the half that does not: **a database that has reached 23 will skip a later-landing 22 forever**, silently, with no error at open (`#migrate` throws only on `version > STORAGE_VERSION`, the opposite direction). So a draft that claims a number past an unlanded sibling's does not defer the sibling — it strands the sibling's tables on every existing database while fresh ones get them, which is worse than a collision because a collision is loud. The register's own practice already refuses holes and every one of its 22 rows is contiguous: migration 16 was *"rebased from an initial 15 claim"* and 21/22 were **reassigned to each other on 2026-08-16** at a cost its own row calls *"nothing but text"*. **Rule: a draft claims the next contiguous number at implementation time and reassigns a blocked sibling upward; it never leaves a hole.** Cheap guard: assert the migration array's versions are exactly `1..STORAGE_VERSION` | ✅ closed by the runtime-invariant batch — storage startup refuses any migration list other than the exact ordered range 1..STORAGE_VERSION, with missing, duplicate and reordered regressions |
| D247 🐞 | **`combinedRun` preserves exactly one identity across the pack-export boundary — the move path — so any key that must survive it is keyed on the UCI sequence, never on ids.** The remedy half of [[D217]], measured at `7650d41`. `exportPackRunPgn` ends `exportPgn(combinedRun(pack, run, branchIds))`; `combinedRun` reduces the authored spine and the played branches to `CombinedPath.moves` (bare UCI arrays), dedupes them by their joined UCI string in `uniquePaths`, and replays them through `createRun` + `commitMove`, forking at `sharedPrefix` — which itself locates a node by walking children whose `moveUci` matches. Node ids, branch ids, seqs and cursors are all regenerated; **the root-relative UCI path is preserved exactly and is unique within a tree**, so it is the only usable address. Two consequences nothing else records: `uniquePaths` collapses two branches with identical move sequences into one, so a remap is many-to-one and must dedupe; and the combined tree contains **authored nodes the run never reached**, so anything keyed on `transposeKey` can surface at a position the run never visited. First applied by `rfc/archive/board-annotation.md` §3.1 | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` author round |
| D248 🐞 | **A guard test that runs below the layer its barrier is breached at cannot fail, and a guard never shown to go red is not evidence.** Both halves are demonstrated inside one RFC. (1) `board-annotation`'s criterion 2 asserts byte-identical `evidencePacket` output with and without marks — but the hazard it is cited against is a **`rest.ts` sentence splice**, which sits *above* `evidencePacket` and outside everything the criterion calls, so the test is sound and structurally blind. (2) Its proposed replacement grepped six pinned line numbers, four of which name assembly sites and two splice sites, while the natural violation is a **seventh** splice line the enumeration cannot see — and the numbers had drifted within a day. **Rule: a barrier criterion runs at the layer the barrier can be breached at, is matched by pattern rather than by line, and carries a positive control proving the assertion can report a planted violation.** Cheap, and it is the difference between a guard and a decoration | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` author round |
| D249 ✅ | **The checkpoint/segment adjacency gap remains synchronously bracketed and is now source-pinned.** A regression extracts `reachCheckpoint`, proves its repeated `appendEvents` calls have no intervening `await`, and includes a planted positive control that the predicate rejects. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by `board-annotation` |
| D250 🐞 | **A draft that writes a shared monotonic integer into its body has written a claim with an expiry date.** Generalizes [[D219]] past migrations to every version constant, and the evidence is one RFC's own history: `board-annotation` was drafted against `STORAGE_VERSION` 20 / pack `0.22`, cross-reviewed against 21 / `0.23`, and by its author round the pack constant already read **`0.24`** in the working tree with `0.25` and `0.26` claimed ahead of any date it could land. Each move made a previously-true sentence false, and each correction cost a review round. `rfc/README.md`'s landing-order rule fixes the migration case — claim a *position*, resolve `STORAGE_VERSION + 1` at landing — and the same discipline generalizes: **state what a document does not move, never what a number currently is.** *"Stays at 0.23"* expires; *"changes no pack document"* does not | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` author round |
| D251 💡 | **Ids in this runtime are path-shaped — `` `${run.id}:node:${n}` `` and `` `${run.id}:branch:${n}` `` (`createRun`, `commitMove`) — so any key built by concatenating two of them embeds the run id twice.** Measured while pricing `board-annotation`'s mark bound: a `` `${branchId}:${nodeId}` `` composite is **98 characters** against a four-field `transposeKey`'s 52, making a branch-scoped record **202 bytes** of JSON against a FEN-keyed one's 158 — the reverse of the assumption that an id key is cheaper than a position key. At 1,000 records that is 198.2 KiB against 155.3 KiB [V]. Nothing is broken; it prices every future composite key, and it means *"key it by id"* is the expensive option in this codebase, not the cheap one | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` author round |
| D252 🐞 | **`kind === "match"` and `boardControl === "match"` are not interchangeable, and the divergent case ships.** `live-session.ts` refuses a `match` board control on any other session kind, so `boardControl === "match"` implies `kind === "match"` — but **not** the reverse: an **imported-Arena** session is `kind: "match"` on a non-`match` board, which is exactly the combination `storage.ts` detects when it seeds `arena_legs`. Eight sites read `kind === "match"` for behaviour (four server, four web) and seven read `boardControl === "match"`, and the two sets are not aliases of each other. Any future rule phrased *"not during a contest"* must test **`kind`**: a `boardControl` test silently exempts every imported-Arena game, which is a whole session type of real contests. First bites `rfc/archive/board-annotation.md` §4.2's relay refusal | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the `board-annotation` author round |
| D234 🐞 | **"Packet membership is a speaking licence" is true of chess TOKENS only, and claude told the owner otherwise.** `voiceCheck` (`packages/runtime/src/voice.ts`) compares exactly three things against the packet: square/UCI/SAN tokens, and membership in three frozen word lists — `CHESS_LEXICON` (18 words), `BANNED_JUDGEMENTS` (19), `PRESCRIPTIVE_VERBS` (24). **Prose drawn from none of those vocabularies is unconstrained.** The `evidence-at-runtime` cross-review *executed* it: against a packet whose only sentence is *"This pack declares: opening."*, `voiceCheck` returns `valid: true` for *"No reading was recorded at this position."*, *"The engine is silent here."*, and *"No measurement exists here; elsewhere on this line there was one."* — the last being the exact cross-node inference that RFC exists to forbid. A wholly invented recorded-reading sentence passes too. **Claude advised the owner (D116) that widening the packet is "law-8-legal by construction because `voiceCheck` still binds every sentence to the packet".** That is overstated: it binds the chess vocabulary, not the proposition. The advice's *direction* survives — widening the packet is still strictly safer than widening the model's licence — but it is not a construction-proof, and any RFC that leans on it needs its own constraint. **This is the SECOND instrument tonight found to guarantee tokens while being described as guaranteeing propositions**; the other is `claim-backing`-s residual sweep (D131). Same failure of description, two independent mechanisms, both claude-s | 💡 open — **RE-EXECUTED AT HEAD (D400) and worse than when filed.** `voiceCheck` still returns `valid: true, violations: []` for all three original sentences — **and now also for a wholly invented recorded-reading sentence in `renderRecordedReading`'s exact frozen format** `[V]`. Since [[D118]] the packet carries genuine readings of that shape, **so a fabricated one is now indistinguishable in form from a real one.** One figure stale: `BANNED_JUDGEMENTS` is **30** words, not 19 — `3e6fe2e` added eleven flattery terms after this row was written |
| D235 ✅ | **Undisclosed selection candidates are projected by allow-list.** `publicSelectionEvent` now constructs the five permitted structural/policy fields explicitly; score, WDL, and any future measurement-shaped field are absent unless disclosure opens. A regression injects a future field and proves it stays private while the opponent move remains playable. Maia `mass` remains deliberately public pending its separately ledgered permission decision. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the projection allow-list fix |
| D236 💡 | **Maia candidate WDL sums to exactly 1000 — the encoding is per-mille, measured rather than assumed.** `engine-leverage` criterion 21a existed to *report* observed sums so a later RFC could pin the encoding without guessing, and the real-Maia integration run at `b65bd4e` reports them **consistently 1000** across three passing tests. That is the criterion doing precisely its job: it produced a fact nobody had, at the cost of one assertion. **Whoever specifies a WDL consumer may now write the divisor as a measured constant with a citation**, instead of inferring it from one sample or hard-coding 1000 and hoping. Recorded here because the number lives only in a test report otherwise, and the next author will not re-run a Maia container to rediscover it | 💡 open, measured 2026-08-16 |
| D327 💡 | **Variants in Just Play, not only in packs — owner idea 2026-08-16. The instrument stack degrades in TIERS, and that is the useful frame.** *"what if I want in just play all these variations as well? if we're going to add packs with ie fischer random or all these other variations."* Grounded first pass, to be extended by whoever takes it: **13 files in `packages/runtime/src` import chessops**, and the single coupling point is `positionFromFen` → `Chess.fromSetup(parseFen(fen).unwrap())`. A `variant` column **already exists** in storage (`storage.ts`). `pgn-import.ts` explicitly refuses anything but `Standard`/`From Position`. **Tier 1 — Chess960**: FEN-expressible (X-FEN/Shredder), same pieces and rules, so structural reading, phase, endgame reading and **tablebase all stay valid**; the explorer carries 960 data; **Maia does not** — it is trained on standard chess, so rung 3 goes dark. Note the connection to **D193**: `UCI_Chess960` is one of the eight advertised Stockfish options with no disposition row, so the engine half is *already* partly surfaced. **Tier 2 — rule-changing variants** (atomic, three-check, crazyhouse, racing kings): each detector-s assumptions break differently and per-variant, so this is not one decision. **Tier 3 — see [[D328]].** The product already owns the vocabulary for this shape: `design/06` §2a-s *difficulty-availability* label (measured / authored / neither) is the same idea one axis over — **a variant should declare which rungs survive it**, rather than the product pretending the ladder is intact | 💡 open, owner idea 2026-08-16 |
| D328 💡 | **Westernised Xiangqi and Shogi — owner idea 2026-08-16, explicitly accepting degraded support.** *"what about a (westernized) xiangqi and shogi? those would be nice — even if we cannot offer most of the support mechanics — it might be nice to play against bot/human these 'foreign chess' variants."* The honest cost, stated so nobody discovers it late: **nothing in the detector stack survives** — different board geometry (9×10 xiangqi, 9×9 shogi), different pieces, different rules; chessops cannot represent either; no tablebase, no Maia, no explorer, no structural reading, no phase classification. **The question is therefore not "which detectors degrade" but "how coupled is the SHELL".** The branch runtime — rewind, fork, compare, checkpoints, the event log — is the actual differentiator and is in principle game-agnostic, but `Node.fen` and `transposeKey` are FEN-shaped today. **Both games have FEN-like notations** (SFEN for shogi; xiangqi FEN is a standard extension), so the coupling may sit at `positionFromFen` rather than in the runtime-s own types — **that is the measurement that decides whether this is a weekend or a second product**, and it is cheap to take. The owner has already accepted the degraded-support trade, so the open question is engineering shape, not desirability | 💡 open, owner idea 2026-08-16 |
| D329 💡 | **Packs are typed on TWO axes, not one — and a famous-game pack is expressible as prose but not as data.** Owner question 2026-08-16: *"are they all opening vs midgame vs endgame? cause what if we want packs based off of famous previous games?"* Measured: a pack declares **`phase`** (`opening | middlegame | endgame | cross_phase`) **and `mode`** (`line | plan | outcome | trajectory`), so *what kind of rehearsal* is already a separate axis from *what part of the game*. **What is missing is a third: PROVENANCE OF THE POSITION.** `$defs/provenance` requires only `reviewStatus` and offers `sources` as a bare `string[]`; there is **no `sourceGame`**, so a pack can say *"from Kasparov–Topalov, Wijk aan Zee 1999"* in free text and **nothing can read it** — no player, event, date, or move number is machine-addressable. Consequences: no surface can index or filter by game, no *"more from this game"*, no *"you have now seen three positions from this match"*, and the campaign cannot use a famous game as a run-level theme. **This is the cheapest of the three rows here** — the format already carries `variantOf` for pack-to-pack relations, so the pattern exists. Note it also gives authored prose unusually strong provenance: annotated master games are the oldest chess pedagogy there is, and a cited game is a rung-5 claim whose source is checkable in a way *"authored consensus"* never is | 💡 open, owner idea 2026-08-16 |
| D330 💡 | **There is no time control in this product at all, and `clockState` is a passthrough nothing reads.** Owner idea 2026-08-16: *"what if we want to simulate the time pressure of a GREAT move during 10+0 chess and then give actual time?"* Measured: `clockState` is typed `Record<string, unknown>` on both `DrillRun` and the start options, is set from `rest.ts`, is persisted — and **has zero readers**. No clock, no increment, no per-move budget, no display, no expiry. Separately and importantly, **`clock_zeroed` is the HALFMOVE clock** — a fifty-move-rule counter — and **`timingWindows` is MOVE-COUNT tempo, not clock tempo**; both names invite the conflation and neither is a clock. So the owner-s proposal has **no mechanism whatsoever** today, and the honest first question is not *how do we build a clock* but *what is the smallest thing that produces the felt pressure* — a countdown that ends the encounter, or a soft budget that changes what assistance can be consumed. See [[D331]], which is why this is worth more than it looks | 💡 open, owner idea 2026-08-16 |
| D331 💡 | **Time is a difficulty axis that constrains the learner WITHOUT touching the power curve — and it may be the missing lever.** `design/06` §5 rules that **what escalates is LEGIBILITY, not power**, and that the power curve is flat by construction. Every difficulty lever found so far is therefore either opponent band (measured, `[1000,2400]`), decidability tier, encounter horizon, or capability suppression. **A clock is a fifth, and it is different in kind: it constrains the learner-s own computation rather than adding or removing anything the product gives them.** It also interlocks with the coaching-versus-cheating criterion landed 2026-08-16 (*distance from the answer*): **a hint you do not have time to read is not a hint**, so time pressure degrades assistance value continuously without any of it being withheld — which is exactly the *"balance cheating to win VS coaching to win"* problem the owner posed, solved by a mechanism that touches neither honesty policy nor inventory. It is also the first candidate that makes *"you-d need proper 2000 Elo skills"* a claim about the **learner** rather than about the encounter-s configuration, which the coaching dossier established the product otherwise cannot make. **Speculative and unmeasured — recorded as the reason D330 is worth more than a missing field** **REFUTED 2026-08-16, and in the direction that matters — this row-s central claim was claude-s and it was wrong.** The interlock hypothesis was *"a hint you do not have time to read is not a hint"*, so time would degrade assistance value continuously and preferentially suppress items nearest the answer. Measured over **43,272 rendered items** from shipped renderers: distance class explains **η² = 0.201** of the variance in reading length, and **0.038** once the refused `move` class is excluded — while **which renderer printed it explains 0.984**. ρ(distance rank, words) is **−0.046**, the order of the ρ = −0.143 this repo already treats as no relationship. **And the sign is inverted at the end that matters: `move` is the CHEAPEST class the product could ever print** — median **1 word / 0.3 s** against `ranking`-s **63 / 15.9 s**. So a clock is a gradient *toward* the answer: under time pressure a learner reaches for the move precisely because it reads fastest, which makes time a **cheating amplifier** rather than the balance mechanism this row proposed. It would also make difficulty a function of copy length, incentivising the compression of evidence into verdicts. **What survives is the by-product, and it is worth more than the hypothesis was:** at a 10+0 budget of 15.0 s/move ≈ 60 words, **≈6.6 `fact` items or 0.94 `ranking` items fit** — the slot number D78 licensed and never supplied, arrived at independently and **with no clock existing**. `design/research/time-as-a-difficulty-lever.md` | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D415 ✅ | **Evidence recovery raced an older evidence page against an explicit analysis enqueue.** Coalescing overlapping timer ticks was valid hardening but not the root cause: the next full gate reproduced the exact two-leaf timeout. Its retained trace showed an evidence GET already in flight when `/analysis` enqueued the missing leaves; applying the older page then recomputed `pendingEvidence` from the run log, overwrote the explicit enqueue count, and canceled polling before the new results were read. Analysis enqueue and evidence-page application now serialize in both directions, with a deterministic regression for this exact ordering. The exact browser scenario passed 10/10 isolated zero-retry repetitions and the following full suite. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by serializing analysis enqueue with evidence-page application **Renumbered D332 → D415 on 2026-08-16:** this id was used three times for two different items — the evidence-recovery flake (this row and its work item) and the owner's learner-Elo ruling. **Every downstream citation in `rfc/learner-rating.md`, `rfc/opponent-contracts.md` and `planning/work-register.md` means the Elo row**, so that keeps D332 and the closed flake moved. Manifestation of the id-block race ledgered the same hour |
| D345 🐞 | **The `plyHorizon` act ramp is a restatement of authored spine length, and no pack has ever used the field that would make it anything else.** Measured at HEAD over the 47 packs in `content/drafts`: `plyHorizon` equals the **deepest authored spine ply exactly** in **19 of 20** opening packs and **10 of 11** middlegame packs `[V]`. Endgame packs are the only family with real slack (12 of 14 positive, median +7). So `design/06` §5's *"median 12 ply"* and the 11 / 8 / 24 per-act medians describe how much spine an author typed, not how long an encounter is licensed to run. And **`authoredBoundary.fenPredicates` has 0 uses across all 47 packs** — the one mechanism that can grant authored territory off the move list, and the one `design/04` §0a names as transferable. Consequence for the brief that raised this: *"should Act II's horizon be longer"* has no answer in the number, because raising the cap alone grants nothing | 🐞 open, measured 2026-08-16 by the 1400–1799 band wave |
| D346 ✅ | **The Carlsbad exemplar's authored boundary now includes its objective-producing node.** `plyHorizon` moved from 8 to 11, equal to the deepest authored spine ply and the `bxc6-recapture` node where the structural objective is achieved. A pack-level regression asserts the objective transition cannot occur beyond the declared horizon. This deliberately does not impose the same rule on trajectory roots, whose leg boundaries make deeper spines a different contract. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the exemplar boundary correction |
| D347 ✅ | **`plan_signature` now resolves at every server-side structural-expression boundary.** The first three content users exposed that objective compilation expanded the reference while checkpoint triggers, authored boundaries, timing-window position clauses, structural key-point grounds, and the expression census evaluated raw expressions. Those consumers now share the registry expander; the registry-free runtime still refuses unresolved raw references. Regressions cover checkpoint/boundary validation and the corpus census. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the vocabulary-wiring follow-up |
| D348 🐞 | **No expression leaf names a shape entry's TRIGGER, so every structure-keyed predicate in a pack is a hand copy of the library.** `plan_signature` resolves a **plan class**; the only trigger-shaped leaf is `named_structure`, whose id vocabulary is closed at `carlsbad`, `iqp-white`, `iqp-black` and `maroczy-bind`. **21 of the 25 shipped shape entries cannot be referred to by name in any expression** — including all three this wave used (`london-wedge`, `open-centre`, `closed-centre-chain`). A pack that wants *"authored while this structure is still on the board"* must duplicate the entry's trigger into its own file, and nothing checks the copy against the entry, so the library and its customer can silently disagree. This is precisely the re-authoring `design/04` §0a warns about, arriving through a field rather than through prose. Cheapest fix is a `shape_trigger` leaf taking a shape id, mirroring `plan_signature` | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the 1400–1799 band wave |
| D349 🐞 | **The band's evidence for a middlegame plan dies within six to eight plies of the tabiya, and that is WHY Act II spines are short.** Measured live on three independent lines at `ratings=1400,1600,1800`, `speeds=rapid,classical`, `2023-01..2025-12`: the London main line has **139 games at ply 6 and 7 at ply 7**; the Exchange French has **141 at ply 4 and 18 at ply 5**; the French Advance reaches **3816 at ply 8** — and its exception is itself measured, six of its eight moves being recaptures played at 72–98%, so depth there is bought by **forcing**, not by popularity. Middlegame tabiyas in this corpus start at ply 13–21 from the game start, so 6–8 more plies lands at 19–29 — the same place `design/06` §2a's inherited *"human outcome data dies at ply ~20"* lands. **The two numbers are the same number**, and together they answer [[D345]]'s question: a longer Act II cannot be bought with more corpus-backed spine, only with territory that is not spine | 🐞 open, measured 2026-08-16 by the 1400–1799 band wave |
| D350 💡 | **The one timing measurement the explorer CAN produce is availability, not outcome — and it is worth naming as a technique.** In `open-centre-french-exchange-black`, `...Re8` — the objective's own move — occurs **339 of 2695** at the root and **48 of 822** six plies later on the same pack's other branch, i.e. above the 100-game floor at one depth and below it at the other. That is a statement about *what the corpus can be asked*, not about which timing is better, so it survives the [[D126]] boundary intact while still being about time. It is not a substitute for [[D155]]'s per-game traversal and does not pretend to be; it is the shape a timing claim can take **today**, and this is the sixth wave to arrive at that missing instrument from a new direction | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 by the 1400–1799 band wave |
| D351 🐞 | **A pack's most concrete-looking claim was wrong in both directions until the evaluator was run — attack counts in prose must be computed, and no target computes them.** The first draft of `closed-centre-chain-black-base-strike` asserted that d4 is attacked **three** times (c5 pawn, c6 knight, b6 queen) and defended **two** (c3 pawn, f3 knight). The repo's own `direct_attack_count` says **two against three**: the b6 queen is blocked by **Black's own c5 pawn** on the b6–d4 diagonal, and the **White queen on d1** defends d4. Both halves wrong, from a diagram any reader would read the same way, in the one kind of sentence that looks least like a chess opinion. Sibling of [[D110]], [[D161]] and [[D119]] — a plausible cardinal that no instrument had been pointed at — and the remedy is the same shape as D119's: **a count is a property of what the evaluator returns and may only be asserted after running it**. Nothing in the `Makefile` computes an attacker/defender count; both packs in this wave that needed one used a throwaway bundle | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by the 1400–1799 band wave |
| D352 💡 | **Proposal, shipped in three packs: authored territory should follow the STRUCTURE, not the move list.** Each of the wave's three packs declares `plyHorizon` **above** its deepest authored spine node (16 / 16 / 18 against spines of 6 / 6 / 8) and grants the extra territory through `authoredBoundary.fenPredicates` — the shape entry's trigger, plus the objective plan's signature where one exists. These are the **first three uses of `fenPredicates` in the corpus** ([[D345]]). The argument is that a shape-library entry is authored guidance valid wherever its trigger fires, so authored territory keyed to that trigger is honest in a way that territory keyed to how far the author typed is not — and it is the only way to lengthen Act II that [[D349]] does not refute. Unmeasured and stated as such in all three packs: whether 16/18 is the right cap, and whether a structure-keyed grant is better for a learner than a move-keyed one. Owner or a later wave should rule; until then it is three packs' worth of proposal | 💡 open, proposed 2026-08-16 by the 1400–1799 band wave |
| D353 ✅ | **The expression census resolves pack-local `plan_signature` references before coverage and satisfiability measurement.** The three new pack subjects now have zero evaluation faults and are each `satisfiable`; the aggregate `satisfiabilityUnknown` count returned from **26 to 23** without changing the 192-subject corpus. Truly unresolved references remain raw so the census reports their evaluation fault rather than crashing. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 with D347 |
| D354 💡 | **The 1400–1799 opponent band is now 5 packs instead of 2, and the step function survives — the rest is a re-band, not more authoring.** Re-measured at HEAD with the same script shape that found the hole: `targetElo` **< 1400: 6 · 1400–1799: 5 · ≥ 1800: 37 · none: 2**, over 50 packs. The three added are all middlegame — `design/06` §5's **authored** act, the thinnest by decidability — all three put the learner in the **received** structure, the one the opponent chose, which applies `design/04` §2c's anti-opening whitespace to Act II for the first time, and all three are the **first customer for a shape entry's unserved chair**: `london-wedge`'s five Black plans, `open-centre`'s two White plans and `closed-centre-chain`'s two Black plans had **zero middlegame customers between them** before this wave. What the wave did **not** fix: 37 of 50 packs still declare `targetElo` ≥ 1800, which is a claim about opponent strength that most of those packs' own `difficulty` bands (1400–2000) do not support. Closing the curve properly means **re-banding existing packs**, which is an owner call about what a declared opponent Elo means, not another authoring round | 💡 open, measured 2026-08-16 by the 1400–1799 band wave |
| D355 💡 | **A game clock is NOT the refused pursuit clock — the discriminator is whether the budget survives a rewind, and it is one field's altitude.** `design/06` §5 refuses *"a pursuit clock is a retry price by another name"*, and §2c's own decomposition says only **how often** you may retry conflicts with `00` §76's *"experimentation without cost"*. Tested: a **run-pooled** clock makes the *k*-th retry cost more than the first — it is a rewind budget with a real-valued counter and the refusal is exactly right. An **attempt-scoped** clock that resets at the fork prices *deliberation inside one attempt* and touches none of §2c's three separable ideas. **The shipped placeholder already picked the safe altitude** `[V]`: `clockState` is declared on **`Node`** (`packages/runtime/src/types.ts:111`, alongside `branchId` at `:107`; mirrored at `schemas/drill_run.schema.json:265`) and written only from `CommitMoveOptions.clockState` (`runtime.ts:57`, applied `:341`) — nodes are path- and branch-scoped, so a clock built on the reserved field **rewinds with the board automatically**, and the refused version needs a **new run-level field**. That makes the ruling checkable in a diff rather than in prose. Two counter-arguments given in the dossier: a resetting clock has no teeth unless flag-fall rides `06` §5's *submitted-branch* verb (it can, and needs no new refusal class); and `06` §5's power/legibility dichotomy has **no slot** for a device that shrinks the learner's own capacity. `design/research/time-as-a-difficulty-lever.md` §2 | 💡 open, answered 2026-08-16, owner ruling pending — see [[D364]] |
| D356 💡 | **Reading cost does NOT track distance to the answer — the interlock hypothesis is refuted, and the number is not close.** D331 proposed that time pressure would degrade assistance *preferentially near the answer*, solving coaching-vs-cheating without touching honesty policy or inventory. Measured over **43,272 rendered items** from the shipped renderers (47 packs / 721 transitions / 609 positions, 25 shape entries, 43 real cached explorer responses, 105 recorded Maia candidate counts) `[V]`: variance in log(words) explained by **distance class** is **η² = 0.201** over four classes and **0.038** over the three the product may ship, while variance explained by **which renderer printed it** is **η² = 0.984**. Spearman ρ(distance rank, words) = **−0.046** over all items, **+0.125** excluding `move` — the same order as the ρ = −0.143 this repo already treats as *no relationship*. **Reading cost is a property of renderer verbosity, not of distance from the answer.** The within-class spread swamps the between-class difference: `kind` runs **6 words** (phase reading) to **298** (full shape panel) — a **50×** range; `ranking` runs **12** (the `human_divergence` marker) to **230** (the corpus page) — **19×**. So a clock would make an encounter's difficulty a function of **copy length**, and its standing incentive would be to compress evidence into verdicts — a move *down* the distance axis, i.e. toward the eval-bar trainer `00-thesis.md` exists to replace. The one point for the other side, stated: excluding `move`, the class medians do order correctly (6 < 9 < 63) — but η² = 0.038, and the effect is carried almost entirely by **one family**, the corpus page | 💡 open, measured 2026-08-16, `time-as-a-difficulty-lever.md` §3, `tools/d355-reading-cost-harness/` |
| D357 💡 | **The answer is the cheapest thing this product could ever print, so a clock is a gradient TOWARD cheating.** Measured per-item medians `[V]`: `move` **1 word / 0.3 s**, `kind` **6 / 1.5 s**, `fact` **9 / 2.3 s**, `ranking` **63 / 15.9 s**. Inside one 10+0 move budget (15 s) a learner can read the answer **50 times over**, or **0.94** of one human-model split. Under time pressure the rational learner reads the shortest item that resolves the most of the live decision — which is precisely the item `coaching-versus-cheating-and-the-band-curve.md` calls cheating. **This interlocks directly with that dossier's open owner question:** under its option (a) `move` stays refused and a clock weakly favours the honest end (weakly — η² = 0.038); under option (c) `move` becomes purchasable pre-commit and **a clock becomes a cheating amplifier**. So the clock ruling should not be taken before the hint-ladder ruling | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16, blocks on the D317–D326 owner question |
| D358 💡 | **The 10+0 arithmetic: the shipped all-on reading costs 16.4× a whole move budget, and it derives the slot number D78 could not.** 600 s over a **40-move reference game** — *Lichess's own* classifier convention (`initial + 40 × increment`), so the denominator is the platform's, not ours — is **15.0 s per learner move**, which at **238 wpm** (Brysbaert 2019 meta-analysis, 190 studies, adult silent non-fiction reading) is **60 readable words** `[V]`. Against that: the all-on rung-0 structural reading at **one** node is a median **978 words / 247 s** = **16.4×** the entire per-move budget (p95 1,273 / 321 s); reading it at **two** of a median encounter's five nodes costs **494 s — 82% of the whole 600-second game**; even handing the **whole 600 s to one median-horizon encounter** (120 s/move) leaves it over budget at a single node. Per item against a 15 s budget: corpus page **3.9×**, full shape panel **5.0×**, human split **1.06×**, guided shape block 0.81×, `bestmove` **0.02×**. **The durable result is a derived number:** the readable loadout is **≈6.6 `fact` items or 0.94 `ranking` items**, and the campaign's chosen five-slot loadout costs **45 words / 11.3 s** — so D78's measured readability floor and a 10+0 move budget land on the same order (5–7 items) by independent routes. **The clock is not needed to collect this**; a slot budget already implements it. Fairness note the numbers require: the shipped default is `SILENT_ASSISTANCE` and costs **0 words**, so these figures describe the opted-in states, which is exactly where the campaign's loadout lives | 💡 open, measured 2026-08-16, `time-as-a-difficulty-lever.md` §4 |
| D359 🐞 | **D78's headline statistic is the wrong summary and moved 34% on ten more packs — quote it per phase.** The all-on state now measures a median **78 observations per position** (47 packs, 609 positions) against Q8's **58** (37 packs, 515 positions), while the **mean barely moved: 57.90 → 62.46** `[V]`. The distribution is **bimodal by phase** and the median sits in its gap — observation-count deciles **27 · 31 · 33 · 37 · 78 · 85 · 87 · 88 · 92**; words per position by declared phase: **endgame 256 (65 s)**, cross-phase 985 (248 s), **opening 1,188 (299 s)**, **middlegame 1,199 (302 s)**. The qualitative conclusion gets **stronger**, not weaker: the all-on state is **4.6× worse in the middlegame** — the phase R4/R9 prove has no oracle — than in the endgame, where an exact one exists. Fix is cheap and mechanical: wherever *"median 58 observations per position"* is cited (`06` §2c, `06` §5, the campaign dossiers, D78 itself), cite the per-phase figures, and quote the all-on state's real headline — **978 words, 247 seconds, one node** | 🐞 open, measured 2026-08-16 |
| D360 ✅ | **REFUTED 2026-08-16 — and its stated remedy would have deleted a live renderer.** The row claimed `clock_zeroed` "renders a shipped sentence the detector never emits" and proposed deleting the subkind and its sentence. **Verified false by claude against the tree:** `packages/runtime/src/transition.ts:360` pushes `{kind: "move_irreversibility", subkind: "clock_zeroed"}` and `:309` evaluates it in `matchesTransitionFeature`. It fires **265 of 771** committed spine transitions — the most frequent `move_irreversibility` observation, more than the other three subkinds combined — and renders through `renderTransitionObservation`. **The measurement error is the lesson:** the row measured `irreversibility()` (`:245-265`), which is *not* that subkind's producer, and concluded from one function's return set that the subkind was dead. Found by the `dead-vocabulary` draft, which reproduced the row's own figures over the 738-edge corpus it was written against in order to isolate the misidentified function — the right way to refute a measurement. **A ledger row is a claim about code and inherits the fallibility of whatever measured it** | ✅ refuted 2026-08-16, remedy withdrawn |
| D361 🐞 | **`clockState` accepts and persists literally anything, and nothing reads it.** Exactly **six** non-test references in `apps/` and `packages/`, all declarations or opaque passthrough — `packages/runtime/src/types.ts:111`, `runtime.ts:57` and `:341`, `apps/web/src/lib/api.ts:468`, `apps/server/src/rest.ts:471-477` and `:1314-1316` — **no `.svelte` file ever sends it** and **no logic consumes it** `[V]`. Its schema definition is `{"description": "Reserved until clock semantics are specified by a later RFC.", "type": "object", "additionalProperties": true}` (`schemas/drill_run.schema.json:206-210`), so the run log will today accept an arbitrary untyped object per node from any client and persist it inside *"the sole source of chess truth"* (`05` §1). Whichever way [[D364]] is ruled, this should not stay open-ended: delete the field, or give it a closed shape in the same commit that gives it a reader | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D362 💡 | **The owner's sentence literally asks for a DEPICTED clock, and it is one retained field away.** *"simulate the time pressure of a GREAT move during 10+0 chess **and then give actual time**"* reads most naturally as: state the recorded time the original decision was made under, and let the learner take as long as they like. That version touches **no invariant, no honesty gate, no inventory and no refused-list entry**, prices nothing, and is a recorded fact so law 8 is clean. **But `05` §1's *"absence is stated, never simulated"* makes grounding a hard constraint, and it currently bites** `[V]`: `parsePgnMainline` keeps **headers whole** (`Object.fromEntries(game.headers)`, `apps/server/src/pgn-import.ts:63`) so `TimeControl` already survives into `ImportedGameRecord.headers`, but each move is reduced to `{san, uci}` at `:54`, **dropping the per-move `[%clk]` comments Lichess emits on every move** — which is exactly the number the owner's sentence is about. So: the game's declared control is importable today; the clock *at the moment of the move* is one retained field plus a renderer. Scope the abstention path first (see the dossier's F6) — if a material share of imports carry no clocks, this is an abstention, not a feature | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D363 💡 | **"A GREAT move" is not expressible, and the nearest honest object is already shipped.** Nothing in this product grades a move (`coaching-versus-cheating-and-the-band-curve.md` §3; law 8). The closest shipped selector is `storyMoments`, which fires on a **≥150 cp recorded swing** (`STORY_PIVOT_CP`, `packages/runtime/src/story.ts:30`, applied `:86`) and renders *"The recorded evaluation moved +X cp across this move"* — a **change**, not a verdict `[V]`. So the honest encounter D362 would depict is *"the move at which the recorded evaluation moved 150 cp, played with 24 seconds left"*: **two recorded facts, no chess claim** — and on this evidence a considerably more interesting object than a countdown. Worth its own row because it is reusable well beyond the clock: it is the general shape for *"replay a famous decision"* content without inventing chess truth, and it connects to [[D329]]'s famous-game pack question | 💡 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D364 💡 | **`DESIGN-GAP:` `06` §5's refused list is one word too broad and its escalation dichotomy is one axis too narrow — both are owner-tier amendments, proposed not written.** (1) *"a pursuit clock is a retry price by another name"* is true of the **run-pooled** clock and false of the **attempt-scoped** one, and the shipped `clockState` field cannot express the refused form ([[D355]]). (2) *"what escalates is LEGIBILITY, not power"* is a complete dichotomy only while nothing constrains the learner's own computation; a clock is a **third kind** — it changes neither what the opponent can do nor what the product will say — and `06` §5 has no verdict on it either way. **The owner question the dossier ends on: is time nothing, a decoration, or a rule?** (a) **nothing** — close the cluster and delete the reserved field, banking the derived ≈6-item loadout as a slot number; (b) **a decoration** — the depicted clock of [[D362]], recommended and cheapest; (c) **a rule** — the enforced attempt-scoped clock, admissible but amending §5 twice, and **a run-pooled budget must be refused in the same ruling** or the design drifts back into the retry price by accident, because the difference between the permitted and the refused object is one field's altitude and nothing today would catch it | 💡 open, owner ruling requested 2026-08-16, `design/research/time-as-a-difficulty-lever.md` §8 |
| D297 📊 | **The three devices that produce fun WITHOUT a power curve — and we own runnable versions of all three, six of them already shipped with no consumer.** 2026-08-16, `design/research/fun-mechanics-outside-roguelikes.md`, the wide sweep past the roguelike frame (15 genres). Outside the roguelike set — where *every* game has a power curve — fun-without-power is produced by exactly three devices: **(1) score the SOLUTION, not the outcome** (Zachtronics' three-axis histogram; Barth: *"the only thing a global leaderboard manages to tell you is that you suck"*); **(2) make KNOWLEDGE the key** (Outer Wilds' ship log, Tunic's manual, La-Mulana items that *"do not grant any abilities, but open up a new area"*); **(3) buy variety with SETUP SELECTION** (Dominion's 10-of-26 kingdom ⇒ ~5.3M configurations with no unlock system; Really Bad Chess' randomised army). **Six shipped objects with zero consumers carry them**: `RETRY_VARIANT_KINDS` (D299), `CheckpointInteraction.prediction`, `AssistancePermission.locked_off` (D298), `shapeRecommendations`, `attempt_concepts` (D300) and `ObjectiveState.degraded`. **The campaign's binding constraint is delivery and sequencing, not content** — the same verdict `feedback-versus-the-dashboard.md` reached for the feedback layer, reached independently for the campaign layer. **Fact refresh that discharges two standing cost arguments** `[V]` at `c55b9cf`: the corpus is **47 packs, 11 of them middlegame** (commit `aee7c64`), so `06` §5's *"Act II is impossible today"* and this ledger's *"Plan Drill is essentially UNAUTHORED"* are both **stale**; `plyHorizon` 46/47 (median 10.5); deviations/checkpoints **330/189**; shapes attested **21 of 25** (D44 shrinks from 9 orphans to 4); `timingWindows` 4 and `variantOf` 2 (both were 0). Also new: **reward branch BREADTH** (Hitman's mastery challenges — *"there is never a 'wrong' way"*, and unconventional approaches unlock options) is the direct answer to *"make experimentation rewarding"*, computable as a census over the learner's own graph via `attempts.countable` + first-divergent-move, with the anti-farming guard being the shipped `plyHorizon` | campaign, D78, D298–D306 |
| D298 📊 | **The capability-suppressing boss is not "architecturally the same shape" — the whole suppression path ships end to end.** `AssistancePermission` is `"free" \| "locked_off" \| "sight" \| "evidence"` (`packages/runtime/src/assistance.ts:20`), with all three layers behind it: the producer (`permittedAssistance` returns `locked_off` on `role`/`deliveryOpen`, `:29`), a **named server refusal** `ASSISTANCE_WITHHELD` (`rest.ts:1090`, `:1107`, `service.ts:826`), and an honest UI sentence rendering the control disabled (`DrillScreen.svelte:717-720`) `[V]`. `AssistanceContext` has exactly three fields, so a boss suppression is one more input. **But it must NOT be a fourth field on `permittedAssistance`** — that function is the *honesty* gate (`06` §3 law 1, honesty outer / inventory inner), and putting a game rule inside it would make a campaign decision indistinguishable from an honesty decision. Required form: an **inner-gate mask** that composes with `permittedAssistance`, reuses the `locked_off` value and the error code, and carries a **different sentence** — *"this encounter withholds X"* is a game statement, *"X is not honestly available here"* is an honesty statement, and one screen must never render them identically | campaign, `06` §3 law 1, `05` §3 |
| D299 💡 | **`RETRY_VARIANT_KINDS` is a five-kind variety vocabulary that is authored, validated, counted — and consumed by nothing.** `["same_root_new_defense", "alternate_plan_class", "related_position_same_idea", "opposite_side", "different_material_details"]` (`packages/schema/src/drill-pack/types.ts:27-33`), **9 variants across 7 of 47 packs**; the only repo-wide consumers are the schema barrel and a `pack-check` counter (`pack-check.ts:24`, `:83`) `[V]`. Those five strings are **Dominion's kingdom sitting in our schema** — *the same board asked a different question*, which is the fun-without-power device that costs zero authoring. `same_root_new_defense` is DDR's difficulty chart (same song, different chart) built from shipped opponent modes; `alternate_plan_class` and `related_position_same_idea` are Chessarama's trick (keep the pieces, swap the goal) over our own nine-value `objective.type`; `different_material_details` is Really Bad Chess, and it is **honest only in the endgame**, where R4's κ = 1.000 makes the modified position exactly assessable | campaign, D297, Dominion, R4 |
| D300 🐞 | **The cross-pack collection axis is authored, stored, indexed — and namespaced apart by one injectable class.** Packs declare `concepts`: **186 tags, 156 distinct across 47 packs, only 24 appearing in ≥2 packs** (`advance-chain-base` in 6) `[V]`. They reach a real table with an index (`attempt_concepts`, `storage.ts:2529-2538`) and two live queries. **But the default resolver is `PackScopedConceptResolver`, whose key is literally ``pack:${packId}#${raw}``** (`progress.ts:56-59`), so the same string in six packs is six keys, and the related-attempt query is additionally scoped `AND a.pack_id = ?` (`storage.ts:1388-1393`). `ConceptResolver` is an **injectable interface** (`progress.ts:52-54`), so a corpus-global resolver is a one-class change. **Two things follow:** the Outer-Wilds-shaped "what you have seen" log is an assembly of two shipped producers (`concepts` = map mode, `shapeRecommendations` = rumor mode); and **it will not be a collection until the vocabulary is one** — 132 of 156 concepts are singletons, so today the honest collection is the 25-entry shape library | campaign, D297, `shapeRecommendations` |
| D301 💡 | **One shared position a day, and a spoiler-free share artifact — the cheapest complete feature in the campaign cluster, and it answers a row that has had no mechanism since it was filed.** Wordle: one puzzle per day identical for everyone, 2,309 curated words, and Wardle's stated posture *"people have an appetite for things that transparently don't want anything from you"* and *"Would I send you a push notification?… Why don't you just forget about Wordle for a little bit?"* — **and the emoji grid was invented by a player (Elizabeth S), not designed.** Chess already has two precedents: **Chessle** (guess an opening, 3 or 5 moves per side, 6 guesses) and **Chessguessr** (guess the five moves *actually played* in a real game, daily at 00:00 UTC, four-band legend) — **Chessguessr is our thesis in miniature and someone else shipped it**, grading against what a human played rather than what an engine prefers, the same choice R9 validated. **Every input ships**: 47 packs + **43 mined candidates in `content/candidates/`**, the run graph, and the `story_read` scoped anonymous token. The share artifact must be the **shape of your branch set**, never a score. Answers the standing *"why open it on Tuesday?"* gap in the skill/progress + return-loop row. **Killers:** a branch set has no fixed shape, so the glyph is the hard part and could become a score by accident; a daily is a cadence promise and a missed day is worse than never having one | campaign, return loop, Q1b |
| D302 💡 | **A three-axis histogram over the learner's OWN submissions — the one device that gives a flat power curve a real progression — with a pre-test that could kill it before any build.** From Zachtronics (Opus Magnum ranks *"speed in cycles, cost in G, and area in tiles"*; Barth chose a histogram over a leaderboard because a leaderboard *"tells you that you suck (and not even by how much)"* and *"is a fantastic incentive for cheating"*). Three axes exist that are about the **learner's process** and not the position's truth: **plies to the objective on the submitted branch** (bounded by `plyHorizon`, 46/47), **assistance rungs switched on at the moment of submission** (the axis **D78 licenses** — all-on is the measured *unreadable* state), and **the sealed objective state** (authored, not graded). **Chess.com already ships axis 2**: bot crowns are 3 for a win with no help, 2 with 1–3 hints/undos, 1 with 4+ — so this is adoption, not invention. **Two hard constraints:** (a) **no count of rewinds, forks or attempts may ever be an axis** — that is C1 in scoring clothes and the most likely accidental violation in the whole campaign; (b) the population is the learner's **own** prior submissions, never other learners (the ChessMonitor finding). **The pre-test that kills it: correlate rungs-on with plies-to-objective on the existing corpus. If they are one axis, the histogram is a disguised single score** | campaign, D78, D297, ChessMonitor |
| D303 🐞 | **`DESIGN-GAP:` `04` §1's Pack row describes an object that does not exist — and the campaign's unit is the RUN, not the pack.** `04` §1 defines a Pack as *"5–15 roots teaching one claim"*. The shipped format has **no `roots` array**: across all 47 canonical packs, `start` is singular and present 47/47 and `roots` appears **0/47** `[V]`. **What ships as a "pack" is `04`'s Root**, so the taxonomy has a shipped level 1 and level 4 (trajectory `legs`, 3 packs) and **nothing at levels 2, 3 or 5** — `family`, `track`, `curriculum`, `campaign`, `run_set`, `playlist` are **0 hits repo-wide**. Meanwhile `RunSessionKind = "pack" \| "position" \| "imported"` (`packages/runtime/src/types.ts:36`) and two of three kinds carry no pack (Just Play, `/start/fen`, flip-sides derivation, **`createRepertoireGapRun` builds a session from a gap key with no pack at all**, `service.ts:585-587`). **The rule that follows is sharp and shipped:** `projectAttempts` forces a pack-less run's verdict to `"open"` (`progress.ts:90`, `:127`), so a pack-less node **cannot seal**. Therefore **intermediate nodes may be pack-less; boss nodes may not** — and the campaign's content bill is bounded by act bosses (3), not by nodes (9). Verdict: the owner's *"packs are micro-DLC"* read is **right about the role, one level off on the grain**; a pack is a content contribution to a node, and the missing container is `04` §1's **Track**. By-product: the community channel is **less unexercised than reported** — `shape-studio.ts:33-34` ships draft→validate→register→export with four named refusals and registers every community shape with `channel: "community"`; what has **no** path is a third-party *Track* | campaign unit, `04` §1, ADR-0007, D297 |
| D304 💡 | **ADR-0007 wants a twin clause: unlocked by playing, never by WINNING.** ⚠️ **owner-facing.** The most consistent finding across the whole genre sweep, in four unrelated systems: *My City* drips on game count (24 games / 8 chapters / 3 per chapter); *Pandemic Legacy* advances after two failed attempts regardless; *Dicey Dungeons* unlocks *"regardless if you win or not"*; **Balatro's Mr. Bones unlocks on *"Lose 5 runs"*** and ships **105 of 150 jokers available on the first run**; *Dungeon World* makes a 6-or-lower the **XP trigger**. **Losing is a legitimate unlock currency in almost every system surveyed.** This matters *now* because `06` §5's accepted ruling gives the campaign a failure state, and the natural next step — gate the next lens on clearing the boss — is exactly the mistake all four avoided, and that WoW paid to undo (attunement: *"This strategy has largely been phased out"*, removed across four patches). Proposed form: **the run can be lost; the catalogue can never be locked.** Costs nothing today, expensive later | ADR-0007, campaign, `06` §5 |
| D305 💡 | **OWNER QUESTION: what is the campaign's progression denominated in, now that the power curve is flat by construction?** ⚠️ **owner-facing**, `design/research/fun-mechanics-outside-roguelikes.md` §10. Three answers, all law-8-legal, all about the same cost, producing **very different products**. **(a) Nothing — cadence and completion** (Wordle + Lichess Practice: a daily position, per-unit mastery marks over a named vocabulary, no number about the learner anywhere). Cheapest and most honest; also the weakest hook for a *run*, and close to the option `roguelike-run-design.md` §4d called *"strictly weaker"*. **(b) The learner's own history** (D302's three-axis histogram). The only option that gives a flat-power product a progression the learner can feel, with an honest basis nobody else has (D78) — but it introduces **the first number this product has ever shown a learner about themselves**, and two findings here say numbers outrun their basis (ChessMonitor's FIDE estimate; Celeste's preamble rewrite, where identical mechanics read differently on a sentence). **(c) The catalogue — a collection** (GW2 + Stardew: shapes met, structures played, the *what's-missing* mark living on the pack card rather than a progress screen). Most chess-shaped, and it turns `04`'s breadth into the reward — but our collection vocabulary is not yet a collection (D300). **(a) is a floor the others build on; (b) and (c) genuinely compete for the same screen** — a histogram of how you played and a map of what you have seen are two different answers to *"how am I doing"*. Picking one first is the ruling; the other can arrive later without being erased | campaign, D297, D300, D302, `06` §5 |
| D306 ⛔ | **Three mechanics whose transformation collapses the invariant — and in two of the three, HALF survives.** Per `design/02`'s amendment the refusal set should be near-empty, and it is. **(1) The prestige multiplier** (Cookie Clicker's Heavenly Chips = cube root of lifetime cookies; AdVenture Capitalist's angels ≈ 150·√lifetime; Kittens' paragon + a whole post-reset tech tree). Refused because a multiplier needs a quantity that measures how far you got *and* multiplies the next attempt's version of it — R4+R9 say no such quantity exists across a run, so it would multiply an invented number (C4 + law 8). **Adopted instead: the reset**, which is Gloomhaven's retirement (*"When a personal quest is completed, the character will retire"* — mandatory, at full power, unlocking a class for anyone) and is the answer to our read-once content decay. **(2) Rarity tiers** (Genshin: 0.6% base, hard pity at 90, 50/50; FEH: 3% + 0.25% every 5, guaranteed at 120, Spark at 40). Refused by `06` §3 law 5 and by ρ = **−0.143** — pricing a lens by scarcity prices noise. **Adopted instead: the pity GUARANTEE**, which is a fairness model over the offer stream, not a rarity model, and composes with the offered-choice draft at zero cost. **(3) The stamina gate** (Genshin Resin cap 200 at 1/8 min; HSR 300 at 1/6 min). **Refused entire** — it prices *playing*, which is strictly worse than pricing retrying (C1), and it is FTL's pursuit clock with the sign flipped. Its only adoptable fragment is its apology (HSR's Reserved Trailblaze Power). **Near-miss resolved by transformation, not refusal: literal chess variants** — Fog of War (*"Players can see only the squares where their pieces can legally move"*) is the capability-suppressing boss as a rule of chess, but `UCI_Chess960` is a **published capability refusal** (`capabilities.ts:105`) and every grounded instrument we own is a standard-chess instrument, so the variant is adopted **in the assistance layer** and refused **in the move generator** | campaign, law 8, ADR-0007, C1/C4, D297 |
| D307 🐞 | **Per-context assistance is ADDRESSING only — there is no per-context content, and the honesty half cannot express a context at all.** `design/05` §4 says *"a curated drill withholds by design; Just Play is the learner's own game and they may want everything"* and §3-forms says each of the five contexts *"gets its own defaults"*. Measured: (a) **`permittedAssistance` takes `sessionKind` and never reads it** — the body of `packages/runtime/src/assistance.ts:27-30` uses only `role` and `deliveryOpen`, so `pack`, `position` and `imported` receive byte-identical permissions and **there is no lever to make Just Play more permissive than a drill even if the owner rules that it should be**; (b) `loadAssistance` returns `SILENT_ASSISTANCE` for **every** unset profile (`assistance-preference.ts:30-33`), so **all six contexts default identically** — the six profiles buy six empty localStorage slots the learner must fill by hand, six times. D82 is correctly closed and its closing sentence *"profiles select defaults and never permission"* is half true: the permission half ships, the defaults half does not. `DESIGN-GAP:` — owner tier | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by `mechanics-by-mode.md` §3.3 |
| D308 🐞 | **Just Play cannot open disclosure mid-run, so rungs 3, 4 and 6 are structurally unreachable while playing — the exact opposite of the owner's *"full toolkit"*.** `attempt_end` is forced by three independent fences (`session-controller.ts:271`, `rest.ts:368`, `events.ts:157`); `permittedAssistance` keys `humanSplit`/`corpus`/`boardLighting`/`arrows` off `deliveryOpen`; and **`api.reveal` has exactly two client call sites, neither in `DrillScreen.svelte`** (`App.svelte:292` story load, `:317` game import). Measured hands-on: a fresh Just Play run's assistance panel renders "Human move split on request" and "Corpus counts on request" **permanently disabled**. The disclosure model is right and ADR-0006 is honoured — what is missing is one control, at `DrillScreen.svelte:712-726` plus an `onReveal` prop at `App.svelte:574`. Same three fences make the **`onramp` profile structurally unreachable from Just Play** (`PositionRun.feedbackPolicy` is type-locked to `"attempt_end"`, `packages/runtime/src/session.ts:47`), so `design/05` §3b's *"natural default for the 1000–1400 on-ramp"* has no path to a beginner who just plays. **Highest cost:value ratio in the audit** | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by `mechanics-by-mode.md` §3.4 |
| D309 🐞 | **Guided mode is inverted: the mechanism ships ungated and ON, while the switch labelled "Named-pattern guidance" gates a strictly smaller duplicate that needs an unrelated switch first.** `design/05` §3b specifies guided mode as *the shape library rendered live*, *a mode a learner chooses*, band-shaped for the 1000–1400 on-ramp and *fading* above. Measured: the shape library **already renders live in every mode with no gate** — `shapeFirings` (`DrillScreen.svelte:206`) → `shapeMarkers` (`:207`) → `Timeline.svelte:74` → `ShapePanel.svelte:30`, which prints §3b's own sentence verbatim; and a position run loads the **full 25-entry catalogue** while a pack loads only its declared subset (`session-controller.ts:620`). What `assistance.guided` controls is a narrower copy inside the pivotal-marker modal (`:305-309`, `:1018-1020`) that additionally requires `assistance.markers === "live"`. Consequences: **§3a's silence default is violated in code** (`SILENT_ASSISTANCE` sets `guided: "off"` and markers render anyway), and **band-shaping does not exist** — nothing in `shapeFirings`, `Timeline.svelte:74` or `assistanceProfile` reads a rating band. `DESIGN-GAP:` — owner tier | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by `mechanics-by-mode.md` §3.5 |
| D310 🐞 | **The pivotal-marker modal is an undeclared chokepoint: three unrelated capabilities render only inside it.** `renderEndgameReading` has **exactly one client call site** — `DrillScreen.svelte:1017`, inside `{#if openPivotalNodeId !== undefined}` — so **B10's "endgame steering names a technique rather than a move" is reachable only when an unrelated forward detector (irreversibility, phase change, Maia divergence, option collapse) has fired on that node, markers are on, and the learner clicks the marker.** In an endgame those detectors may never fire, and the surface naming Lucena/Philidor/Vancura sits behind them in every mode. The same modal is the only trigger for spoken voice (`:338-352` fires from `openPivotalMarker` at `:357`) and the only home of the `guided` shape list. A capability gated on a *different* capability's detector is a routing accident, not a disclosure rule — nothing in `design/05` puts endgame steering behind pivotal detection | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by `mechanics-by-mode.md` §3.6 |
| D311 🐞 | **CORRECTED 2026-08-20: Settings now configures all nine axes across six profiles (54 controls); the older “six axes / three overlap / 36 controls” finding is false at HEAD.** The remaining defect is narrower: (a) `arrows` is persisted/permissioned/rendered as a setting and has no production reader ([[D158]]); (b) `ambient` renders `<button aria-label="Open assistance">♟</button>` with no `onclick`, a status glyph mislabelled as a control; (c) `DrillScreen` loads the profile once in `onMount`, so settings changes do not update an already-mounted run; (d) `boardLighting: "sight"` and `"evidence"` produce the same selected-observation overlays—the latter only changes downgrade/caption behaviour because no disclosed-evidence overlay producer exists. The in-run panel exposes six axes and every one also exists in Settings; `boardLighting`/`arrows`/`ambient` are Settings-only. | 🐞 open on four residues; original measured 2026-08-16, re-derived 2026-08-20 during D635 |
| D312 🐞 | **Rung-2 analysis on request is still reachable only from an active branch-group panel.** ✅ **(a) closed 2026-08-16:** `tablebase:` refs now render the attached exact category explicitly for the side to move, piece count, DTZ and source; absent payloads say details are pending rather than inventing them. ✅ **(b) closed 2026-08-16:** comparison objective grounds resolve the run's attached payload map, with a regression proving an engine ref no longer stays permanently pending. (c) **Rung 2 on request is reachable only through "Analyze missing evidence" inside a branch-group panel** (`GroupPanel.svelte`, rendered only when `activeGroup !== undefined`) | 🐞 open for (c); rendering holes (a)/(b) closed 2026-08-16 |
| D313 🐞 | **Simulate is the largest implemented-with-no-entry-point in the repository: two complete server verbs and zero client bytes.** `service.simulate` (`service.ts:1233-1304`) and `enterSimulation` (`:1305-1339`) are routed (`rest.ts:1408-1426`), documented as shipped surfaces (`docs/n-way-comparison.md:29-32`) and put explicitly in scope by `rfc/archive/n-way-comparison.md:91`. Measured: `grep -rn -- "/simulate" apps/web/src` → **0**; no method on `DrillApi`, none on `session-controller`, no control. The only client trace is *display* of the resulting label (`BranchRail.svelte:54`, `CompareView.svelte:54`). And the RFC's own named acceptance test (`:1383-1395`, *"a simulation is scratch until entered, and a prediction shows a distribution"*) was **never written** — `grep -rn -i simulate tests/` → **0** — which is why nothing caught it. Refusal in Just Play is correct and stated (`service.ts:1246` `NO_AUTHORED_VARIATIONS`); the Drills and Live absence has no stated reason | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by `mechanics-by-mode.md` §5.2 |
| D314 🐞 | **The learner-initiated half of the return loop is server-only, and Just Play can never contribute to the other half.** (a) `duplicate` and `schedule` are the **only two run verbs whose sole producer is the REST route**: `api.duplicateRun` (`api.ts:798`) has **zero callers** and `schedule` has **no client method at all** (`grep -rn -- "/schedule" apps/web/src` → 0), against `design/03:303`'s claim that B7 shipped *"duplicate, related retry"*. Auto-schedules still populate `/learn` (`storage.ts:1584-1610`), so the surface is not empty — the *learner-initiated* verbs are unreachable. (b) **Every Just Play attempt is `graded: false, verdict: "open"` forever** (`progress.ts:107,125`) and **contributes zero concept tags** (`:138-149`, pushed only `if (pack !== undefined)`). Concepts are the only cross-pack grouping key, so the mode the owner wants to be the wide one adds nothing to the return loop's vocabulary. The ungraded state is honestly rendered (`App.svelte:737`); the concept omission is the sharper hole and has no stated reason | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by `mechanics-by-mode.md` §5.2 |
