| D315 🐞 | **Live still lacks browser vote participation, working rotation setup, spectator links, and academy-specific behaviour.** (a) **Nobody can cast a vote from a browser**: `castVote`, `closeVote`, `resolveProposal` and `updateGrants` all have **zero Svelte callers**, so the audience half of the stream/academy loop is server-complete and browser-absent, while `docs/live-sessions.md:67` claims the browser exposes the full range (true for *opening* a window at `App.svelte:806`, false for *casting into it*). `rotation` board control is offered in the picker (`App.svelte:797`) and **always 400s** because the client never sends `rotationHandles` (`api.ts:620`); friend links hardcode `invitedRole:"participant"` (`App.svelte:468`) and only render for native matches, so **spectator links are unreachable**. (b) ✅ **Closed 2026-08-16:** Fork, Branch group, and Timeline rewind are disabled with `HonestControl` reasons for read-only viewers; `B`, `R`, and `Enter` are inert; component and granted-spectator browser regressions prove no write callback fires. Also: **`academy` has zero behavioural consumers anywhere** — the mode the owner calls *coaching/teaching* is, in code, the one with least behaviour of its own | 🐞 open for (a) and academy behaviour; read-only mutation surface closed 2026-08-16 |
| D316 🐞 | **"It steers you by classifying openings, strategies, endgames" is aspirational: classification describes, and exactly one instrument steers.** Censused, `classifyPhase`/`structuralReading`/`endgameReading`/`shapeFirings`/`pivotalMarkers` have **four** non-test consumers and all four are renderers — `DrillScreen.svelte` (sentences), `guidance.ts:30-46` (voice packet), `story.ts:97-122` (post-hoc slides), `pivotal.ts:47` (passive markers). **Nothing selects an opponent, a band, a position, an objective, a pack, a next node or an assistance rung from a classification.** Three specific breaks: **`shapeRecommendations` computes a `packIds` list and the client discards it** — `onclick={() => navigate("/play")}` labelled *"Find {packId}"* (`App.svelte:667`), dropping the learner on an unfiltered grid; **`PackList.svelte` renders `phase` as a chip with no filter, sort or grouping** (`grep -c 'filter\|sort\|select'` → 0) against `design/03:70-72`'s *"first-class navigation and filters"*; and **`/learn` contains the words opening/middlegame/endgame zero times**. The one genuine steer is **repertoire gap finding** (`/learn` → *Go to biggest gap* → `createRepertoireGapRun` at a gap FEN, at the repertoire's band) — and even it exposes only the **top-ranked** gap (`{#if index===0}`, `App.svelte:694`) while the server's `enter` accepts any `gapKey`, and its write-back verb `chooseRepertoireAnswer` has **zero client callers**, so scanning never converges. Also blocks `design/06` §1's encounter unlock, whose detector this is | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 by `mechanics-by-mode.md` §4 |
| D332 💡 | **Measure the learner-s Elo — the owner-s answer 2026-08-16, and it is LAW-8-LEGAL, which nobody had noticed.** Offered three denominators for progression (own-history histogram / catalogue / cadence) the owner declined the fork: *"we need history of learner too… and maybe catalogue progress… but the idea is again: go from elo 1000-2000 so maybe we need to just measure player ELO properly."* **The apparent collision dissolves on inspection.** `design/research/coaching-versus-cheating-and-the-band-curve.md` established the product can say neither *"played well"* nor *"run succeeded"* — no learner number exists anywhere, prediction grading was deleted at schema 0.8→0.9, and law 8 forbids grading moves. **But an Elo is not a claim about a move.** It is arithmetic over win/loss/draw against a known-strength opponent — structurally the same object as the explorer result splits the owner ruled admissible as `corpus_observed` (rung 4) on 2026-08-15: it says *what happened*, never *what was good*. **So Elo is the one learner measurement that requires grading nothing**, and it is the denominator the campaign has been missing since the power curve was ruled flat. It also makes *"you-d need proper 2000 Elo skills"* a claim about the **learner** rather than about the encounter-s configuration — the exact gap the coaching dossier identified and could not close. Both other denominators stay wanted (*"we need history of learner too… and maybe catalogue progress"*); this is the spine they hang on | 💡 open, owner ruling 2026-08-16 |
| D333 ✅ | **[[D332]] has one hard prerequisite and it is already ledgered as [[D324]]: nobody has measured that Maia-s band moves the RESULT.** R10 measured that the band moves the **distribution** — the policy vector differs by band, bit-identically reproducible. **That is not the same claim.** An Elo computed against Maia bands is meaningless unless Maia at 1500 actually *loses more often* than Maia at 1800 over played games. Evidence now points at the gap rather than closing it: `design/research/maia-wdl-versus-human-outcome.md` found band-to-band WDL movement correlates with the human population-s at **Pearson 0.02–0.04, sign agreement 47–52%**, against a real human movement of 1.2–2.2 pp after sampling noise — *"doesn-t settle D324, but it points."* **This is now the highest-value unrun experiment in the repo**, because it gates the campaign-s only viable progression denominator. Its shape is simple and it is the one thing R10 did not do: play games, band against band, and count results | ✅ **ANSWERED 2026-08-16, `design/research/maia-band-outcome-transfer.md` — YES, and the units are the finding.** 16,660 whole games, 0 voids, paired-opening estimator with CIs clustered on the opening. **The band moves the result monotonically at every gap tested down to 100 points**, and D324's pre-registered ladder **passes** — all three adjacent CI pairs disjoint under both naive and cluster-robust intervals. Controls behave: same-band arms sit at −3.1 and −0.7 Elo, and a temperature positive control fires at +468. **But the transfer ratio is ~0.29.** Maia's declared `[1000, 2400]` is a **~290-Elo ladder with about five learner-distinguishable rungs**, not a 1000→2000 journey. **For [[D332]]: the denominator survives; its units do not.** |
| D334 💡 | **Long-term unlocks are an ideation topic, deliberately not decided now — and ADR-0007-s twin clause is refused as a HARD rule.** Offered *"progression is never unlocked by winning"* as a companion to ADR-0007-s *never purchased*, the owner declined it as an absolute: *"not as a HARD rule… IE reaching a high checkpoint, maybe you want to start at the latest level vs 2000 ELO or w/e… or a weird variant run… we don-t have to have all this answered immediately, we can ideate on long term rewards/unlocks that are fun and motivate more experimentation or playing."* **The distinction that survives, and it is the useful one: winning may unlock CONVENIENCE and VARIETY, never CONTENT.** Starting a later run at a higher band, or opening a variant run, costs the learner nothing they have already earned — whereas locking a structure, a technique or a pack behind a win would make losing subtract from what you have learned, which cuts against the thesis and against the submitted-branch ruling. Candidates to ideate on, none decided: skip-ahead starts, variant runs (see [[D327]]), modifier runs, cosmetic or titular marks, and a New Game+ that changes the *rules* rather than the *numbers* | 💡 open, owner ruling 2026-08-16 |
| D335 💡 | **The band's TRANSFER RATIO is 0.29 over the corpus and 0.40 at full material, and it is the number [[D332]] has been missing.** Measured 2026-08-16 over **16,660 games** (`design/research/maia-band-outcome-transfer.md`). Read off D324's four-rung ladder, where all rungs share one reference so nothing is chained: **28.90 Elo per 100 band points, ratio 0.289 [0.269, 0.309]**; restricted to positions with all the material still on (≥21 pieces), **39.98 per 100, ratio 0.400 [0.379, 0.421]** `[V]`. Seven independent pairwise gaps agree — 0.207 [0.183, 0.234] at 1000↔2400, 0.261 [0.233, 0.291] at 1000↔2000, 0.233 [0.185, 0.281] at 1500↔1800, 0.221 [0.124, 0.318] at 1500↔1600, 0.269 [0.165, 0.373] at 1900↔2000. **The band is an Elo-shaped dial that is not denominated in Elo**, and the factor is between 2.5 and 3.5. Every future statement of the form *"this encounter is band N"* now has a conversion, and every statement of the form *"the learner is at Elo N"* needs one | 💡 open, measured 2026-08-16 |
| D336 💡 | **A 100-band step is real and is NOT a rung — the campaign's usable granularity is ≈300 band points.** The two adjacent-step arms give **22.1 Elo [12.4, 31.8]** (1500→1600, p 7.7e-06, n 3,400) and **26.9 Elo [16.5, 37.4]** (1900→2000, p 4.1e-07, n 2,720) `[V]`. Against the rung threshold derived from the learner's own arithmetic — a 30-game session estimates an Elo to about **±60**, so a step under ~60 real Elo cannot be told from its neighbour by the learner's own results — both fail by more than half. Dividing the threshold by the measured slope ([[D335]]) gives the smallest step that clears it: **≈150 band points at full material** and **≈208 over the corpus as authored**, i.e. **five to nine rungs across `[1000, 2400]`, not fourteen**; on the pairwise arms the first step measured wholly above the line is **1500→1800**. **The campaign's band ladder should be specified in steps of ~150–300, and no surface a learner can see should offer 100-point band granularity** — it is a distinction they cannot experience | 💡 open, measured 2026-08-16 |
| D337 🐞 | **[[D332]]'s 1000→2000 journey does not fit in the instrument that would measure it.** Denominated against Maia bands, the stated journey is worth **260.7 real Elo [233.3, 291.0]** the whole usable range `[1000, 2400]` is worth **289.6 [256.7, 327.4]**, and the most favourable cut the instrument offers — the full-material ladder from band 1000 to 2200 — is **479.8 [454.9, 504.7]** over 1200 band points `[V]`. The coverage requirement was derived before the run from D332's own journey over R10's own range — the band range must span at least what it denominates, ratio ≥ **1000/1400 = 0.714** — the corpus-wide 0.207 reaches **29%** of it and the best case 0.400 reaches **56%** — **the journey does not fit on any cut of the data.** **D332 is not refuted; its units are.** Three exits, none this dossier's to choose: restate the journey as the OPPONENT's band rather than the learner's Elo (cheapest, but re-opens the conflation `coaching-versus-cheating-and-the-band-curve.md` was written to close); calibrate the axis and publish the ratio; or widen the ladder with a lever other than the band, which `06` §2b already reaches for in two of three phases | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D338 💡 | **R10's usable range is wider than the USEFUL range: above ~2000 the band buys nothing measurable.** The 400 band points from 2000 to 2400 are worth **+28.9 Elo, 95% CI [−16.7, 74.5], p = 0.21** `[V]`. This does not contradict [[D60]]/R10 — `[1000, 2400]` is a bound on where the band still *reaches the model*, measured on the policy, and it is correct on its own terms. It is the second edge R10 explicitly did not measure: where the band still *buys difficulty*. Consequence for `06` §2b: a boss specified at band 2400 is not meaningfully harder than one at band 2000, so the top of the campaign cannot be built out of band alone | 💡 open, measured 2026-08-16 |
| D339 🐞 | **What attenuates the band is MATERIAL, not the phase label — and below ten pieces the band is not a difficulty lever, with nothing in the schema or the tools saying so.** Cut by start-position piece count, the widest gap (1000 v 2400) is worth **−468.9 Elo at ≥21 pieces**, −145.5 at 11–20 and **−72.4 at ≤10**; at a **100-band step** it is categorical — **−28.7** and **−37.2 Elo** at full material with CIs excluding parity in both arms, against **−10.1** and **−4.0** at ≤10 pieces with CIs straddling it in both `[V]`. The declared-phase cut says the same thing less cleanly, and only because this corpus's endgame packs are its low-material ones: split by phase, the widest gap tested (1000 v 2400) is worth **−459.1 Elo in the opening, −459.5 in the middlegame and −83.3 in the endgame**; at a 100-band step the endgame effect is **−6.8** and **−3.0 Elo** with score CIs of [0.478, 0.502] and [0.483, 0.509] — **straddling parity in both arms**, while the same step is measurable in the opening in both `[V]`. Part of the compression is that a decided low-material position decides the game rather than the opponent (these arms carry the highest draw rates), but the effect survives at the widest gap where that would compress both sides equally — the ≤10-piece cut is still −72.4 Elo at p ≪ 0.001, so the dial is **attenuated, not absent**. **`06` §2b's endgame BOSS is `perfect_tablebase` so the campaign spine is unaffected** — but every endgame pack setting `opponentPolicy: human_common` with a `targetElo` is turning a dial worth roughly **7 Elo per 100 band points** rather than 40, and no lint, schema field or authoring surface warns the author. Cheapest remedy: a lint that warns when a pack's start position is below ~15 pieces and its `opponentPolicy` is `human_common` with a `targetElo` | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D340 🐞 | **Maia IS seeded — `--seed`, default 42 — and the shipped ENTRYPOINT passes none, so every sidecar this product has ever started ran at seed 42.** `maia3-uci` calls `seed_everything(cfg.seed)` before the engine is constructed (`/opt/maia3/maia3/uci.py:525`, `:68`; `utils.py:12-18` seeds `random`, NumPy and `torch.manual_seed`), and `workers/maia/Dockerfile`'s ENTRYPOINT is `maia3-uci --model 5m --use-uci-history` `[V]`. Measured, not inferred: two fresh containers given an identical 20-request sequence returned a **byte-identical** move sequence; a third at `--seed 7` returned a different one, itself reproducible; inside one process the same request repeated 20 times still returns 4 distinct moves `[V]`. **Both are true — the stream is stochastic per REQUEST and deterministic per PROCESS.** This corrects a reading, not a measurement: R5 (`maia-policy-scalar-stability.md` §9b) describes the mechanism exactly right, but its §10 concludes `human_common` is not reproducible and that flipping `seedHonored` *"would make the record less true"* — `seedHonored: false` is correct about the **UCI surface** and there is no UCI seed option, but the **process** is seed-honoring via a CLI flag, so `rfc/archive/resistance-spectrum.md` open question 1 has a determinism route nobody costed. Whether to pass `--seed` in the image, and whether the identity record should say so, is the RFC tier's call | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D341 🐞 | **An unseeded parallel engine-vs-engine harness manufactures duplicate games, and the failure mode is a control that returns exactly the right answer.** The first run of the D333 harness used twelve workers with no explicit seed; over 1,222 games its same-band control returned **611 of 611** mirrored pairs with **byte-identical move lists**, a **50.8%** duplicate-game rate, and a paired score of **exactly 0.500000 with a standard error of exactly 0.0** `[V]`. Mechanism is [[D340]] plus an even worker count: workers *s* and *s+1* received mirrored schedules from the same seed 42 and stayed in lockstep. **The control did not fail to detect a difference — it reported a perfect, zero-variance null, which is the most confident possible wrong answer.** Fixed by a distinct `--seed` per worker and an ODD worker count so worker never lines up with colour; the final run has 16,013/16,660 distinct move lists and 133/8,330 identical mirrored pairs. **Standing rule for this repo's future engine-vs-engine work: seed each worker explicitly, count distinct move lists, and treat a zero-variance control as a defect rather than a result** | 🐞 open, found 2026-08-16 |
| D342 💡 | **[[D324]]'s pre-registration was aimed at ORDER when the question was SCALE — the criterion passes and settles nothing.** *"Monotone score across all four arms with non-overlapping 95% CIs"* is a shape test. R10 had already shown the band-conditioned distribution is ordered; the open question was how far the dial actually moves the result, and **any dial with a positive transfer ratio passes this criterion at sufficient n** — the measured 0.23 passes it, and so would 0.05. The pre-registration instinct is right and should be kept; the lesson is that **a criterion a positive-but-useless effect passes cannot fail informatively**, and the replacement is an effect-size threshold with a CI ([[D335]], [[D336]]). Worth generalising to the other 🔬 rows that specify a direction and no magnitude | 💡 open, raised 2026-08-16 |
| D343 💡 | **The product's SYMMETRIC band conditioning is not leaving difficulty on the table.** Upstream's `Elo` sets **both** `self_elo` and `oppo_elo` (`uci.py:383-389`), so a band-1500 mover is always told its opponent is 1500 whoever it is facing — which is the obvious explanation for a small transfer ratio. Tested: a sensitivity arm sending the true `SelfElo`/`OppoElo` pair and no `Elo` returns **−249.7 Elo [−283.2, −219.8]** at the widest gap against the symmetric arm's **−289.6 [−327.4, −256.7]** — a difference of **+39.9, 95% CI [−7.6, 87.4], p = 0.10**, i.e. no improvement and a slightly *smaller* gap `[V]`. **So ~0.23 is a property of the model's band conditioning, not of the request shape, and there is no cheap win available by changing what the selector sends.** Records the arm so it is not re-proposed | 💡 open, measured 2026-08-16 |
| D344 💡 | **[[D365]]'s Glicko update needs the MEASURED opponent scale, not the declared band, and the factor is 4.3.** D365 already says *"a Glicko update against an opponent whose declared rating does not predict its results produces a confidently wrong number faster than Elo would"*. That is now a number: the declared band over-predicts the result gap by about **2.5× at full material and 3.5× over the corpus** ([[D335]]). A rating system fed the band as the opponent's rating converges quickly to a rating that is wrong by construction and whose RD shrinks anyway, because RD narrows on *volume*, not on *validity* — the uncertainty term does not protect against a mis-specified opponent. Fed the measured strength instead (the D324 ladder gauged at band 1400 ≡ 0, ≈0.29 per band point over the corpus and ≈0.40 at full material) it has a defensible input. **Concrete requirement for whatever implements D332/D365: the opponent's rating in the update must be a CALIBRATED value, stored separately from `targetElo`, and `targetElo` must never be passed to a rating update directly** | 💡 open, raised 2026-08-16 |
| D365 💡 | **Rating systems already solve [[D332]], and the one property that matters here is UNCERTAINTY.** Owner extension 2026-08-16: *"we can think of other ways to gauge progression… make it configurable in the profile or when opponents win/lose against bots readjust… there must be measures for this."* There are, and they are standard: **Elo** (K-factor, no uncertainty term), **Glicko/Glicko-2** (Lichess-s own system — carries a **rating deviation** and a **volatility** alongside the number), **TrueSkill** (μ and σ). **Glicko-2 is the obvious candidate and the reason is not accuracy, it is honesty.** RD lets the system say *"1450 ± 180"* and, more importantly, lets it **refuse to say anything** until RD narrows — which is the same discipline as the tablebase abstaining outside range, the explorer abstaining below 100 games, and the `unmeasured` disposition. A product that refuses to manufacture chess truth should not manufacture a rating either, and every alternative here either asserts a bare number or has no term for its own ignorance. **The owner-s two mechanisms map exactly onto it**: *"configurable in the profile"* is a self-declared seed with a wide provisional RD (Lichess-s own onboarding), and *"readjust when they win/lose against bots"* is the update step. Neither needs invention. **Both are gated on [[D333]] in the same way** — a rating derived from games against Maia bands is only as meaningful as the bands are real, and a Glicko update against an opponent whose declared rating does not predict its results produces a confidently wrong number faster than Elo would. Note also the encoding fact from `design/research/maia-wdl-versus-human-outcome.md`: Maia already emits an expected score directly, `0.5 + cp/2000` exactly, which is the same quantity a rating system predicts — so there is a cross-check available that most implementations never get | 💡 open, owner extension 2026-08-16 |
| D99 ✅ | **CLOSED as a prevented propagation hazard.** D64 was repaired by `fixture-realism`; `engine-leverage` scoped criterion 4 to the engine path, implemented, passed A0's independent current review and archived. No tablebase-derived cost was certified. The row remains the historical example of why a new consumer must name upstream evidence debt before landing. | ✅ dependency and consumer both closed; reverified 2026-08-20 routing audit |
| D97 ✅ | **37 of the 61 unbacked claims can NEVER earn admission.** `/feedbackClaims/\d+/text` is a `PROSE_POINTER` admitting only a registered explorer or engine template, and `tablebase_result` can be neither — so the 37 `tablebase_exact` claims fail `EVIDENCE_OVERREACH` at **error** severity, permanently. Worse, the one shipped path that *does* work overwrites the author: `explorer.ts` assigns `pack.feedbackClaims[i].text = renderExplorerFrequency(values)`. **0 of 61 have a prose-preserving attach path.** So a rule that withholds them pending backing is not deferral — as the review put it, *a debt you are forbidden to pay is not a debt, it is a silent deletion with a rule attached* | 💡 open — **OWNER RULED 2026-08-15: fix it properly.** Asked to choose between withholding the 61 claims, delivering them with stated absence, or tiering by label, the owner refused all three: *"why not fix them properly?"* The debt is to be made **payable** — the validator gains a prose-preserving attachment path so an instrument record can *back* authored prose instead of *replacing* it — rather than the product choosing which authored sentences to silently drop. This is the third time this ruling has landed in this shape (cf. *"we need to fix this asap. fix all to include it properly. we are the authors"*). Owned by `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md`; dissolves `feedback-delivery`'s C6 fork rather than answering it |
| D98 ✅ | **Support pointers are index-keyed into `feedbackClaims`**, so inserting or reordering a claim silently rebinds every pointer past it to a different claim. Latent while nothing points there (0 of 764 do today) and live the moment the delivery work lands | 🔨 owned by `rfc/archive/claim-backing.md` — closed structurally rather than by a test: claim backing moves out of `record.supports` into a `claimBindings` artifact keyed by `claimId` + `sha256(text)`, and a claim pointer in `record.supports` becomes an unconditional `EVIDENCE_OVERREACH`. Found 2026-08-15 |
| D96 🐞 | **Per-leg trajectory expressiveness is CLOSED 2026-08-16 by `format-surface`; one bundled vocabulary gap remains.** Pack 0.25 adds optional per-leg `opponentPolicy` and `shapes`, active-leg resolution on ordinary and group reply paths, a derived `requestedByLeg` resistance projection, and pointed validation refusals. `deviation.planClassId` remains inexpressible and unowned, so this bundled row stays open for that named residue rather than losing it during closeout. | 🐞 partially closed 2026-08-16 by `format-surface`; `deviation.planClassId` remains |
| D95 ✅ | **`selectionCacheKey` includes `targetElo`.** A direct regression proves otherwise-identical selection requests at different Elo bands cannot share a memo entry. | ✅ closed 2026-08-16 by the permission-and-correctness batch |
| D92 🐞 | **A ninth reader of `run_grants`, and it is disclosure-facing rather than structural.** `RunStorage.listLiveSessions` (`GET /sessions` → `LiveSessionService.list`) joins `run_grants` and is **not** on the `runRole`/`requireRead` path, while projecting live FEN, side to move, ply count, lease-holder handle, last-move time, pause state and match-player handles. An unfiltered **expired** grant therefore keeps a learner's live board on a watcher's simul wall — on the exact primitive the teacher RFC credits as *"N student boards on one page"*. Found 2026-08-15 by cross-review after the draft enumerated eight readers and claimed completeness | 🔨 owned by `rfc/teacher-surface.md` |

| D30 ✅ | **CLOSED — verified 2026-08-15 by two independent passes (the reconciliation gate flagged it un-flipped; claude re-checked the code): `outcome.ts` now reports the fifty-move draw. *Original:* **The fifty-move rule and threefold repetition are not terminal outcomes.** `terminalOutcome` (`outcome.ts:5-11`) reports only under chessops' `isEnd()` = insufficient material or no legal moves. So a **Hold drill saved by repetition — one of the most common real defensive resources in chess — never fires `outcome.reached`**, and the automatic outcome rules cannot grade it. Consequence is wider than the cursed-win case that surfaced it: it silently constrains every defensive objective we ship. **OWNER RULING 2026-08-15: this is a defect, not a question.** (*"we're making drill packs — what do you think should happen if someone achieves the goal that would ALWAYS resolve in a draw in GM games? DRAW IS A COMMON OUTCOME IN TOURNAMENTS."*) Claude framed claim-vs-automatic as an open chess-rules question; it is not one. In a drill, a position repeated three times or a fifty-move counter run out **is a draw** — both sides in any real game take it — so the outcome fires and grading resolves it by objective: success under `hold`/`save`, failure under `win`, exactly as `pack-orchestrator.ts:243` already treats draws. No new vocabulary is needed. Fix: `terminalOutcome` reports a draw on threefold repetition and on the fifty-move count, not only under chessops' `isEnd()`. This also removes the reason `authoring-frictions` §8 grades through an authored condition — that workaround exists only because of this defect | 🔨 shipped by `authoring-frictions` §9 |

Found by the structural-reading draft, which would have been the first to add one. A predicate language whose dispatch fails open is the never-silent contract at the layer that decides whether an objective fires | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D25 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `pack-studio §13c`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **`provenance` is a passthrough, so any future trust field can be forged in the document.** `provenance.additionalProperties` is `true` (`schemas/drill_pack.schema.json:598`) and `projectPackDocument` returns the object whole (`apps/server/src/pack-registry.ts:70`) — so an author writing `"channel": "official"` or `"reviewedBy": "…"` gets it validated, registered **and served to the browser**. The publication channel is safe only because it is server-derived and never read from the document; this row records that the *general* hazard remains for anything added later. `pack-studio.md` §13c closes it with a rendering allow-list rather than a schema narrowing, which would invalidate every committed pack | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D22 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-14 by `defect-batch-2`.** `opponentPolicy` now rejects unknown fields under pack schema 0.12, with an exact-path negative fixture and full committed-corpus validation. *Original defect:* an author could write a policy key that nothing reads and validation blessed it — D3's never-silent shape surviving in the pack format | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D23 ⛔ | **FILED IN ERROR; residual closed 2026-08-14 by `defect-batch-2`.** The emitter has emitted `phase` since its first commit (`608d80a`), which predates this row; all four candidates carry it and emitter tests already pinned honest omission for ambiguity. The added browser regression proves a null-phase pack remains visible as `unclassified`. Kept for the record per the rejected-rows convention. *Original claim:* `position-seeds` emits packs without `phase` | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D27 ✅ | **CLOSED by `shape-library` (`7cf2e32`); docs residual completed by `defect-batch-2`.** The structural envelope records finite samples without gating them; `docs/structural-reading.md` now carries quiet/load observations, the 100 ms investigation tripwire, and the visible-output command. *Original defect:* a wall-clock envelope asserted inside the unit gate could report either answer on identical code under load | 🐞 found 2026-08-14 |
| D24 ✅ | **CLOSED by `defect-sweep`; regression completed by `defect-batch-2`.** Secure cookies remain the default unless the exact string `false` opts out; both the environment encoding and omitted-option behavior are tested. *Original defect:* the light release profile needed the F3 auth boundary's transport default pinned | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D21 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-14 by `defect-batch-2`.** A segment now exists iff a validated `segment.completed` event exists. Coincident checkpoints emit none; genuine pre-guard zero-length records remain readable; forged branch/node/order/adjacency scope is rejected before it can widen authored reveal. *Original defect:* the producer refused coincident segments while `deriveSegments` invented them | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D20 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `pack-studio`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **A pack-digest mismatch silently disables orchestration while the run keeps accepting moves.** `#registeredPack` (`apps/server/src/service.ts:621-625`) returns `undefined` when `pack.digest !== run.packDigest`, and the four call sites then skip `orchestratePackMove` entirely — so checkpoints stop firing, objectives stop grading, and every authored disclosure stops, **with the run still accepting moves and reporting success**. The learner plays on inside a drill that has quietly stopped being one. Unreachable today only because a pack id resolves to one document per process; **pack versioning makes it live for every in-flight run of every pack that gets a new version**. `rfc/pack-studio.md` §3 makes run→pack resolution digest-addressed *before* the write path exists. **Review 2026-08-13 found digest-addressing alone is not total** — an official pack's bytes are git-mutable, a playtest run is orphaned by the author's next save, and a restored database misses rows; in all three the run kept accepting moves silently. §3a adds `PACK_UNRESOLVABLE` thrown from all four call sites *before* `commitMove` | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 by the studio draft |
| D17 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `live-session-platform`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **The lease claim has no compare-and-swap and no transaction — any write-capable learner can seize the board.** `claimLease` (`apps/server/src/storage.ts:758-777`) runs `UPDATE drill_runs SET active_writer_id = ?, active_writer_learner_id = ? WHERE id = ?` with no predicate on the current holder, and the role check is not in the same transaction. So a `participant` can take the board from a host mid-session, and two simultaneous claimants both pass the check, both update, and both believe they hold it. F3 delivered *who may write*; it never delivered **policy over possession**. **Scope corrected 2026-08-13 by adversarial review: the hole spans *every* run carrying a `participant` grant, not only coached sessions.** `mayWrite` returns true for `participant` (`apps/server/src/authorization.ts:21`) and grants are issued on any run through the shipped path, so a board-control mode keyed on session existence would have left every ordinary run exposed. Fixed in `live-session-platform.md` by deriving board control from the write-capable grantee count when no session exists, plus the CAS the claim never had | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D18 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `live-session-platform`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **A withheld spectator's board freezes with no signal.** `publicEvents` truncates at the barrier and pins `nextSeq` to `sinceSeq` (`apps/server/src/feedback-policy.ts:45-50`), so a polling reader asks for the same sequence forever. A player eventually reaches a checkpoint and unblocks; a spectator cannot, and nothing distinguishes "nothing new" from "withheld" — the honest-absence problem on a surface where the viewer has no way to resolve it. Fixed additively with `withheld: true` | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D19 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `live-session-platform`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **A multi-writer run cannot say who played a given ply.** `Node.actor` is `"user" \| "opponent" \| "system"` with no identity. It must **not** widen: in an Arena match both legs' reference-side moves are legitimately `"user"` though different humans played them, so putting a learner id there would break the very comparison the mode exists for. Attribution belongs beside the node, not inside it — `live-session-platform.md` derives it from a `board.granted` possession journal by interval lookup, with no run-schema change. **Additional gap found by review:** `deleteLearner` moves possession to `__legacy` (`apps/server/src/storage.ts:630-636`) and journals **nothing**, so a deleted learner's plies have no attribution at all | 🐞 found 2026-08-13 |
| D16 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by owner ruling: branch switch worries at 100 ms and intervenes at 200 ms**, superseding the unsourced `<50 ms`. Measured 45–53 ms sits comfortably inside. The ruling also fixed the shape — a worry/intervene band instead of a single pass/fail line — and `design/02` now records that the budgets are a tripwire for when to go look, not the criterion itself. *One operation carried two different budgets, and I had escalated an imperceptible delta against the stricter one* |
| D15 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13** — `policyModeApplied` records the applied mode, and the off-spine fallback records `human_common` by construction. *The `theory_strict` fallback was invisible to the product.* When a position leaves the authored spine the selector silently falls back to `human_common` and emits only a `console.warn` (`apps/server/src/opponent-selector.ts:455-459`) — it reaches no event, no run, no response and no screen. Consistent with `DEGRADED_POLICY_MASS` (`:469-471`), but consistency with an invisible signal is not a defence for **Line Drill**, a mode whose entire subject is theory: the opponent can stop playing theory mid-drill and nothing the learner or the run can see records it. **Owner ruling 2026-08-12: closed inside the Line Drill RFC, and it blocks acceptance.** `policyModeApplied` (`human_common | strong_engine | theory_strict | unknown`) is added to `opponent.move_selected.selection`; on-spine `theory_strict` records `theory_strict`, off-spine fallback records `human_common`, historical selections migrate to `unknown` and are **never inferred**. Run schema v0.7, migration 5. This reverses `outcome-drill-grading`'s refusal of the field, deliberately: that RFC declined it to preserve no-migration scope, and the owner ruled the migration is worth paying to turn a disclaimer into traceable run evidence | 🐞 found 2026-08-12 reviewing the Line Drill draft |
| D10 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `defect-sweep`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **Both shipped Stockfish specs report `version: "unknown"`.** `parseIdentity` fills `version` from the UCI `id name` line *only* when `spec.name` is unset (`apps/server/src/engine-supervisor.ts:116-126`), and both specs set it — so engine provenance recorded against any evidence is anonymous. B6b works around it by omitting `name` in its authoring spec, which is a workaround, not a fix | 🐞 found 2026-08-12 by the sourcing revision |
| D9 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `defect-sweep`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **`start.side` is schema-optional, and the real consequence is an inverted grading verdict — not the crash originally ledgered.** Re-verified 2026-08-13 while drafting the defect sweep. The stated symptom (`packStartSide` throwing) is now unreachable: `apps/server/src/service.ts:183-185` 400s a side-less pack before any screen renders. Underneath it is worse. `apps/server/src/pack-validation.ts:369` silently coerces a missing side to white (`=== "black" ? "black" : "white"`), and `:346-348` then evaluates `learner === sideToMove` with `learner` **undefined** — always false — so the declared Syzygy category is passed through `opposite()` unconditionally. A `save` pack (tablebase loss) reads as a win. **It passes `pack-check` green and cannot be run**, so the wrong verdict is latent rather than observed — which is why nobody caught it. A silent inversion in the one place the product claims machine-exact grounding | 🐞 raised in severity 2026-08-13 |
| D8 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `defect-sweep`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **The JSON Schema and the runtime validator disagree on two declared values.** `perfect_tablebase` (opponent mode) and `immediate_blunder_guard` (feedback policy) both validate against `schemas/drill_pack.schema.json` and are then rejected at load by `apps/server/src/pack-validation.ts:104-129`. Consequence beyond tidiness: the **on-ramp layer's declared feedback knob** (`design/00-thesis.md` §Target player specifies the 1000–1400 lane on exactly three knobs, one of them this) and the **endgame perfect-play opponent** are both currently unencodable. Same divergence class as D4 — two sources of truth for one vocabulary | 🐞 found 2026-08-12 by the sourcing draft |
| D7 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `line-drill-theory-grading`.** *`deviations` were not linted at all.* The new `AUTHORED_PROSE_AFTER_LAST_CHECKPOINT` warning covers `annotations` only, so unreachable deviation prose passes silently — but Pack C found the gap is wider than reveal: **no lint checks that a deviation's `moveUci` is even legal in its anchor position, or that it belongs to the side to move.** An author can ship an illegal deviation, or one for the wrong colour, and `pack-check` passes. Both pack authors resorted to hand-auditing every deviation with chessops (10 and 12 respectively) | 🐞 found 2026-08-12 by authoring |
| D6 ✅ | **CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `defect-sweep`** (status refreshed 2026-08-14; prose below is the historical record). **`phase` never reaches the client.** `PackSummary` omits it (`pack-registry.ts:16-24`); `grep -rn "phase" apps/web/src` finds two unrelated prose strings. The Learn IA is organized on this axis | 🐞 |

## Authoring-format friction (💡 — raised by authors while writing real packs)

The point of authoring one pack per phase is to find these. Each is a concrete
proposal from someone who hit the limitation with content in hand, which is the
evidence the withdrawn contracts RFCs never had.

| Friction | Proposal | Raised by |
|---|---|---|
| A plan drill's objective cannot be expressed. Success is relative to the **captured intent** — did you execute the plan you committed to — but `successConditions` supports only intent-blind `reach_checkpoint`, so plan packs ship with no objective at all | Intent-relative success conditions. **Path found 2026-08-13 (`05` §5c): grade the plan by its structural consequence.** The predicate machinery ships and is authorable; only feature-level vocabulary (Q4b) is missing. Still connected to `intent_capture` being inert and to the objective-type widening in program #4 | Pack B (Carlsbad), 2026-08-12 |
| The on-ramp's blunder-guard knob has no encoding. `defect-sweep` removes `immediate_blunder_guard` from the schema because `feedbackPolicy` has no capability publication, no applied-policy record and no honest degradation — so `design/00-thesis.md`'s 1000–1400 lane loses one of the three knobs that define it. Re-add it as a *real* policy with those three properties, not as an enum value. **SHIPPED 2026-08-14 (`onramp-guard`): `immediate_guard` at pack 0.14, post-commit, two rung-0 tiers + recorded-eval tier. Scheduled note kept for history: v1 form is the post-commit guard — the move commits, the guard offers play-on-or-rewind — per the Dr. Wolf transformation (their pre-commit retract inverts commit-before-learning). Pre-commit form is invariant-review material if the owner's session says beginners need it** | 💡 raised by the defect sweep, 2026-08-13 |
| **For a middlegame pack the two authoring rules fight each other, and no instrument sees the fight.** "Follow the most-played move at the band" is what makes a spine corpus-grounded; "the referenced shape must keep firing" is what makes it a *structure* pack. Measured 2026-08-15: from the French Advance family root the most-played walk liquidates the d4 pawn and `closed-centre-chain` stops firing within three plies; the Grünfeld's most-played reply is a central lever that ends the fianchetto race; the Maroczy's most-played line survives intact. Authors resolved it by hand, one extra walk at a time (~12 min each), and each stated the departure in a SPINE SELECTION RULE provenance line. A walker that reports, per node, whether a named expression still holds after each candidate move would make the conflict visible instead of archaeological | Middlegame wave (10 packs), 2026-08-15 |
| Deviations have no link to a plan class. 11.a3 and 11.Rab1 are literally the *same plan*, and `accepted_alternative` says nothing about that | Optional `planClassId` on a deviation | Pack B, 2026-08-12 |
| `concept_violation` does two different jobs — a **timing** error (right plan, wrong moment) and a **plan-coherence** error (a move that contradicts the committed plan). Collapsing them loses the distinction the feedback most needs to make | ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-08-15 by `deviation-classes`.** Optional multi-valued `mistake` separates `plan`, `timing`, and `tactical` from the unchanged objective-relative class; no machine reclassifies the author's judgment. | Pack B, 2026-08-12 |

| Comparison column ceiling | 💡 **owner ruling wanted.** `n-way-comparison.md` caps a comparison set at 8 branches and originally cited `design/03-product-breadth.md:288` for it — **that citation was fabricated; no such statement exists** (the only "eight" there is eight program items). The RFC now asserts 8 on its own grounds. Design should either ratify the number or set its own, because it is a product constraint on the surface the whole rewind-and-compare loop leans on | `03-product-breadth.md`, Q9 |
| Prediction distribution delivery | 💡 the withholding barrier **truncates** the event stream at the first engine-feedback event, so a synchronous response is a legitimate and distinct delivery path. Worth stating in design, since "it isn't gated" and "it is delivered" were conflated once already | `explanation-grounds`, ADR-0006 |

## In-run assistance surfaces (💡 batch, owner ideas 2026-08-13)

Three ideas from the owner, ledgered on utterance. They compose: (1) says *what
kind of position this is*, (2) says *what evidence applies to that kind*, and (3)
is a distinct thing — deterministic sight, not evidence at all.

**Design home: `05-in-run-experience.md`**, written the same day so these do not
sit as loose candidates. All three are generic in-run features — true whether or
not a pack is loaded — and that document places them: the classifier is what
makes the assistance rail selectable (§4), configuration per session context is
one of its four varying axes (§4), and discovered-threat sight is **rung 0** of
the assistance ladder (§3), the only rung that cannot manufacture chess truth.

| Idea | Take | Home |
|---|---|---|
| **Phase classifier with an in-run sidebar** | Early/middle/endgame surfaced live during play, not just as a catalogue filter. Partly covered already: Q4c owns automatic recognition, D6 is `phase` never reaching the client, and the Learn IA is organized on it — but all of that is *navigation*. A live in-run classifier is new, and it is what makes (2) selectable. Must stay non-authoritative over curated pack boundaries (standing rule) and must abstain honestly rather than guess | Q4c, D6, `03-product-breadth.md` |
| **Configurable engine/assistance sidebar** | Run simulations, ask for best move or a move *set*, from whichever source is applicable — Stockfish for evaluation, Maia for what humans at a level actually play, Lichess explorer for frequency, Syzygy where the material allows. **The new part is not the sources — B4 already owns those — it is that assistance becomes configurable per session context.** Just Play, streamer mode and a curated drill want different defaults, and "what assistance is available here" is currently implicit everywhere. Includes structural classifiers for middlegame ideas (the Dragon bishop wanting an open long diagonal), which is the deterministic-features work in Q4b rather than an engine question | B4, Q4b, Q8, `03-product-breadth.md` §Intelligence |
| **Discovered-threat visualisation ("shift to see two moves of consequence")** | 💡 **transition arithmetic shipped 2026-08-15; the visual/hypothetical surface remains open.** `transition-primitives` now answers “what changed on the committed move?” on request with rules-derived attack, defence, line, flight-square, duty and irreversibility facts. R3 refused an unasked live tier. Holding a modifier over a candidate move or projecting two plies remains a distinct anti-contamination/form decision and is not claimed by that RFC. | ADR-0005, ADR-0006, opponent-intent row, Q4b, Q9, `transition-primitives` |

## Strategic reading and pivotal detection (💡 batch, owner 2026-08-13)

Specified in `05-in-run-experience.md` §5/§5a/§5b; rows kept so none is lost.

| Idea | Take | Home |
|---|---|---|
| **Prophylaxis / denial as a first-class readable** | "After a4, Black's knight can never use b5 again" is rules arithmetic — rung 0, cannot be wrong. A denial move is **invisible to every eval-first tool** because nothing happened, which makes it the clearest demonstration that rung 0 sees things an evaluation cannot explain. Mirror of the ledgered opponent-intent prompt | `05` §5, ADR-0005 |
| **Structural readables: outposts, diagonals, pressure balance** | All three are computable without an engine — an outpost is a pawn-defended square in enemy territory unattackable by enemy pawns; a diagonal's blockers can be named; attackers and defenders can be counted. **Detection is free; significance is rungs 2–5 and must be attributed.** Q4b's deterministic-feature work is this | `05` §5, Q4b |
| **Tactical build-up ("what is each side aiming at")** | The one part of the owner's list that is *not* deterministic. Rung 2/3 territory, and the place where a strategic classifier would be tempted to manufacture truth. Keep separate from the structural readables above rather than bundling them into one "threat classifier" | `05` §5, ADR-0005, Q8 |
| **Auto-detected pivotal moments for pack-less play** | The gem, per the thesis amendment. Honest detectors: irreversibility (rung 0), phase change, **human divergence** — the Maia distribution at the learner's level splitting several ways, which is a fact about the distribution rather than a claim about chess and uses a model already shipped — and option collapse. Engine eval swing is deliberately *excluded* as a primary detector: it finds where someone erred, which is the post-mortem framing this product replaces, and it cannot fire before the error | `05` §5a, Q4c, B2 |
| **Content transfer test** | 📐 owner test 2026-08-13, audited in `04` §0a: *does authoring this make Just Play better?* Result for the shipped format — **no**. Every field is anchored to the pack's own move tree except four, and the three that carry reusable knowledge cannot escape: `planClasses` are inlined per pack, `concepts` have no cross-pack identity, `feedbackClaims` have no trigger so they can never fire. Authoring a pack today improves exactly one drill and contributes nothing to an unauthored game. **Rule: content earns its cost by how much of it fires in a game nobody authored** — not a ban on line content (opening theory *is* a move sequence) but a budget and a direction, and the reusable half currently rounds to zero. Minimal fix: packs **reference** shape entries instead of inlining them; `fenPredicate`/`materialBalance` are already the two shipped triggers that key on the position rather than the tree, so the machinery exists and the authoring habit does not | `04` §0/§0a, B11, Q4b |
| **Feature-level structural predicates (the unification)** | ✅ The shared structural-expression grammar is executable in pack objectives and shape entries. Predicate waves 1–3 supply eighteen detector kinds, and `plan_consequence` now binds an authored plan class to its reusable shape-plan success signature without claiming intent. Pack B exercises the binding on its authored line. | `05` §5c, Q4b, `predicate-wave-3` |
| **Game story — a finished game as ~8 pivotal slides** | ✅ **SHIPPED — verified 2026-08-15**, `docs/game-import-and-story.md` §Grounded moments: `GET /runs/:id/story` is a disclosure-gated derived projection over shipped deterministic detectors (irreversible moves, phase changes, option collapse, eval pivots, first endgame entry, shape spans); the payload returns all moments with a deterministic rank and **the client displays up to eight**, ordered by ply. Voice may phrase the packet, never add chess claims. Was carried as open in claude's 2026-08-15 inventory — an error caught the same day by checking `docs/` instead of the ledger — original row: owner 2026-08-14: *"we have our pivotal moments... maybe we can summarize a game with states and how it influenced the game? a set of 8 slides."* **Everything it needs is the backward half of machinery we already specified or shipped**: retrospective pivotal detection (`05` §3a — eval swing is *permitted* backward, plus objective-state transitions and last-point-the-result-was-available), phase classification (B10, in flight), structural naming (B9, shipped), and the outcome grade. A slide = position + what kind of moment it was (detector-attributed) + what changed after it. **Post-game, so ADR-0006 does not bite** — the game is over, nothing left to contaminate; this is the one surface where the full ladder may speak at once. Natural tie-ins: the share/spectate links (a story is the shareable artifact a run link never was), session-to-pack distillation (a slide's moment is a candidate drill root), and Just Play's review path. Law-8 rule: each slide *names* its detector and evidence; the connective narrative is LLM-as-voice territory (packet-bound, §3b-i). Slide count is presentation, not contract | `05` §3a/§5a, B10, share links, teardown-taketaketake (research in flight) |
| **Own-game review — import one game into the rehearsal frame** | ✅ **SHIPPED — verified 2026-08-15**, `docs/game-import-and-story.md`: `imported` is a third session kind; one pasted PGN or one public lichess game URL (chess.com refused with guidance, no public per-game contract); selecting a story moment rewinds to its `entryNodeId` and creates a `story-reentry` branch, so the game opens back into live play rather than a report — which is exactly the line that keeps it out of the rejected v1 identity. **Export ships too** (all branches, rehearsal branches as legal PGN variations). Browser-tested end to end — original row: owner 2026-08-14: *"we said it's not a game review, but we have all the pieces (+the LLM) — in context of our rewind / classifiers."* **The v1 boundary, pinned:** what stays rejected is mine-games→detect-weaknesses→generate-lessons as identity and mandatory entry (AGENTS.md, ADR-0003). What this is: **one game, imported by choice, as an entry context** — backward pivotal detection finds the moments (`05` §3a), game-story renders them as slides, tapping a slide rewinds *there*, branch, replay vs human-like resistance with structural reading on. The review IS the rehearsal entry — the anti-version of the engine-review screen, not a return to it. **Shipped pieces:** PGN export w/ variations (B3), Arena leg PGN import on a request path (live-sessions — verify reusability as the parser seam), backward detectors (B10), structural naming (B9), position runs (F2 + B11's player). **Missing:** a first-class import endpoint creating an imported run from PGN or a lichess/chess.com game URL (external fetch = ToS/licence note needed), and the review surface itself. Composes with game-story into one RFC — both consume B10's backward detection. Account-level fetch-all-my-games stays out | `05` §3a, game-story row, ADR-0003, B2 entry contexts |
| **Recurring sharp edge: rewind cancels pending evidence** | 📐 pattern note, 2026-08-14. `onRewound` (`apps/server/src/evidence-queue.ts:148-169`) cancels queued jobs and drops staged results for off-path nodes — correct for live play (a pruned line's evidence is moot) but **two independent drafts designed around it in one day**: branch-groups (lockstep rotation's rewind cancels sibling jobs mid-round) and game-import (a slide tap IS a rewind, and mid-batch would destroy later slides' evals). Both solved it locally (recovery via `/analysis` + honest absence; a `ready` gate). If a third consumer hits it, the fix is structural: evidence jobs for *historical/batch* analysis should not ride the live-play queue's pruning rules — a batch class exempt from `onRewound` | evidence-queue, branch-groups §, game-import § |
| **Opening-wave authoring frictions (2026-08-14)** | 💡 batch from the six-pack wave: ✅ candidate identities key by learner side; ✅ first-move alternatives use `atStart`; ✅ `predicate-wave-3` distinguishes present from prospective shape references and publishes uncheckable plan consequences; still open: the missing post-dxc5 residue shape. | `planning/content-era/log.md` 2026-08-14, shape library, pack schema |
| D28 | **An outcome leg with no `successConditions` compiles to zero grading rules and passes `pack-check` silently.** `objectiveRules` early-returns before the automatic win/draw/loss rules attach, so a trajectory's endgame leg can declare `win` and grade nothing — the D12a silent-pass shape at the leg level. Found by the first real trajectory packs (both carry a `material_balance` condition purely to dodge it). Fix: either attach the automatic outcome rules before the early return, or make a grading-free outcome leg a load refusal like `OBJECTIVE_GRADES_NOTHING` | 🐞 found 2026-08-14 by trajectory authoring |
| **Trajectory-format frictions (first real packs, 2026-08-14)** | 💡 `legs` carry only id/entry/objective — per-leg `shapes` and per-leg `opponentPolicy` are inexpressible (a trajectory crossing three phases arguably wants phase-appropriate resistance); piece censuses need a six-leaf existence hack (predicate wave 2's `quantified` should collapse it — verify on landing); and the `rook-4v3-same-side` trigger names a family it cannot census, so it fires on ANY rooks-only ending — kept with a census-honesty provenance line, but the entry name over-promises its trigger | trajectory packs log entry, predicate-wave-2 |
| **On-ramp wave frictions (2026-08-14)** | 💡 from wave 5a: ✅ guard thresholds now support per-subtree overrides, mate independence, rules-tier control and ply windows, and the position-seeds emitter carries `--guard-cp` / `--guard-mate` (`authoring-frictions`); still open: validated intent answers, `theory_strict` off-spine consequence behaviour, and the Node v26 zstd stream failure. | wave 5a log, guard archive |
| **Endgame-wave frictions (2026-08-14)** | ✅ closed: (1) `grounding-pair` attached tablebase evidence through the unmodified nine-condition admission; (2) `authoring-frictions` added machine-proved `variantOf` sibling relations; (3) `authoring-frictions` added `atStart` checkpoints so learner-moves-first roots can capture intent before ply 1. | wave 5b log, sourcing pipelines |
| **Wave-4a follow-ups** | 💡 from the openings wave: ✅ the first-move-alternative idiom ships as `atStart` deviations (`authoring-frictions`); still open/commissioned: London wedge, KID arrangement chain, Scandinavian depth work, and explorer fixture recurrence. The harness refutation of the illegal ...Bxg3 annotation remains part of the evidence record. | wave-4a log |

So the honest form ships comparison *with* that limitation stated, rather than pretending it away. **What it unlocks:** the club/coach cohort, the league shape (whose hosted form is separately refuted on randomised evidence), and the honour-roll middle path as a presentation option rather than a compromise | [[D332]], `learner-rating` R10, league research |
| **A campaign boss is a full game, not a pack (owner ruling 2026-08-16)** | ✅ **RULED.** Packs cannot be rated because `plyHorizon` truncation leaves no rules-terminal result and manufacturing one needs a position verdict law 8 forbids. | **Leagues as a return loop: REFUTED on randomised evidence, and we already shipped the lever that works** | ✅ 2026-08-16, `design/research/league-as-return-loop.md`. The appealing claim was that social obligation beats self-paced scheduling. **Tested, and it is null-to-negative.** Kizilcec et al. 2020 (*PNAS*, **preregistered, ~250,000 students**) randomised this exact mechanism: **P = 0.115** on completion, despite moving week-1 activity. Baker/Evans/Dee (n=18,043): a scheduling nudge **cut certificates 4.8pp, a 52% relative drop**. The only cohort-vs-solo RCT puts the cohort arm **directionally worst**. parkrun retains **4.3%** over three years. The *"cohorts complete at 96%"* figure traces to **vendors and founders, not studies**. **The corollary is the finding.** The intervention with the cleanest randomised support is **imposed, evenly spaced deadlines** — Ariely & Wertenbroch, **no social component**, where controlling for even spacing killed the effect. **That is `return-and-progression`'s 1/3/7/16/35 ladder: we already shipped the evidence-backed lever.** The single licensed upgrade is to make the spacing **imposed rather than freely dismissible** — *not* to add a person | return loop, C7, ADR-0007 |
| **A league needs a ranked table, and `learner-rating` R10 refuses one — owner ruling wanted** | 💡 2026-08-16, `design/research/league-as-return-loop.md`. Its cheating rationale is now *observed* rather than theoretical: 4545's own history records investigations altering standings in Seasons 7, 8 and 17, against our accepted limitation that we do not prevent self-cheating. **A transformation survives either ruling:** permanent gold/silver/bronze **name shading** — an honour roll with no number and no ordering | `learner-rating` R10, D332 |
| **`00-thesis` mechanism 1 collides with leagues harder than R10 does** | 💡 2026-08-16: the thesis sells *"every other context charges you a lost game for that curiosity"* — **and a league IS that context.** 4545 resolves it by banning all in-game assistance (even talking to your captain) and displacing learning to strictly before or after the game. So a hosted league would import the exact cost our pitch is written against. Sharper than the leaderboard question and easier to miss | `00-thesis.md`, campaign cluster |
| **Nothing in the product can reach a learner who is not on the site** | 🐞 2026-08-16, the largest structural hole the league study found: term census **0 hits** for `notify`, `notification`, `email`, `webpush`, `reminder`; Live polls. Against Chesster's twelve notification behaviours, and **it is a precondition for D301's daily too** — any return loop that depends on arriving at the right moment currently cannot say so. Five smaller holes alongside it: no learner directory, `session_invitations.state` is written `'open'` and **never transitions** (the obligation state machine is schema-only), a **forfeit is inexpressible** (4–5% of league games), and `service.ts:1763` forces `countable:false` on a match run's primary branch — **so two-human play and the return loop are disjoint today** | return loop, D301, live sessions |
| **A league is a better-controlled human-anchor experiment than the one we designed** | 💡 2026-08-16, `design/research/league-as-return-loop.md` §C7 — the unexpected gift. `rfc/learner-rating.md` calls a human anchor *"the single highest-value unrun experiment this RFC creates"*: our whole rating study is engine-vs-engine, so the scale has no offset. A league supplies exactly the controls that experiment needs — an established Lichess rating, **all assistance banned**, no rewind, and pairing within ~100 points. **Participating in one measures our anchor better than hosting one would** | `learner-rating`, D332 anchor |
| **Intention is not a signal; a per-occasion declaration is** | 💡 2026-08-16, free adoption: 4545 **deleted** its season-long commit-affirmation as *"a useless question"* and replaced it with **per-round availability**. Directly applicable to anything we ask a learner to promise — the scheduling ladder, the campaign's run commitment, `scheduledFor`. Asking someone to affirm they will keep going tells you nothing; asking whether they are available *this round* is data | return loop, campaign |
| **Live game and tournament relay as a drill source** | 💡 owner 2026-08-16: *"is there a way we can import easily the live game? like live tourneys or w/e? are those exposed from lichess or chess.org? I can imagine for streamers you want to cast a live game."* **What ships today is the finished-game case only** — one pasted PGN or one public lichess *game* URL, with chess.com URLs **refused** for want of a per-game public contract (`docs/game-import-and-story.md`). A live relay is a different object: an ongoing move stream with **no result yet**. **Why it may fit this product better than it fits a review tool:** a broadcast position becomes a **drill root the moment it appears** — *the game is at move 25 and a GM is thinking; you take over from here and play the consequence*. That is the thesis applied to a position whose answer does not exist yet, which is the one case where nobody can be shown the verdict because there isn't one. For the streamer surface (`design/03` §B5: Twitch host/chat/overlay) it is the natural feed: the host casts the live board, viewers fork the position and play it themselves. **Open and unresearched:** which relays are actually exposed (Lichess broadcasts, chess.com, DGT/TWIC-style tournament feeds), under what terms, and whether relaying someone else's broadcast is licensed. **Collisions to check rather than assume:** a live game has no terminal outcome, so outcome grading has nothing to resolve against; `05` §1's *"the run is the sole source of chess truth"* must survive a root that keeps moving; and the events row (pack nights, cohorts, **team relays**) is already ledgered and parked — check whether this is that row | ✅ **RESEARCHED 2026-08-16, `design/research/live-relay-as-drill-source.md` — every endpoint exercised live, unauthenticated, one request at a time.** **Lichess: yes, and it is the aggregation point.** `/api/broadcast` + `/round/{id}.pgn` + the round streams cover **in-progress third-party OTB and online tournaments**, no auth for public rounds, and the ToS grants *personal or commercial use in your own products* (revocable). Measured: **67 active broadcasts, 23 rounds ongoing**; one live Hungarian OTB round returned **42 games, 42/42 `[Result "*"]`**. **There is no per-game broadcast endpoint — the round is the retrieval unit.** **Chess.com: a clean no**, and our existing refusal **generalises rather than needing revision** — Archives is documented as *finished* games, `/pub/player/{u}/games` is Daily correspondence on a 12-hour cache, four plausible live paths 404, and the terms are personal **non**-commercial with AI scraping prohibited. Verified workaround: their marquee events get relayed **on Lichess** (EWC 2026 fetched, `[Site "Chess.com"]`, publicly readable). **Ownership: ecosystem at the source, platform at the aggregation** — organiser DGT boards → LiveChess → a public PGN URL that **Lichess polls like anyone could**. Integrating there is a convergence point, not lock-in. **Rights are green:** moves are facts (Moscow 2016; S.D.N.Y. denied World Chess's injunction on hot-news and contract — **no copyright was pleaded**). **Verdict: SCOPE, and the framing is stronger than it was pitched** — on a live root the law-8 dashboard failure is foreclosed **by the object**, not by a rule, because there is no verdict to show. **Two corrections to claude's own framing:** *"the root keeps moving"* is a **misframing** (the snapshot is a copy, so `05` §1 survives untouched provided nobody builds a run that *follows* a broadcast), and the outcome-grading collision is **already solved** — a live root inherits `imported`'s non-pack `attempt_end` status and `Result "*"` is handled at `pgn-import.ts:57-60`. **This is NOT the parked events row** — that row's *"team relays"* is a roster-with-calendar; word collision only. Cost is **a third adapter** beside the two in `import-source.ts`, **two polite one-shot fetches, not a stream**. **And the strongest argument is not the drill — it is the return beat:** a live game supplies **its own deadline**, which is the one return lever `league-as-return-loop.md` found randomised evidence for |
| **The rejected shape is not "a rewind button" — it is an attempt that does not survive** | 💡 **the sharpest reframing of our own criterion to date**, `design/research/teardown-chessigma-desk.md` 2026-08-16. Chessigma's paid *Bot Challenge* does real re-entry: *"You blew a +3 in a real game. Pick it back up at that exact move. Same clock. Bot at your level. Finish it this time."* That is further into play-the-consequence than anything else in the corpus — past chess.com's one-ply Retry, Chess2Story's read-only slides, Dr. Wolf's erasing undo, Chessbook's no-opponent. **And then it stops: it re-enters but never preserves.** Nothing claims a second attempt survives, that rewind forks, or that two attempts can be compared; its unit of truth is the aggregate metric, not the attempt. **So `AGENTS.md` §Rejected's *"engine review screen with a rewind button"* is under-specified — a competitor can have the rewind and still not be us. The line is whether the second attempt SURVIVES TO BE COMPARED WITH THE FIRST.** E1 intact, and now intact for a stateable reason. Candidate for design-tier promotion | E1, `00-thesis.md`, AGENTS.md §Rejected |
| **The pricing inversion: analysis is free, the doing is paid** | 💡 `design/research/teardown-chessigma-desk.md` — the incumbent has just paywalled analysis; the companion gives **unlimited** analysis away on client-side WASM (near-zero marginal cost, engineered rather than charitable) and charges €12–17/mo for the weakness model and drill queue, **guaranteed in Elo**. **A companion tool concluded that the doing is what people pay for — our half of the market, sold by someone else**, to a band of 800–1400: the third teardown running whose ceiling sits **below our floor**. Bears on Q2, and on whether our free surface should terminate in a report or an offer to replay | Q2, adoption audit |
| **The sharpest live ADR-0005 violation in the corpus is a competitor's advertising copy** | 💡 `design/research/teardown-chessigma-desk.md` — Chessigma's own blog sells: *"…usually due to weak dark squares." That's prophecy.* and *"You played Bg5 because you wanted to trade pieces…"* — an LLM causal claim and **attributed learner intent**, with true counts laundering the invented clause. Their stated defence is a self-reported ChessQA score, **not a checker**. This is the anti-pattern law 8 was written against, in the wild, sold as the feature. | **Adoption candidates from Chessigma, each with its transformation** | 💡 `design/research/teardown-chessigma-desk.md` §adoption. **Cheapest first — "conversion entry: won it, lost it, drill it"**: both halves already ship (`save`/`hold` grading + the story's near-level-moment-in-a-loss detector); only a named entry composing them is missing, **no collision**. Then: **re-entry at exact ply with the original clock** — adopt the clock as *provenance/fidelity* on `imported` runs, never as a difficulty knob (D331 refuted time-as-difficulty but says nothing about time-as-provenance); **publish a restraint metric** (over-calls per 1,000 ordinary moves) — we measure it internally in `census-hint-false-positives.md` and have never published it, and the answer key must be rules/tablebase, not a competitor's labels; **named sparring personas** over `targetElo` + declared repertoire, where D333 caps honest rungs at 5–9 so nine partners is the *ceiling*, not a target; **streaks are ADR-0007-illegal as shipped elsewhere** (a day-streak unlocks by elapsed time — the compatible form counts attempts finished). **Refused outright:** the mistake→puzzle re-cut (find-the-tactic, `00-thesis` §§70/93–94). **Highest value, highest risk, ledger don't fast-track:** grounded chat restricted to querying recorded evidence packets, absence returned when unanswerable | adoption audit, `05` §3b-i |

I don't think I'll use game review again."* **Read against `design/06-campaign.md`: this is the exact failure mode our Spire map, encounter unlocks and pack-opening ceremony are exposed to** — reward layered *on top of* the analysis surface, interrupting it, reading as an upsell. The distinction that may save ours is that the campaign gates **assistance the learner assembled**, not confetti on moves already played; that distinction is currently a design intention and nothing enforces it. **R7 should test frustration, and this says it must also test *interruption*.** `[P]` | campaign cluster, R7, ADR-0007 |

| **The incumbent's moat is habit and feel, not features** | 💡 same thread: *"I want to cancel my membership and switch exclusively to lichess so bad I just can't make the jump"* → *"Just habit, easy to change"*; and *"no matter how hard I try to convince myself otherwise, I find the chesscom interface way more pleasant to play on."* Also measured in-thread: **3 minutes to find a 5\|3 game at 500 Elo vs 2–3 seconds** on the alternative — which bears on the parked native-pool row, since pool liquidity is a retention feature we have deferred. `[P]` | Q2, native pool (parked) |
| **Adoption candidates named unprompted by players** | 💡 same thread: **chesstempo** (*"much better than any alternative other than puzzle books"* for tactics), **chessigma** (*"play on chess.com, review on chessigma"* — a review-only companion, which is the shape we are), **taketaketake** (*"I find awesome the game review"* — we hold a teardown), **lichess studies** (*"amazing to keep your openings, games"*), **Lichess4545 leagues** (*"a very positive impact on my chess learning"* — a league as the return loop), and a browser extension that imports chess.com games into lichess analysis. The last two are the interesting ones: a **league** is a return-loop shape we have not considered, and the **importer** is the workflow our own game-import already serves | adoption audit, return loop |
| **Just Play difficulty: what is the opponent, and what can "fair but challenging" mean?** | 💡 owner 2026-08-15: *"if you just play, what is the difficulty of the opponent? do we track elo and slide it from 1000 to 2000? or configurable? or IM/GM level? we have so many sources for moves — we can make it fair but challenging?"* **As shipped:** Just Play runs `human_common` — Maia sampling its policy head at a `targetElo` the session may set — so an Elo slider already exists, but unbounded (D60) and un-inherited-safely (D58). Five modes ship: `human_common`, `strong_engine`, `theory_strict`, `perfect_tablebase`, `practical_resistance`. **The honest ceiling:** Maia is a human-behaviour model trained at bands ≈1100–1900. **There is no IM/GM-like option and cannot cheaply be one** — above the band the only shipped opponent is `strong_engine`, which is not a strong human but a different species, and weakening it is rejected doctrine. **The honest floor on "fair":** *adaptive* difficulty needs to measure how hard a position is for this learner, and R4+R9 jointly say that is impossible in the middlegame — engines are silent on undecided positions, humans past ply ~20. So mid-game difficulty can be **selected** (a band) or **authored**, never **measured**. **What that leaves, and it is more interesting than a slider:** a *phase-aware* opponent — theory in the opening, Maia at band through the middlegame, tablebase-perfect or `practical_resistance` once the endgame is decided. The sources the owner names are real but they cover different phases, not different strengths | Just Play, R4, R9, resistance spectrum |
| **Q3 answered: tolerate mobile, and the TABLET floor is the unmet one** | ✅ 2026-08-15, `design/research/mobile-scope.md` — verdict **tolerate, responsive-only**, with a two-tier floor: phone = the run loop, read surfaces and 2-column compare; full surface at 768 px and up; **mobile-native stays a non-goal**. Not phone-shaped, named: 4–8-column compare, branch groups ≥4, live session detail, pack studio. **The finding that reframes it:** the **720–992 px band (iPad portrait) is worse than the phone** — it loses the fitted grid at ≤62 rem without gaining the compact tabs at ≤719 px, which is exactly the *"drill scrolls like a document"* shape the owner rejected, and **no browser projection covers that band**. The tablet floor is currently **unmet**; that is the one thing worth fixing. Market corroboration from the repo's own dossiers: Lichess shipped its phone client **without** studies, learn-from-mistakes or analysis and absorbed the complaints — phones win where the unit is one board, never where it is a tree | Q3, E5, `branch-set-scale` |

Three real packs grade it and Just Play renders committed-edge facts on request. R3 removed the live tier; routing, prophylaxis, forcing claims and multi-move history remain refused by name rather than smuggled into the census. | question shapes, predicate wave, `docs/transition-primitives.md` |
| **R1 answered: the transition census is cheap** | ✅ 2026-08-15, `design/research/move-primitive-computability.md` — 593 spine transitions replayed from all 35 packs. The **entire** census (attacks, defences, lines, control delta, escape squares, overload, tempo proxies, irreversibility, routing) costs **29 µs/ply** from two FEN strings: 0.58 ms for a 20-ply branch, 4.7 ms for an 8-branch comparison. **Cost is not an argument against the category in either direction.** ~70 of 290 implementation lines are verbatim copies of code already in the tree but private (`pivotal.ts:32-57`, `structure.ts:401-410`). Measured selectivity, which nobody had — **now stated as UPPER BOUNDS**: the `transition-primitives` draft read the harness and found it keys attack/defence relations by `(attackerSquare, targetSquare)`, so a rook that moves and still defends c1 is scored as a *lost* defence. Both readings are coarsenings, so the true firing rates are lower and require re-measurement against the target-keyed definition. Overload **6.7%** < check 7.1% < irreversibility 13.2% ≪ attacks 50.6% < lines 52.6% < escape squares 61.2% < defences 74.9%. **The owner's "overload" instinct picked the sharpest instrument in the set** | question shapes, predicate wave |
| **The engine request contract (promote to design tier)** | 💡 named 2026-08-15 by `rfc/engine-request-contract.md`, and it is the sibling of the declared-vs-executable law rather than an instance of it: **that law governs what a document may SAY; this one governs what a call must DO.** *An engine request must close over the instrument state its answer depends on* — **state** (every option whose value changes the answer is sent by that request, never inherited), **clear** (every accumulated search state the request does not want is cleared), **bind** (the clear and the search it protects are ONE queued exchange — independently violable, and the `position-seeds` reset is a pure *bind* failure with correct clearing), **bound** (every value sent lies inside a published range, or the request is refused by name), **record** (every value applied and the answer taken appear in the persisted record). Its one-line form: ***state a request does not state is state the previous request chose.*** Four ledgered defects are each exactly one missing obligation — D35 is *clear*, D58 is *state* + *record*, D59 is *state* + *record* (the RFC's own preferred remedy widens the window, which is *state*; the original *record*-only mapping was retrofitted), D60 is *bound* — and the two laws overlap on **two obligations** (*bound* wholly, *record* partly) at **one ledger row**, D60 — the draft's "exactly one point" was contradicted two sentences later by its own text — which is why that row cites the older law correctly and is silent on the other three. Belongs in design tier once the RFC lands | declared-vs-executable law, `engine-request-contract` |
| **`piece_distance` — the attestation base is weaker than the ruling was told** | ⚠️ **owner-facing correction, 2026-08-15.** The owner ruled to absorb `piece_distance` into 0.18 on the stated ground that it "meets the wave's own bar — two attestations for non-king roles". Measurement afterwards showed **both** of those attestations (`knight-vs-bishop`, `up-an-exchange`) live in shape entries **no pack references** (D44), against `piece_count`'s 143 corpus leaves. The bar is about what authors hit, and they did hit it, so this does not overturn the ruling — but the owner decided on a base materially thinner than presented and should see it stated. Two further measured limits argue for scoping: **rook, bishop and queen take only the values {1,2}** across all 615 corpus positions against a king target, so slider-distance leaves are near-useless; and the **vacuity trap bites where authors would reach for it** — 85 of 440 positions have every white bishop off-shade from the black king, concentrated in `mate-bishop-knight`, a bishop-and-knight mate pack. Under an ∞ convention `atLeast n` would have been *true* across the wave's own demonstration packs | `predicate-wave-3`, D43, D44 |
| **Shape plan success signatures are executable grading targets** | ✅ closed 2026-08-15 by `predicate-wave-3`: `plan_consequence` resolves `planClass.shapePlan` to `plans[].success.signature`, compiles it to a FEN predicate, records the plan-class binding plus structural evidence, and refuses absent or unsatisfiable signatures. | Q4b, `predicate-wave-3`, shape library |
| **We deferred a bigger family than three we shipped** | ✅ corrected 2026-08-15. The Scandinavian was deferred from wave 4a on 'depth-commensurability' — 56.3M games but only 2-ply depth. **The 2-ply depth was a property of the PULL, not the opening.** Four fresh authenticated pulls through the repo's own emitter measure the family at tabiya depth: 3...Qd8 = **7.84M games at 6 plies**, 3...Qa5 = 7.16M — while three families we *did* author sit below that (KID 5.35M, Dutch 4.85M, London 3.85M). Two packs now authored. **The process lesson generalizes past this row: a deferral justified by an instrument reading needs the instrument checked, exactly as a claim does** | wave 4b, sourcing |
